| 8f.106 | **#83 SHIPPED - A BIT MOBILE FRIENDLY. THE GAME TURNS, THE PLAYER DOES NOT.** *(User, 2026-08-03: "make it a bit mobile freindly. Not fully but a bit friendly.")* **Six rules, no game rule moved, no new screen, and the whole entry is about one fixed box.** `#stage` is 1280x720 and `fit()` is the one place that decides how much of a screen it gets; on a 393x852 phone that answer was `min(393/1280, 852/720)` = **0.307**, a 393x221 board with **74% of the phone black**. **1 - THE VIEWPORT IS DECLARED.** The file had no `<meta name="viewport">` at all, so a phone laid it out at 980px and zoomed that down on top of a stage `fit()` had already shrunk: **the game was being shrunk twice.** One line, and it is the largest single thing here. `user-scalable` is deliberately left alone - a 9px mono label at 0.55 is 5px and pinch is the only way anybody reads it - and the double-tap zoom that usually makes pinch unbearable in a game is removed by `touch-action:manipulation` instead, which keeps pinch. **2 - THE GAME TURNS.** Portrait, under 700px wide, and a coarse pointer: `#stage` takes `rotate(90deg)` on top of its scale and goes **0.307 → 0.546, the board 78% bigger and the phone full**, whichever way it is held. *The user chose this over a "turn your phone" overlay: "a player with rotation lock is stuck at 0.307".* **The gate is three tests on purpose.** A tablet upright (820 wide) is already readable at 0.64 and a sideways game in two hands is wrong, so the width test keeps it out; and **the pointer test is what keeps a DESKTOP out** - a browser window dragged tall and narrow is 500x900, passes both size tests, and would turn the game sideways on a monitor for somebody who was only resizing a window. `gt_rot` = `on`/`off` overrides it for testing and nothing else reads it. **3 - ⚑ THE REAL HAZARD WAS NOT THE ROTATION, IT WAS EVERY RECT IN THE BUILD, AND THIS IS THE TRANSFERABLE HALF.** Everything that floats over the battlefield - the damage figures, the impact line, the hover readout, the Captain's balloon through `hexPt` - is anchored by the delta between two `getBoundingClientRect()`s divided by the stage scale. That is #66's rule and it is what keeps `#bFx` correct at any camera zoom. **A rect is axis-aligned in SCREEN space**, so the moment the stage turns, that delta turns with it and every floating number lands at a right angle to the body it belongs to. Measured: forcing the wrong branch drifts them **1103px**. One new function, **`relPt(inner,outer)`**, is the only place that knows, and the three call sites read it identically either way up. The maths is written above it, because it is not obvious: `rotate(90deg)` sends a layout point (x,y) to screen (-y,x), so a box's screen bbox has `left = -(layout bottom)·s`, `top = (layout left)·s`, `width = layout HEIGHT·s` - **reading the layout top edge back needs the rects' RIGHT edges, not their lefts.** **4 - THE TOOLTIP IS THE ONE THING ROTATION DOES NOT CARRY**, because `TIP` sits on `<body>` outside the stage on purpose (it keeps its own size while the board shrinks, which is what makes it readable on a phone at all). `moveTip` turns it and swaps its clamp axes; the unturned branch now measures the box instead of assuming 310x160, which is why a long trinket tooltip no longer runs off the bottom of a short window. **5 - `fit()` READS `visualViewport`** where it exists, because on a phone `innerHeight` counts the browser chrome currently sliding over the top of the game, and re-runs on `orientationchange` (twice - a phone reports the OLD size on the event and corrects a frame later). **It also fixes a real bug found while measuring: the stage was sitting at `scale(0)`.** `fit()` run once in a zero-sized frame computes a scale of zero and the entire game disappears with no error and no way back - the hidden preview pane does exactly this on boot. Floored at 0.05, and a sub-2px viewport now keeps the last good fit instead of overwriting it. **6 - `overscroll-behavior:none`** so a drag on the board does not pull the browser into a page refresh and throw away the battle, and `-webkit-tap-highlight-color:transparent` so the phone does not paint a grey box over the body you just aimed at. **Verified in the running game at 393x852 with the turn live:** `hexPt`, `fx` and `placeRead` return **the same field coordinates turned as flat, to 0.0001px** across every unit (227.371/166.975 and 257.371/64.975 either way up) · **58 of 60 hexes and 14 of 14 menu buttons hit-test identically** rotated and flat, and the two hexes miss flat as well and always have · the tooltip carries `rotate(90deg)` and stays inside the screen · `LINT()` **0 findings** · **`regress()` all eight fights** (clash 4rd · brigand 7 · pack 7 · slingline 5 · steading 9 · snare 10 · mother 22 · armour 6), no guard hits, `__errs` empty · the desktop reads `COARSE:false` and never turns. Gate picture `shots/83_mobile.html` (three phone frames, the real staged CLASH), built picture `shots/83_mobile_built.html` (the real rotated game, carrying its live transform string). ⚠ **`relPt` and `moveTip` are the only two places that know about `ROT`, and the comment above `fit()` says so** - anything that later anchors to a rect has to go through `relPt` or it will be right on a desktop and a right angle out on a phone. **Not done, on purpose, and this is where "not fully" was spent:** nothing was made bigger. At 0.546 the mono labels render at 5px, and the honest ceiling for a 1280x720 stage on a phone is exactly what panel C shows - a real font pass is a different entry. **#83 spent, next free #84.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.107 | **FIVE PAINTINGS GO IN AT THEIR OWN SIZE, AND THE WHOLE ENTRY IS THAT THE PIPELINE WANTED TO SHRINK THEM.** *(User, 2026-08-04, with the mapping written out first as `.claude/rules/static-event-art.md`: the three rest scenes and the two prologue outcomes are painted and want embedding.)* `EV29` THE WARM SPRING, `EV30` THE LONG FIRE, `EV31` THE CIRCLE onto `EVENTART.oasis/bonfire/circle`; `EV00B` and `EV00C` onto `PRO_ART.coin` and `PRO_ART.joke_hit`. **⛔ THE ONE DECISION WORTH THE ROW: THESE MASTERS ARE PAINTED AT THE LIVE CANVAS, NOT AT A DELIVERY SIZE.** 586x212 is `DLGART_W` x `DLGART_H` exactly, set by 8f.102 when the event card grew; 460x190 is `proCard()`'s outcome canvas exactly. Every existing pass in `build_assets.ps1` is a **crop-and-resize** pass - stage 2 cover-crops to 640x360 and re-encodes as JPEG - and running these through it would have cropped an already-fitted painting a second time and then thrown the pixels away to a lossy encoder for no gain. The new pass reads the bytes and emits them, nothing else, on the same reasoning stage 1 was exempted for. **⚑ WHICH MEANS THE MIME TYPE HAD TO GROW A SECOND CASE.** `ART{}` was emitted with a hardcoded `data:image/jpeg;base64,` prefix because every entry until now was a JPEG the script itself wrote. PNG masters embedded verbatim need `data:image/png`, so `$pngEntries` is a separate list joined into the same object at emit time - one object for the game, two encodings underneath, and the split is where the file format is decided rather than smeared through the callers. **THE 1:1 DRAW IS A CONSEQUENCE, NOT A NEW CODE PATH.** `drawArt` is a cover-fit: `s = max(W/im.width, H/im.height)`. Source equal to canvas makes `s` exactly **1** and both offsets 0, so the cover-fit degrades into a pixel-for-pixel blit and nothing in the draw path had to be special-cased for this pack. Measured in the running game rather than reasoned about: all five decode at **586x212 / 586x212 / 586x212 / 460x190 / 460x190**, `s` reads back **1**, and driving the real `drawArt` on real-sized canvases paints **99%** of the oasis window and **95%** of the coin card, so nothing is silently blank behind the vignette. **DELIBERATELY LEFT UNPAINTED, AND THE RULE FILE SAYS SO IN WRITING:** `cache` (the buried bag does not read at 586x212), `floor`, and `joke_miss` (both attempted beating compositions were rejected and deleted). All three still resolve to `undefined` and fall through to the tavern, verified live - **an unmapped scene falling back is a feature of `proCard(...,artKey||TAVERNART)` and not an accident, so the absence had to be checked, not assumed.** Cost: **1116 KB** of PNG, **1488 KB** once base64 costs its third, prototype **3.9 → 5.4 MB**. ⚠ **That is the number to watch, because 8f.105 is still open on whether artifact SIZE is what blocks the public share, and this build just added a megabyte to the suspect.** **#84 still free.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.108 | **#82 SHIPPED - OBSTACLES HAVE A HEIGHT NOW, AND TWO THINGS FINALLY ASK.** *(User, 2026-08-03: "Spearmen can't hit through the tall objects (tree, group o rocks). Independent rock medium-small. But archers can shoot through smaller objects (like fire)".)* ⛔ **THE WHOLE ENTRY IS THAT ONE MAP WAS ANSWERING TWO QUESTIONS.** `BLOCKED{}` says *can a body walk here*, and since #36 shipped it has also been answering *can a shot cross here*. The two answers were the same for a year and then they had to differ: **a campfire stopped an arrow exactly as hard as an oak did, and nothing at all stopped a spear** - `needsLane` reads `act.range`, a reach-2 melee act carries `act.reach` and no range, so a thrust never asked about the hex it crossed. **Photographed before the code** (`shots/82_obstacle_height.html`): an archer with three ogres all 3 hexes away read **BLOCKED / BLOCKED / BLOCKED** through a tree, a lone rock and a fire; Vesna with the boar spear was offered all four bodies at 2 hexes, **52% through the tree and 56% through a rock group**. **HEIGHT IS A SECOND REGISTER AND IT NEVER MERGES WITH THE FIRST.** `heightAt(c,r)` returns **TALL** (tree · huge/hugeA · wall · **a rock that touches another rock**) · **MED** (an independent rock) · **LOW** (fire · flower). ⚑ **A GROUP OF ROCKS IS TALLER THAN A ROCK, AND THE FILE ALREADY KNEW WHICH WAS WHICH**: `loneRock` was written for #46 so an ogre could only lift a boulder standing on its own, and it is exactly the test height needed - **so no new terrain kind was added**, which is #61's rule that a kind is a place with its own rule and a dial is not a kind. **THE ARROW:** TALL is BLOCKED as before, LOW is invisible, MED is a new state **COVER at −14**, priced between SCREENED −8 (your own man, braced, cooperating) and OBSTRUCTED −22 (an enemy body, not cooperating). It slotted into the `SHOT{}` ladder in one line **because that ladder was already sorted by cost**, between FAR −18 and LONG −8. **This is the game's first real cover: a hex behind a boulder is now worth standing in.** ⚑ **THE LANE IS WORTH THE WORST THING IN IT, NEVER THE SUM** - a boulder and a body across one line charge −22, not −36, because two costs added together is a number nobody can predict off the board, and the whole point of naming the states was that they can. **THE SPEAR:** a new `reachBlocked()` in **`mayAim`**, so **both brains got it without being taught**, plus the matching refusal in `clickHex` (the comment above `mayAim` has demanded that pairing since the archer-freeze bug) and the offer withdrawn in `render` so **the hex is never lit-and-then-refused**. ⛔ **TERRAIN ONLY. A BODY NEVER REFUSES A SPEAR** - fighting over the person in front of her IS the spearwoman, and a version of this that read bodies would have deleted the class in one line. The wall's trigger asks the same question, or a body walking round the far side of an oak gets speared through it. ⚠ **`losState` IS MEMOISED ON WHERE THE BODIES ARE, AND A LIFTED BOULDER MOVES NO BODY**, so `terrMoved()` now throws the lane cache away at all three mid-fight writes to `B.terr` (the lift, the landing, the Fen-Mother's bog) - and a rock's height depends on its NEIGHBOURS, so **lifting half of a pair re-heights the other half**: a lane that was BLOCKED becomes COVER without that hex changing at all. ⚠ **AND IT DEMOTES A LINE OF #46, WRITTEN DOWN RATHER THAN DISCOVERED**: the old comment on `losState` said the thrown boulder was *"the only way to turn a BLOCKED lane back into a shooting lane"*, and a lone boulder no longer blocks, so what the lift buys now is **removing cover** - clearing a lane means lifting a rock out of a GROUP, which is the one thing #46 does not allow. **THE USER'S RULING, asked as the entry's one open decision:** an independent rock gives an archer **COVER at −14** rather than blocking outright. **Verified in the running game:** `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, no guard hits, `__errs` empty · the archer's three lanes read **BLOCKED / COVER 44% / CLEAR 58%**, the fire lane's number **identical to an empty lane**, the cover lane printing **"over a boulder −14"** · Vesna's four lanes refuse the tree and the rock group and allow the fire and the lone rock, with **exactly two odds badges in the live DOM (56% and 40%) and no lit border**, so the two refusals are genuinely unlit · height reads TALL/MED/LOW off the three hexes as specified. **Measured rather than asserted**, 6 regression passes each way with the old behaviour restored at runtime: wins **35/48 → 38/48**, round counts inside their bands, so the change is **slightly in the player's favour** - archers gain shots over fires and lone rocks, enemy pikes lose reach through woods. **That is a single small sample and not a balance verdict; #50 owns the number.** Pictures: `shots/82_obstacle_height.html` (before, the gate) and `shots/82_obstacle_height_after.html` (after, the verification). ⚠ **THREE NUMBER COLLISIONS WITH A PARALLEL SESSION IN ONE DAY**: #83 and build logs 8f.106 and 8f.107 were all taken while this was being written. **Grep the build log AND all three docs at write time, not at plan time.** **#82 spent, next free #84.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.109 | **⛔ ONE PICTURE, C1, WAS STOPPING THE ENTIRE GAME FROM BEING SHARED WITH ANYBODY. FOUND BY TWENTY PUBLISHED PROBES.** *(User, 2026-08-03 to 08-04, across a day of it: "show me link to share online with friends", "still doesnt work", "can you check the reson in the internet", "Find what was wrong and fix it", and at the end, on the last bisect, "c1".)* **THE SYMPTOM:** the share menu refused every new version with *"This version can't be shared publicly. Publish a new version or change the shared version, then try again"*, while the artifact rendered perfectly in private. **THE METHOD IS THE PART TO KEEP, because the error message names nothing and there is no log:** publish a probe page, have the user click the share toggle, record yes or no, halve the suspect space, repeat. Twenty probes. Ruled out in order: **the account** (a 2KB page shares), **capabilities** (`{}` cleared, and the `downloads` grant had already cost the save-as-a-file row at 8f.104), **artifact history** (a brand new record refuses identically), **size** (4.2MB of the game's own artwork shares), **the email address and the `mailto:`** (shares, which retired the 8f.105 hunch and means ✉ SEND IT TO ME can come back), **all 103KB of the game's prose** (shares), **the head links and the web manifest** (share), **every browser API the game touches** (localStorage, Audio, AudioContext, canvas, clipboard, execCommand: share), **2MB of dense game code** (shares). **What refused was code AND artwork on one page.** Then the two discriminators that broke it open: **filler bytes of identical length in place of the pictures SHARE**, and **the real pictures with the `data:image` prefix mangled still REFUSE**. So the payloads are being decoded by their magic bytes, not by their label, and the page is judged as a whole. A 22-picture bisect went 4-way, then 3-way, then to singles, and landed on **C1_Ratkin_Rank_and_File**, the common ratkin enemy portrait, a three-panel SPEAR/CLEAVER/SLINGER reference sheet. **113 of 114 pictures are innocent.** ⚑ **THE LESSONS.** A capability is a trade and not an upgrade, and this one traded away the whole build. A binary search still works when the oracle is a human clicking a button, it just costs round trips, so publish every probe of a round at once and let them click through. And when a check reports one sentence and no reason, the only way through is to make the smallest thing that fails. ⛔ **What was refused and stays refused: scrambling the image bytes so the scan cannot see a picture.** That is defeating a content check on a public page, it was offered as an option and turned down out loud, and the note is here so nobody quietly reaches for it later. **SHIPPED AS:** the published page drops the one `ART.C1` line, `artFor('C1')` returns null and `bust()` draws the ratkin silhouette that was already the fallback for faceless enemies, so nothing breaks and no other art moves. `art/C1_REPLACEMENT_BRIEF.md` holds the redraw brief, the generator advice and the exact pipeline steps. ⚠ **THE MAIN BUILD STILL HAS C1** and was deliberately not edited: a parallel session was working in it (8f.106 to 8f.108, five new paintings among them), so the removal lives only in the published copy until the replacement lands. ⚠ **Those five new paintings from 8f.107 have never been share-tested**, so if the link is refused again they are the next suspects, one probe each. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.110 | **C1 WAS A BLOCKER AND NOT THE BLOCKER, SO THE RATLING IS BACK AND THE WHOLE ARTIFACT ROUTE IS ABANDONED.** *(User, 2026-08-04, an hour after 8f.109: "it is blocked again. Stop for todya and giveme quick answer - if i use githuba and share it through git, will it work better? Reterun ratling, make the note".)* **The share was refused again with C1 gone**, which retires the single-picture theory: removing it bought nothing, so it goes back and the published page is the complete game again, all 114 paintings. **The prime remaining suspects are the five paintings a parallel session added the same day at 8f.107**, none of them ever share-tested, but they are not being chased. ⚑ **THE CALL, AND IT IS THE ENTRY:** a day of bisection produced a real mechanism (the page is judged whole, code and pictures together, payloads read by magic bytes rather than by label) and still no shareable link, because **the oracle is one bit with no reason attached and the suspect space is every byte in a 5.6MB file**. That is not a search worth continuing when a static host has no oracle at all. **GitHub Pages is the answer given**: push the file to a public repo, switch Pages on, public URL in a minute, updates by `git push`, and the `audio/` folder can ride along, so the published game stops being silent, which the artifact could never fix. Netlify Drop for speed, itch.io with a password for the real playtest. **The lesson is about knowing when a diagnosis is finished:** twenty probes bought the mechanism and one named picture, and the twenty-first would have bought a second named picture and still no link. When the constraint is somebody else's unexplained check, route around it instead of mapping it. ⚠ `window.claude` stays forbidden **for artifacts only**; on a real host the rule evaporates and the save-as-a-file row of 8f.104 could come back. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.111 | **THE GAME IS A GIT REPO NOW, AND THE SHAREABLE FILE HAS SOUND IN IT FOR THE FIRST TIME.** *(User, 2026-08-04, the morning after 8f.110 abandoned the artifact route: "I added git. Can you please put build with music and arts there, so people can actually play full version in their brwoser".)* **THE SHIP:** `git init` on `main`, 73 files, 19MB, one commit, plus a new `tools/build_site.ps1` and a root `index.html` that is the thing people play. ⚑ **THE ENTRY IS THAT "PUT THE BUILD THERE" AND "PUT THE WORKING FILE THERE" ARE DIFFERENT ACTS, AND CONFUSING THEM IS THE BUG 8f.70 SHIPPED.** `prototype/grimtoll_slice.html` has an **empty `AUDIO_EMBED` on purpose**, because at this desk `sndSrc` falls through to the 44.1k masters in `../audio/`; a static host has no such folder, so copying the working file to a host reproduces the silent build exactly. **So the hosted file is generated, never copied:** `build_site.ps1` reads the prototype, pours `audio/out/audio_data.js` into the marker block, writes `index.html`, copies `prototype/icons/` beside it and **rewrites the manifest's `start_url` from `../grimtoll_slice.html` to `../`**, which is the one thing that silently breaks when a page changes its filename. **It WARNS LOUDLY when the audio block is missing rather than writing a silent page**, because a silent page looks like nothing at all from the game's side and that is precisely how this went unnoticed for forty builds. **THE NUMBERS:** `build_audio.ps1` at aac 64k turns **79MB of WAV into 3.7MB**, 4.93MB as base64, and the page goes **5.4MB to 10.33MB** with 115 paintings and 11 cues inside it and **nothing left to fetch**. ⚑ **VERIFIED ON THE BUILT FILE AND NOT ON THE PROTOTYPE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT** - and the first attempt accidentally measured the prototype (`serve.ps1` maps `/` to it) and read `AUDIO_EMBED` **empty**, which is the exact false pass this entry is about. On `/index.html`: 12 cues embedded · `main_menu` decodes at **97.22s** and `battle` at **83.48s** against a README that says 1:37 and 1:23 · the road theme plays with `currentSrc` starting `data:` · `click`/`hit`/`coin` fire from the pool at 0.13/0.16/0.52s, their exact table durations · 64 art keys · **no console errors** · icons and manifest all 200. **WHAT IS NOT IN GIT AND WHY:** `art/src` and `audio/music` are **~210MB of masters for a 10MB page**, so `.gitignore` keeps them out along with `audio_review/` and `shots/`; they live in the Drive folder, which is where both pipelines already read them. **The base64 blocks are committed, the sources are not**, and `.gitignore` says so in prose so nobody has to guess later. ⚠ **`SNDBASE='../audio/'` IS NOW DEAD ON THE HOST** and that is accepted: from a project page it resolves above the site root. The embed always wins, so it never fires; it stays because it is what makes the working file work at this desk. ⚠ **The `.git` folder sits inside a Google Drive folder.** Drive syncing a repo mid-write is a known way to corrupt one. **NOT SHIPPED, AND IT IS THE USER'S FOUR CLICKS:** the repo has no remote, GitHub Pages is not switched on, and there is no public link yet. `gh` is not installed on this machine and creating somebody's public repo is not a thing to do unasked. Steps written out in `WHAT_TO_TEST.md`. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.112 | **DEPLOYING IS ONE COMMAND NOW, AND IT REFUSES TO SHIP A SILENT PAGE.** *(User, 2026-08-04, immediately after 8f.111: "save how to deploy it for furure - so it is fast and easy".)* **`deploy.ps1` at the root** plus **`docs/DEPLOY.md`** as the card. Five steps: encode (**skipped by default**, because ffmpeg costs 30s and the approved pack almost never changes, so the normal path is about twenty seconds) · build · **check** · commit · push, then it derives `https://<user>.github.io/<repo>/` from the remote and prints it. With no remote it prints the four clicks instead of failing. ⚑ **THE ENTRY IS STEP 3, AND IT IS A GENERAL RULE: ASSERT ON THE THING THAT SHIPS, NOT ON THE THING THAT WAS MEANT TO SHIP.** The script reads `index.html` back off the disk and counts `data:audio/` and `data:image/` in it, refusing at fewer than 11 cues or 100 pictures. Every earlier link in this project was silent while every step that produced it reported success, because **success was measured at the pour and never at the page**. **The guard was proved by a negative test, not by reading it**: `audio_data.js` was swapped for an empty table, `deploy.ps1 -NoPush` built a 5.4MB page, printed `0 audio cues`, and **exited 1 with nothing committed and nothing pushed**; the real file was restored and the rebuild came back byte-identical to the commit. ⚠ **TWO POWERSHELL 5.1 TRAPS FIXED BEFORE THEY BIT**, both the same shape: `git remote get-url origin` and `git log` write to **stderr** when they have nothing to say, and 5.1 turns a native command's stderr into an ErrorRecord, which under `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'` would kill the script **on the exact path where there is no remote yet**, which is every first run. Rewritten to ask `git remote` and test membership, a question that cannot fail. **DOCS RE-POINTED, because a stale orientation line is worse than none:** `docs/README.md` still said the build was *"~1 MB, no build step"* and *"published as a static artifact"*, three things that are all now false; it names the working file and the generated file separately and sends you to DEPLOY.md. `WHAT_TO_TEST.md` and the root `README.md` both collapsed to the one command. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.113 | **#84 SHIPPED - A DODGE AND A MISS ARE TWO DIFFERENT EVENTS, AND THE FIELD HAD BEEN TELLING THE PLAYER BOTH HALVES OF ONE LIE.** *(User, 2026-08-04: "Can you please make diffrent messeges - when dodge and when miss".)* One `if` in `strike()` handled every failed attack roll and it did two contradictory things at once: `say(a.name+' misses.')` blamed the attacker, and `fx(d,'DODGE','#8ca35a')` in the same breath credited the defender. **So a swing that went nowhere near anybody floated a green DODGE over a body that never moved, and a dog that genuinely got out of the way was reported as a bad swing.** ⚑ **ONE ROLL STILL, TWO READINGS, AND NOT ONE NEW CALL TO `Math.random`.** This is the whole entry and it is why it is four lines of code: `hitBreakdown().total` is `clamp(skill - dodge + flank + other, 5, 95)`, so **the target's dodge is already the only difference between two numbers the function has both of**. The same roll therefore answers a second question for free: `roll <= clamp(skill+flank+other,5,95)` asks *would this have landed on somebody who never moved?* If yes, the dodge is the **only** reason it did not and the defender earned the line. If no, the attacker would have missed a fence post and **the defender is credited with nothing they did not do.** Rolling a second time for the flavour would have been the obvious version and it would have been a lie of a different shape - a blow that failed by 30 points reading as a nimble dodge one time in three. ⚑ **CLAMPED THE SAME WAY `total` IS, and that is not decoration**: unclamped, a roll of 96 against a 95-capped chance on an ogre with 2 dodge reads as a dodge, and the cap exists precisely because those points are not real. Both clamps floor and ceil identically, so a target with no dodge can **never** produce a dodge line. **SIX LINES, BECAUSE ONE WORD FOR THREE WEAPONS IS WORSE THAN THREE.** A bow does not swing and a working is not sidestepped: MISS reads **"X swings wide." / "X shoots wide." / "X's working comes apart."**, DODGE reads **"Y leans back out of it." / "Y twists, and the shot goes past." / "Y is not where it lands."** - and the last of those is `hitBreakdown`'s own design note ("you do not sidestep a blight-wind, you just fail to be where it lands") finally said out loud to the player instead of only to the next programmer. **THE BOARD SAYS IT TOO, WHICH IS THE HALF THAT DOES NOT NEED READING.** The floater is **MISS in #7d9490** (the same dull grey-green the `.hitflash.miss` dashed ring has always used) against **DODGE in #8ca35a**, and `animClash` took a fourth argument: the defender's `evade` animation now plays **only when the defender is the reason**, so a wide swing leaves them standing still and the two failures are told apart at a glance with the log unread. `sfx` is keyed on the verdict rather than on whose side the target was - both names still reach the same sample, so nothing sounds different today and nothing has to be rewired the day a dodge sample lands in the pack. **Verified in the running game, at the boundary rather than by playing until it looked right:** the Unasked into an ogre reads `total=55 undodged=58`, and forced rolls print **40 HIT · 56 DODGE · 57 DODGE · 90 MISS** - the dodge band is exactly the three points of ogre dodge and not one number wider. Same test on **HUNTING BOW** (`total=50 undodged=53`: 51 dodges, 58 misses) and on **EMBER** (`total=67 undodged=68`: 68 dodges, 73 misses), and the ranged cases correctly never call `animClash` at all. `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve with no guard hits and `__errs` empty (clash 5rd · brigand 8 · pack 6 · slingline 5 · steading 8 · snare 9 · mother 6 · armour 14). **THE DISTRIBUTION IS THE INTERESTING PART AND IT WAS NOT DESIGNED, IT FELL OUT:** across the whole regression, **51 dodges to 169 misses**, and it is not spread evenly - the dog and slinger fights run 10-12 dodges apiece while **steading reads 2 and the Fen-Mother reads 0**. A ratkin has 18-28 dodge against a ~55 skill and an ogre has 3, so the ogre fights now read as *you swung badly* and the fast fights read as *it got out of the way*, which is what the numbers have always said and what the field has never once shown. **NO BALANCE CHANGE: not one roll, threshold or damage number moved.** **#84 spent, next free #85.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.114 | **#85 SHIPPED - THE DREAM, AND THE ENTRY IS THAT THE CARD HAD NO CORPSE TO BE ABOUT.** *(User, 2026-08-04, the scene written out whole: "Сон. После кого-то из твоей тимы умер. Он приходит в твоем сне. Его лицо изрядно впало а вместо глаз пустые глазницы, но лицо все еще узнаваемо. Он говорит, что ты мог бы спасти его. Но не спас и это все твоя вина. Но ты не можешь ничего сказать в ответ, а просто безмолвно. Просыпаешься в холодном поту".)* ⛔ **THE FIRST THING THIS COST WAS FINDING OUT THAT NOBODY ON THE ROSTER EVER DIES, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE AND LOAD-BEARING.** `carryEverybodyOut()` says it in one line - *"nobody is lost, the downed are scarred and come back at 30%"* - `afterBattle()` never splices a body, a lost fight at THE CIRCLE goes through `toBeaten` which scars and does not bury, and WITHDRAW was changed at some point to carry everybody out (`const lost=[]; /* nobody. */`). **Scars ARE the death system here**: units are meant to be disposable and the dilemma is sentiment versus arithmetic, not a graveyard. So the request was raised with the user before a line was written, they chose *"whoever the run has actually lost"*, and **`lostSoul()` is the whole of the answer**: exactly three states mean gone, and they are ranked by how much of it is your fault. **1 - `cut`: THE ONLY REAL DEATH IN THE BUILD, AND YOU DID IT YOURSELF.** The third door of `openReturner` - a veteran of three fights walks back to the fire and asks for his place and you cut him down for his kit. **Nothing recorded it, because nothing had ever needed to know**: `done()` splices the record out of `G.dismissed` on all three of its doors, so the man vanished from every table the game keeps. One line (`G.killedOwn=p`) and the card can finally be about the one death the player is actually guilty of. **2 - `hole`:** the sinkhole at THE GROUND OPENS (`strandOne`) has them and they have not walked out. **3 - `sent`:** dismissed, and still out there - unresolved only, which `G.dismissed` already means. ⚑ **THE DREAM IS THE ONLY THING IN THE CARD THAT CLAIMS THEY ARE DEAD, AND THE NARRATION NEVER DOES.** `travel`'s arrive rolls **50% a leg** to hand the sinkhole's man back, so a man walking out of the treeline four days after you buried him in your sleep is the best thing this card can produce - and it is safe because an incident id fires **once a run, full stop**, so it can never be dealt again to correct itself. **ONE DREAM, ONE SENTENCE OF DIFFERENCE.** The accusation is identical in all three cases because guilt does not check the roster; what changes is the evidence your eye cannot leave alone - *"There is not a mark on them. Not where you put it, not anywhere"* / *"There is no dirt on them. Nothing under the nails"* / *"They still have the bundle on their shoulder, roped the way it was roped the morning you sent them off with it."* **⚠ IT IS A CAMP INCIDENT AND NOT A ROAD NODE, AND THAT IS FORCED RATHER THAN PREFERRED:** a dream is not a place the company can decide to walk to, so it cannot be a slot on the map; between two stops, at night, with the watch already set, is the only frame the game has for it. **THE SCENE HAS NO ANSWER IN IT, SO THE MORNING CARRIES THE RULING.** The user's text ends with your mouth working and nothing coming out, which is the point of it - so the dream plays, ends, and the three doors are the only three a captain has: **say it out loud** (−1 day, +10, nobody marches and by midday somebody has told a story about them you had never heard) · **count out their share and put it back in the chest in a bag with their name on it** (−30 crowns, +6, `need:{crowns:30}`) · **kick the fire out and march** (free tonight, −7). The free door is allowed because the other two cost a day and thirty crowns, and **its price is written into its own aftermath instead of into a sequel card**: it comes back on the fourth night, you know exactly what is coming, and it makes no difference whatsoever. **`castLost` IS A FOURTH CAST GATE AND THE ONLY ONE THAT ASKS ABOUT SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT AT THE FIRE.** castRace asks *are there two ratkin here*, castTie asks *are these two particular people here*; this asks *has this company lost anybody at all*, and a clean run never sees the card. ⚑ **`a` IS THEREFORE THE ONE CAST IN `openCamp` NOT DRAWN FROM THE PARTY**, and everything downstream was checked rather than assumed: `stillHere(a)` is false so `bothHere` is false, `pa` comes back undefined and every use of it was already behind `if(pa)` or `pa&&pb` - **the dead do not get a memory on their sheet and do not form ties.** The memory falls to `pb` instead, scoped to this gate on purpose, because everywhere else a missing `pa` means somebody left mid-chain and handing their line to the other person would be rewriting whose scene it was. **⚠ AND `b` PREFERS SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT YOU:** `src` is the whole shuffled party, so a company dismissed down to one body would have cast the Captain as the man on watch watching himself wake up. It also excludes `gone.p.id` outright - belt-and-braces, since every real path splices first - and carries a solo line for a party of one, because preferring is not guaranteeing. **⚠ A PRE-EXISTING SAVE BUG CAME OUT WITH IT, AND IT IS EXACTLY #38'S GAP IN THE SAME BLOCK: `G.stranded` HAS NEVER BEEN SAVED.** `dismissed` is on the line above it and always was; the person the sinkhole took was not, so **closing the tab quietly killed anybody who was down that hole** and the 50%-a-leg roll that walks them back out had nothing left to walk. Both `killedOwn` and `stranded` go into `flags` now, restored by the generic `Object.keys(r.flags).forEach`. Both hold a roster object that is never in `G.party`, so the reload makes a separate copy on purpose and **everything that compares them does it by `id`**. **Verified in the running game rather than reasoned about:** `LINT()` **0 findings** (with a new check - `castLost` beside any other cast gate is a card whose other gate `openCamp` silently ignores) · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, no guard hits, `__errs` empty (clash 4rd · brigand 12 · pack 6 · slingline 4 · steading 11 · snare 9 · mother 6 · armour 13) · the gate reads **null / not-in-pool on a clean run** and opens on all three states, ranking `cut` over `hole` over `sent` · the real path drives it end to end: dismiss → returner card → third door → `G.killedOwn` set to the right id, `G.dismissed` emptied, the card in the pool · all three evidence sentences render against the right `how` · **200 crowns → 170** on the middle door and at **12 crowns** that door greys out reading *"cannot afford"* while the other two stay live and no fallback option is appended · a party of one prints *"There is nobody on watch. There has not been for some time."* and never names the Captain · **save → reload → `lostSoul()` returns the same person by id, still not in the party** · dealt twice in one run: **false**. **OPEN, and small:** `openCamp` draws its picture from a hardcoded `pickOne(['E4','E8','E9','E7'])`, which is four daylight paintings shared by all 19 camp cards, so **the dream gets a sunlit rock**. Per-card art on a camp incident is a separate decision and `.claude/rules/static-event-art.md` owns what may be painted. **#85 spent, next free #86.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.115 | **THE DOCS WERE CUT DOWN, BECAUSE THEY HAD THE SAME DISEASE THE PLAYTESTERS REPORTED.** *(User, 2026-08-10, with new feedback: "The biggest thing: lack of clarity and too many options/systems from the start" + "I think this version we will work only on it. Can you please clean backlog and other docks. And structurise for me backlog in readeable view".)* ⛔ **The finding is that `00_PLAN_AND_BACKLOG.md` was running FOUR overlapping priority systems at once** - a thirteen-step gate plan, three date buckets, a 1/2/3/4 tier strip, and six screen groups - each individually reasonable, and which together meant a session had to reconcile four orders before it could pick anything up. **Two of them openly contradicted each other in writing** (*"the buckets say #13 first, the tier strip says #24"*, with a paragraph explaining which wins). Every entry also appeared in two or three places, which is the aliased-source-of-truth shape this project has shipped as a bug more than once. **That is the players' own complaint applied to the working file, which is why it was worth doing before any build.** **Collapsed to ONE order: 🔴 NOW · 🟡 NEXT · ⚪ LATER · ⏸ PARKED · 👤 YOURS**, with the feedback itself at the top as the current focus and a rule under it that decides the whole pass: **nothing is added, the only moves are cut, delay and merge** - because the report is *too much at the start*, so a new tutorial layer or a new hint card would be the wrong direction by definition. **#86 was claimed for it**, THE FIRST FIFTEEN MINUTES: it opens with a measurement step (words, cards, clicks and systems from launch to the first decision that costs something) because the opening is *crowded, not empty*, and it holds three candidate cuts plus **#60's per-round-cap remainder - which had been sitting in `SHIPPED.md` describing this exact complaint a week before it was made**. **Sizes:** backlog **3,013 → 282** lines · `README.md` **1,148 → 429** · `WHAT_TO_TEST.md` **3,822 → 559** · `SHIPPED.md` **203 → 115**, re-cut to one line per entry with every open remainder kept verbatim, because the remainders are the only actionable thing in that file. **Nothing was deleted.** Eight files under `docs/archive/`: four dated snapshots of the docs as they stood, plus **`BACKLOG_ENTRY_SPECS.md`** (every unbuilt entry's full text, and the file a session opens when it picks one up), **`PLAN_REASONING.md`** (why an entry waits, what was rejected and on what grounds, the user's words), `README_WHERE_IT_STOOD.md` and `WHAT_TO_TEST_OLDER.md`. ⚠ **Two stale facts fell out on the way through**: `SHIPPED.md` still said *"next free number: #81"* while the backlog header said #86, and its two number lists had drifted out of agreement with each other - which is the exact failure that section exists to prevent, and it went unnoticed because nobody rereads a registry. **Next free is #87.** ⏳ **The rest of the feedback is still coming**; the focus block has a marked place for it and the work below it is scoped to the one line that has arrived. | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.116 | **#87 SHIPPED - THE VERB HAS A SHAPE, BECAUSE THIRTY SKILLS HAD BEEN SHARING THREE ANIMATIONS.** *(User, 2026-08-10: "Can you create proper animation for all skill moves so far in the game? So they are readible visully? It could be small - but distinct. If push or kick - kind of wind in that direction. If sweep or scare - to show that it has impact".)* **THE STATE IT FOUND:** `.lunge` for the attacker, `.recoil`/`.evade` for the defender, `.hitflash` + `.spark` at the contact point, and `.shot` for a projectile. That is the whole vocabulary, and it was written for one act - a body hitting another body. So **a KICK looked exactly like a sword**, **a SWEEP played one sword-swing per neighbour** (it resolves as one `strike()` per adjacent body, so a brute in a ring of four played four lunges in four directions inside a third of a second and read as four attacks for two actions), and **ROAR · COMMAND · HOLD THE LINE · SPEAR WALL · THE FEN ANSWERS · SHE CALLS THEM IN put nothing on the board at all** - one line of floating text on a log the player is not looking at. ⚑ **THE ENTRY IS EIGHT PRIMITIVES AND NOT THIRTY ANIMATIONS, AND EACH ONE IS PICKED FROM WHAT AN ACT *DOES*.** `GUST` (displacement) · `ARC` (a sweep) · `RING` (a radius) · `TETHER` (one body has hold of another) · `MOTES` (something landed and stayed) · `PLATE` (something closed over you) · `SHUDDER` (it reached you where you stand) · `GHOST` (the body was here and is not). The dispatcher reads `act.push` / `act.pull` / `act.sweep` / `act.fear` / `act.bog` / `act.rally` / `act.buff` / `act.grasp` / `act.venom` / `act.cripple` / `act.drag` / `act.bounce`, **never a name**, for the same reason `gathers` is derived rather than declared: the next act that grows a `push` gets its gust the day it is written, without being told. ⚑ **TWO DOORS, WHICH IS WHY BOTH AI BRAINS AND AUTO GOT IT FOR FREE.** Actor-centred shapes hang off **`spend()`**, the one function every paid act in the game passes through - `aiTurn`, `autoStep` and the player's own click all call it, so the enemy ogre's sweep and yours are the same picture and neither brain was taught anything. Blow-borne shapes hang off **`strike()`**, which is likewise where both sides resolve damage. Only four verbs needed a hand-placed call (`grasp`, `distract`, `hex`, `buff`), and all four are player-class acts no enemy carries, reached through `clickHex`, which is also AUTO's door. ⚑ **THE TWO OPPOSITIONS ARE WHERE THE TEACHING ACTUALLY LIVES.** **A ring travels outward and means broadcast; a plate snaps inward and means protection** - one difference, and it is what tells COMMAND (+morale, ring alone) from HOLD THE LINE (+dodge, ring *then* plates) with no word on the screen. And **a recoil means something hit you and you went back; a shudder means something reached you where you stand** - the scare verbs displace nobody, so borrowing the recoil would have told the player a hex had changed hands when none had, which is the same class of lie #84 closed between a dodge and a miss. ⚑ **THE RING IS DRAWN AT THE ACT'S REAL RADIUS IN HEXES, MEASURED OFF THE LIVE DOM.** `hexSpan()` asks the board how far apart two neighbouring hexes actually are, through the same `relPt()` contract `hexPt` uses, so ROAR at 2 and THE SOUND SHE MAKES at 5 are visibly different sizes **at all three camera stops** - a hardcoded 42px would have drawn a two-hex ROAR as a four-hex one at CLOSE. Verified: 198px against 494px on a 49.4px span, exactly 2 and 5 hexes of diameter. ⚠ **`animClash` TOOK A FIFTH ARGUMENT AND IT IS THE SWEEP FIX**: the attacker's lunge is suppressed when `act.sweep`, so the arc IS the swing and the defenders still recoil, because each of them really was hit. Measured after: **one `swarc`, three `hitflash`, two `recoil`, zero attacker lunges.** ⚠ **THE `aoe` RING BELONGS AT THE RELEASE AND NOT AT THE CLICK, and that was caught by testing rather than by reading**: every aoe school in the game is arcane with a `dmg` pair, so `gathers` is true for all of them and `clickHex`'s own aoe branch is **unreachable** - BLIGHT-WIND is only *named* on the click and comes down a round later in `releaseGather`. Drawing it where it was paid for would have put the picture on the wrong turn. **Verified in the running game:** `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, no guard hits, `__errs` empty (clash 6rd · brigand 7 · pack 6 · slingline 9 · steading 9 · snare 8 · mother 9 · armour 8) · all eight primitives spawn with `--pace` applied (0.42s → 0.735s at ×1.75) · **KICK** 3 gusts and the body one hex back, and correctly **nothing at all against an ogre**, because `maxSize` refuses first · **VENOM** 5 motes · **CRIPPLE** 6 gusts at the feet · **DRAG UNDER** tether then inward gust · **THE SOUND SHE MAKES** ring plus a shudder on all four bodies it reached · **BLIGHT-WIND** tether when named, ring when it lands · **DISENGAGE** ghost · **a plain sword swing spawns nothing**, so nothing regressed. **PICTURES, and the first one is the gate's:** `shots/87_skill_animations.html` is a **live sheet holding the exact CSS that ships**, 23 verbs looping at the real pace with a ×1 / ×1.75 / ×2.50 switch, because **a still frame cannot show an animation** and a hand-drawn mockup of motion would have been a lie the real screen could not tell. `shots/87_in_game_verbs.html` is six of the eight spawned into the live `#bFx` on the real board. ⚠ **NOBODY HAS WATCHED THIS ANIMATE IN THE GAME.** The hidden preview pane composites no frames, so every claim above is a DOM-and-duration assertion; the sheet is the only thing a human eye has been able to judge. ⚠ **AND IT ADDS MOTION TO A SCREEN THE 2026-08-10 FEEDBACK ALREADY CALLED CROWDED.** It adds no card, no option and no word of text - it is legibility, which is complaint A - but the per-round motion budget is a real question for #86's measurement step, and it is written into the focus block rather than left to be discovered. **Everything is in the `prefers-reduced-motion` block with the existing impact FX.** ⚠ **FIVE NUMBER COLLISIONS NOW: this was written as #86 and renumbered afterwards**, because 8f.115 claimed #86 and the build log the same day. The grep was run at plan time and the collision arrived between plan and write. **Grep at WRITE time, and grep the build log too, not only the backlog.** **#87 spent, next free #88.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.117 | **#88 SHIPPED - THE PANEL YOU READ A TURN OFF, AND THE CARD WAS STRUCTURALLY UNABLE TO SHOW ITS OWN CONTENTS.** *(User, 2026-08-10, the second round of the clarity feedback, with a screenshot of the archer's action panel and three red boxes drawn on the hotkeys: «1) What is hard - understanding what is going on even in the menu - very small type and lots of numbers everywhere. 2) It took me a very long time to see where to read a character's step count - also hidden in small type. 3) Make the hot keys either smaller or not overlapping (screenshot).»)* ⛔ **ALL THREE COMPLAINTS HAD ONE CAUSE AND IT WAS MEASURABLE, NOT A MATTER OF TASTE.** The skill list was a two-column grid. Two columns of 133px leave a card **116px of inner width**, and measured on 8f.116, the build he played: the longest skill name in the game, POISON THE BLADE, is **112px** at 11px Cinzel and with its icon and gap wants **128px** of that 116px · any act carrying a cooldown wrapped its cost line onto a second line, so **KICK and CRIPPLING SHOT held 48px of content inside a 46px box** · and the hotkey was `position:absolute;left:4px;top:3px`, which is the exact corner the icon occupies, so **it sat ON the icon on 5 cards out of 5**. **The card could not be made more legible because there was nowhere to put the type.** ⚑ **SO THE LIST WENT TO ONE COLUMN, AND THAT ONE MOVE PAID FOR EVERYTHING ELSE.** A full-width row has **213px** of inner width against 116px. Every skill name in the game now fits on one line - the widest, THE SWORD COMES ROUND at 182.6px, clears the 195px text budget with 12px over, checked against all 59 names in the file - and the type went **UP** while the crowding went away: name 11 → 12.5px, cost line 9 → 10.5px and off the watermark grey, icon 12 → 13.5px, hotkey 8.5 → 10.5px. ⚑ **THE HOTKEY GETS A RESERVED GUTTER, NOT A NUDGED OFFSET, AND THAT DISTINCTION IS THE ENTRY.** `.act` carries `padding-left:25px` and the key is centred in it; the cooldown has its twin gutter on the right at `padding-right:30px`. A moved badge would have been correct until the next long name; **a column nothing else may enter survives a longer name, a taller card and a second icon.** And it got **bigger** rather than smaller - the report offered «smaller or not overlapping», and a hotkey you cannot read is not a hotkey, so the overlap was the actual fault. ⚑ **STEP DID NOT GET A LABEL, IT GOT A HOME: the two things a turn spends are now one box at one size.** It used to be one of five integers in the 9.5px grey grid at the FOOT of the panel, which is exactly why he could not find it - a player asking «how far can I walk» has no reason on earth to look there. It is now the second big number in the bordered box at the top, beside ACTIONS, reading off **the same `moveBudget(u)` call** the MOVE button, the reach preview, `aiTurn` and `autoStep` all make, so the loud number cannot drift from the hexes the board is about to light up. **It teaches its own rules by changing in front of you:** a second move in one turn is a hex shorter (verified: the box drops 4 → 3 on `movesUsed=1`), water and an open bloom take more off, crippled halves it. ⚑ **THE THREE ACTION PIPS PAID FOR THE ROOM AND WERE THE CHEAPEST THING ON THE PANEL TO LOSE**, because the numeral beside them already said «3 OF 3» in words - three ways of saying one fact is complaint 1 in miniature. ⚑ **AND INIT LEFT THE PANEL ENTIRELY.** The ORDER strip along the top of the field already shows initiative, and it shows it for **everybody**, which is the only arrangement in which the number means anything; one row of a ranking, in 9.5px grey, at the bottom of a different box, was noise. The copy went and the original got bigger (8 → 9.5px). **Five integers became three.** **Nothing was added: every move here is a cut, a merge or a promotion of something that already existed**, which is the rule the clarity pass runs on. **Also raised on the same screen:** `#bClass` 9 → 10px · `.statLine` 9 → 10.5px · `#bDerived` 9.5 → 11.5px · the hover readout `.rr` 9.5 → 11px with the card widened 240 → 262px to hold it without wrapping, and **it is the card the player reads before every swing** · the log's round rule 8.5 → 9.5px · `#bTrait` clamped to two lines, with the full text already one hover away, because a wordy trait ran to four lines and **shoved the skill list down the panel according to whose turn it was**. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME, and the instrument is `tools/harness.js`:** **0 overlaps and 0 clipped cards across 184 cards / 35 unit panels / 8 fights**, with cooldowns and used-counters forced on so the corner badges were showing - against **5 out of 5 overlapping** on the same two panels before · `LINT()` **0 findings** · `regress()` all eight fights resolve, `__errs` empty (clash 5rd · brigand 8 · pack 6 · slingline 7 · steading 8 · snare 10 · mother 20 · armour 7) · every hotkey 1-7 pressed on the archer, the Captain and the warmage, and **every selection lands visible**. **PICTURES:** `shots/88_panel_before.html` and `shots/88_panel_after.html` - the same two bodies in the same fight with the same forced badge states, captured with `grabUI()`/`shotUI()`: the archer from his screenshot, and the warmage, who is the worst case in the game. ⚠ **THE ONE OPEN REMAINDER, AND IT IS MEASURED: THE WARMAGE SCROLLS.** Seven acts want 317px and the list box is 259-270px, so Marrow's list overflows by **47-58px, about one card**. Every other body in the game fits with room (archer 5, Captain 6, rabble 3). `#bActions` is an `overflow-y:auto` scroller with a styled thumb and **the selected card scrolls itself into view**, so no hotkey can select something off-screen - but a skill you have to scroll to is still a skill you might not know you have. **The two candidate cuts that would close it are the panel's `#bDerived` block (41px, and all three of its numbers exist somewhere better) and merging the ARMOUR/HITPOINTS labels onto their own bars (26px). Neither is taken here: they are the user's call, not a session's.** ⚠ **AND THE REST OF THE GAME IS UNTOUCHED.** The battle screen was the whole screenshot and the whole of all three complaints, so that is the whole scope - but the style block still holds **61 declarations below 10px** on the other screens (2 at 7px, 5 at 7.5px, 9 at 8px, 16 at 8.5px, 13 at 9px, 16 at 9.5px). That number is written down here so the next pass has a target and not an impression. **#88 spent, next free #89.** | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.118 | **#90 SHIPPED - FOUR BATTLEFIELDS, AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT SAID THERE HAD ONLY EVER BEEN ONE.** *(User, 2026-08-11: «create 3-4 different battfield patterns for fields using in a fights in the first act (diffrent position of rocks, trees and other objects)».)* ⛔ **THE ARGUMENT FOR THE ENTRY IS A DUMP OF THE NINE BOARDS THE ACT ACTUALLY GENERATED, AND IT IS WORSE THAN THE REQUEST IMPLIES.** **Seven of the nine fights ran the same code** - two to four random blobs of `rock`, and the only dial between them was the blob COUNT; the camp and the outpost were the only boards in the game with a shape somebody chose. **Not one act-1 fight contained a single tree**: `scat:'trees'` was a label meaning "three clumps, cap sixteen", `TERRAIN.fen` sets no `trees` count and `plantTrees` is guarded on that count, so **six fights were tagged with the name of an object none of them had**. **And the mass landed where nothing meets** - you deploy in columns 0-3 and they deploy in 10-13, so the lines meet in **columns 5-9**; across the nine seeds the obstacle sat at rows 9-12 on five boards, and on the Thing in Armour it sat at **columns 1-5, inside your own approach, behind you before the fight started**. The middle of every board was empty and the terrain was decoration. ⚑ **THE RULE THAT DECIDED WHAT GETS AN ENTRY, and it is #61's rule one layer down: A PATTERN IS A DECISION THE GROUND FORCES; A DENSITY IS NOT A PATTERN.** "Four blobs instead of three" is a density. "One thing, and you go round it north or south" is a decision. That is the whole of why there are four and not eleven, and each is named for the question it asks. **ANVIL** - *which side of it do you go round*: one mass, one row above centre so the two ways round are not the same length, and the teaching fight gets it because Blood on the Road already carries eleven Captain lessons and a side to pick, so its ground may ask exactly one question. **SPINE** - *which gap do you take, and can you change your mind*: **two hexes thick, because one is not a wall** - a single file leaves the column beside it open the whole height of the board, which is the exact hole #68 found in the outpost's first line - with walled ends and **two gaps, two rows each, seven rows apart, neither of them the middle row**. **BROKEN** - *is there a stone between you and the shot*: eleven boulders and **no two of them touching**, and that `hdist>=2` test IS the pattern rather than tidiness, because #82 makes a rock that touches a rock TALL and a rock alone MED, so it is the only board in the game where every obstacle is cover you shoot over and thrust over instead of a lane you lose - and #46 makes every one liftable, so it doubles as an ogre's ammunition dump. **FLANKS** - *the short way in the open, or the long way out of sight*: the spine inverted, and **the masses are ragged and that is load-bearing** - solid ones would make the open middle the only road, which is #33's queue with scenery on it. Plus **OPEN**, which the Sling-Line keeps on purpose because cover on that board deletes the fight. ⚑ **THE MATERIAL IS THE TERRAIN'S, NOT THE PATTERN'S**, so the same spine is a run of boulders on the ridge and a line of trees in the fen: #82 already made them behave alike when massed and #46 already made them differ when alone, so the shape can be shared and the noun cannot. ⛔ `broken` is the one exception and is always stone, because its entire point is #82's MED height and a lone tree is TALL. ⚠ **EVERY PATTERN OWNS ITS BAND AND WIPES IT FIRST**: the base roll still sprinkles ~1.5% gravel, and one stray pebble beside a placed boulder promotes BOTH from cover to a wall - a pattern the dice can silently contradict is not a pattern. **And both gaps in the spine and the whole open middle of the flanks are `keep()`-ed into `LAYOUT_KEEP`**, so #62's trees, #64's flowers and #63's channel may not close the one hex the board is about - which is #33's own lesson being reused rather than rediscovered. ⚑ **TWO FIGHTS WERE WRITTEN DOWN ON INTENT AND SENT BACK BY THE MEASUREMENT, AND BOTH FINDINGS OUTLIVE THE ROWS THEY CHANGED.** **THE CIRCLE** (#74's unwinnable fight) was authored as `anvil` and would have shipped **winnable**: n=8 a side, anvil 4/8 won, flanks 3/8, spine 2/8, open 1/8, the old roll 0/8 and **broken 0/8 at 6.8 rounds**. The reading is one sentence - **against ten bodies any wall is a chokepoint, and a chokepoint is exactly how six beat ten**; lone boulders are the only obstacle that gives cover without giving a line to hold, so #74 now holds harder than the accident it replaces. **THE FEN-MOTHER** was authored as `broken` and is now **the one fight in the act #90 does not re-ground at all**: measured n=16 a side, her old roll gives **10.6 rounds and 15/16** against a deliberate anvil's **15.8 and 13/16** - same win rate inside the noise, and a boss running half again as long is a pacing change #50 owns, not a terrain session. So the retired generator survives as a named pattern, `strewn`, live for exactly one row, on #64's own precedent (*"a hand-tuned boss whose round band cost real work"*) - **and her board is byte-identical to the one she was tuned on, checked hex by hex**. ⚑ **CHASING THAT GAP PRODUCED THE ENTRY'S SECOND FINDING AND IT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE ROW IT SAVED: A CENTRED MASS TAXES WHOEVER NEEDS RANGE.** The same anvil moved to the top edge measures **16.4rd 7/8 against 17.0rd 5/8 in the middle** - the mass itself costs almost nothing, and standing it between a shooter and a closing boss costs a great deal. **So her board was never neutral: its blob happened to sit ON HER APPROACH, and the 12-14 band quoted ever since is partly the work of an accident of seed 29.** 👤 Whether to re-tune her onto a real arrangement is the user's ruling and is written into the code beside the table. **VERIFIED IN THE RUNNING GAME, instrument `tools/harness.js`:** **135 boards generated** - every fight x every pattern x every practice-field ground - **all pass `terrainCheck`, narrowest choke 3** (fails below 2, and #33's queue is choke 1) · `LINT()` **0 findings**, with two new structure rules added (9e: a fight naming a pattern that is not in PATTERNS, which would silently be fought on bare ground; and a pattern with no stated question, which is a density wearing a pattern's name) · **A/B at n=10 a side against the old generator, every fight inside the noise floor**, and the floor is measured rather than assumed: the camp runs identical code on both sides and moves 6.0→6.2 rounds, the Fen-Mother's byte-identical board moves 13.3→11.4, so ±2 rounds is noise (clash 4.9→4.9 10/10 · brigand 7.7→8.7 9/10 · slingline 6.7→6.0 · steading 11.5→8.5 0/10 · armour 8.6→6.7 9/10 · snare 9.6→8.7 6/10 · circle 7.3→7.7 **0/10 both**) · `__errs` empty throughout. **PICTURE:** `shots/90_battlefield_patterns.html` - three panels, six boards, **every one of them serialised out of the running generator in the practice field and none of it drawn**, with the old roll beside the new anvil for the same fight. ⚠ **NOTHING WAS ADDED TO THE PLAYER'S SCREEN**, which is the clarity pass's rule: no card, no option, no hint, no word of text, and **deliberately no fourth column on the practice field** - each pattern is reached by picking the fight that carries it. ⚠ **AND THIS WAS VERY NEARLY THE SIXTH NUMBER COLLISION.** It was written as **#89** and renumbered to #90 mid-session after `shots/89_battle_redo_variants.html` turned up on disk, timestamped twenty minutes earlier by a parallel session working the same file. **The tell was not in any document - both docs still said "next free #89" at the time.** ⚑ **So the check that would have caught it is `ls -t shots/`, and it is now in the rule: grep the build log AND all four docs AND the prototype AND `shots/`.** **#90 spent, #89 released unused, next free #92** (#91 is the parallel session's battle-screen redo). | ✅ DONE |
| 8f.119 | **#92 SHIPPED - THE COLLISION GUARDS. THE NEXT FREE NUMBER STOPPED BEING A SENTENCE.** *(User, 2026-08-11: «это ж окей, что я по проекту работаю в нескольких паралельных сессиях, верно? Как я понял - главное правило не комитить?» then «yes, write some logic to prevent collisions».)* ⛔ **THE ANSWER TO THE QUESTION WAS NO: "do not commit" IS THE WRONG RULE AND IT IS THE ONE THAT BITES.** `git worktree list` returns **one line**. Both sessions edit the same bytes on the same disk, so a branch isolates nothing between them and refusing to commit only removes the restore point. ⚑ **THE DIAGNOSIS THAT DECIDED THE BUILD, and it was written in the registry paragraph itself the whole time:** *"both docs still said next free #89 while both sessions were running, because the code and the shots get written before the docs do."* **The counter lived in a sentence in a document, and the document is written LAST.** So *"grep before you take a number"* was never a discipline problem and could not have worked: at the instant session B greps, session A has decided and written nothing. **Five collisions were answered five times with a stricter instruction to grep.** ⚑ **A COUNTER THAT LIVES IN PROSE IS NOT A COUNTER, IT IS A NOTE ABOUT A COUNTER.** **THE MECHANISM IS ONE PROPERTY:** `tools/claim.ps1` reserves a number by creating a file with **`CreateNew`**, which throws if the file exists, and NTFS makes that check-and-create **one indivisible operation** - so two sessions asking in the same instant cannot both win, the loser takes the next number and never learns there was a race. Nothing else in this repo has that property; a counter in a document does not, and neither does a check-then-write in a script, because **the gap between the check and the write is where the collision lives**. Three layers, because one is never enough: **claim** before (`claim.ps1 number`), **lock** during (`claim.ps1 lock`, one session owns the prototype), **verify** at the gate (`.git/hooks/pre-commit` refuses a commit spending a number somebody else holds). **AND TWO COLLISION SURFACES WORSE THAN THE NUMBERS WERE FOUND ON THE WAY.** `art/inject.ps1` **reads the whole prototype and writes the whole prototype back**, which is the one shape that erases a parallel edit outright, and it is now gated. ⚠ **But the worst is `deploy.ps1`: it runs `git add -A` and then PUSHES**, so a deploy from one session **publishes the other session's half-finished prototype to the live link**. It is gated too, and it now **prints every file `-A` is about to sweep up** before committing, because a deploy quietly carrying eight files you did not touch is how the other session's work ends up in your commit. **ENFORCED WITHOUT ANYBODY REMEMBERING**, because a script you must remember to run prevents nothing: a `PreToolUse` hook in `.claude/settings.json` **blocks Write/Edit on a file another live session holds** and tells that session why (exit 2, stderr, no JSON schema to drift), plus the two build gates and the commit gate. **All four are escapable** - `-Steal`, `release`, `--no-verify` - because a guard you cannot get past is a guard somebody deletes; and a lock **expires after 4 hours** so a session that ends without releasing does not block the desk. ⚠ **THE SCAN'S ONE REAL TRAP, FOUND BY SHIPPING IT WRONG: `#NN` AND A THREE-DIGIT CSS HEX COLOUR ARE THE SAME STRING.** The first cut read **`#373` out of a stylesheet and issued it as a backlog number**. Entry numbers are read from **prose and `shots/` only** (fenced and inline code stripped, capped at 200); `8f.NNN` is unambiguous and read from everywhere. **And `shots/` is in the scan because it is the only honest source**: it is written first, and `ls -t shots/` is what caught both near-misses on 2026-08-11. A second pass taught it that **"next free #92" is a POINTER, not a use** - old build-log rows are frozen by this file's own rule, so every such phrase left standing would burn a real number for good. **VERIFIED, and then verified by accident:** gate/lock/steal/expiry, the hook's three decisions (other session blocked · holder allowed · unlocked file allowed) and a staged-commit collision were all exercised and all behaved. ⚑ **Then the guard fired for real on its first afternoon**: a genuinely parallel session, `38e9215b`, took the prototype lock and was issued **#93 and 8f.120** while this one held **#92 and 8f.119** - the sixth collision, prevented instead of discovered afterwards. **Rules: [`PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md`](PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md).** The "next free number" headline in this file's siblings is gone and now points at the command. ⚠ **This row's own numbers are frozen like every other row here: do not read a number out of the changelog, ask `claim.ps1` for one.** | ✅ DONE |

Replaying a fight gave the same field for ever. ⚑ **SO A PLACE OWNS A `pool` INSTEAD OF A PATTERN, AND THE POOL IS WHAT THAT TERRAIN CAN PLAUSIBLY BUILD OUT OF WHAT IS LYING AROUND.** That sentence is the whole design and it answers the request literally: **the ridge draws from six** because stone is the one material that does every arrangement (it stacks into a spine, scatters into cover, piles into an anvil, lies about as rubble, and sometimes leaves the ground bare), **the fen from five**, **the forest from four**, **the swamp and the plains from three** because a drowned channel and a dry plain are already arrangements and a wall laid across either makes it somewhere else. ⚑ **TWO DICE, NOT ONE, AND THEY ARE INDEPENDENT ON PURPOSE: THE POOL DRAW PICKS THE ARRANGEMENT, THE SEED DRAW PICKS EVERYTHING ELSE** - where the water lies, where each stone falls inside that arrangement, where the litter is. **That split is what lets a fight vary honestly even when its arrangement is fixed**, and it is how the Sling-Line draws from a pool of one and still never gives you the same field twice. ⚑ **`strewn` STOPS BEING AN EMBARRASSMENT.** #90 kept the retired random-blob generator alive for one boss and apologised for it in a comment; the moment variety is the point, "broken country nobody arranged" is an honest member of the pool, and it is now in three of them. ⚠ **THE FOREST'S MASSES MOVED TO STONE, and it is the one material decision made on legibility rather than plausibility.** A wood is the obvious material for a wood and it is invisible: against twenty-six scattered trunks a stand of six more is not an arrangement, it is more forest. An outcrop reads instantly, which also makes the forest **the only ground in the game carrying both materials at once** (measured on the gate picture: 32 timber and 11-17 stone). ⛔ **A POOL IS A PROMISE ABOUT WHAT MAY HAPPEN TO A FIGHT, NOT A LIST OF WHAT LOOKS NICE**, and four rows narrow their terrain's, every one of them measured. **THE CIRCLE** keeps lone boulders alone, because #74's whole premise is that you cannot win it and **any wall is a chokepoint, and a chokepoint is exactly how six beat ten**. **THE SLING-LINE** draws from one, and this is the only narrowed pool that is not about tuning: cover there does not make the fight harder or easier, it makes it a *different* fight, because "cross the open" is the entire brief. **THE FEN-MOTHER** does not roll at all and **`seed:29` is the only pin left in the table** - rolling her seed while holding her arrangement measures **15.7rd worst 27 won 5/6 against 11.5rd worst 17 won 6/6 pinned**, which is #90's own finding arriving a second time (her board is tuned by *where* the blob lands relative to her approach, so a fresh blob is a fresh difficulty), and **#32 was filed against this exact fight for never ending**. Variety is not worth reopening that. **AND BLOOD ON THE ROAD DRAWS FROM THREE RATHER THAN FIVE**, which is a clarity-pass decision and not a terrain one: `spine` and `broken` are held back because a wall that punishes committing to the wrong gap, and eleven separate objects to keep track of, are each a second lesson on a board already teaching eleven Captain lessons. **They arrive at the next fight, which is what "delay it rather than explain it" means.** ⚑ **AND THE ENTRY CORRECTED #90'S OWN TABLE ON THE WAY, WHICH IS THE FINDING WORTH KEEPING: THE `0/8` ROWS WERE NEVER 0.** Re-run at **n=40 a side**, the Circle is **4/40 rolled against 2/40 pinned** - no difference, so the seed does not move that fight - but both are five to ten percent, not zero. The ARRANGEMENT still matters enormously (`anvil` at 4/8 is a different order of magnitude, not a sampling artefact), but ⛔ **n=6 CANNOT TELL 0% FROM 20%**, and across these two entries **three decisions were nearly taken on eight runs and two of them would have been wrong**. the Snare drew `broken` three times in four when it was left unpinned, which is why panel 1 is pinned and says so. ⚑ **AND THIS IS THE FIRST ENTRY IN THE PROJECT WHOSE NUMBER WAS ISSUED RATHER THAN GREPPED FOR:** 8f.119's `claim.ps1` handed this session #93 and 8f.120 while the other held #92 and 8f.119, and its `PreToolUse` hook **blocked an edit to the prototype mid-session** while that session owned it. chip**. ⚑ **THE ENTRY'S CENTRE IS ONE VARIABLE, `offA` - THE OFFER ACT.** With an attack selected it is that attack; with MOVE standing (**the default now - nobody clicks MOVE again, render() re-arms it after every act, ESC included**) it is the weapon in their hands, so the board shows **move reach AND priced targets AT ONCE** - and the lit hex, the odds, the damage preview, the lane word, the readout card and the accepting click all read the same variable through one gate (`offerAtk`), so **nothing can be lit and then refused**, which is the project's oldest UI contract applied to its newest layer.
