In the running build (his own day-1 save, backed up and restored byte-identical): a level-4 spearwoman with 2 scars, 1 change and 2 memories runs **1241px of sheet in a 678px window (183%), 564 words**, the SKILLS block alone is 281px/230 words re-stating what the battle's hover already says, the four main stats sit ~520px down BELOW the history prose, and gear is a text grid that never touches the body it hangs on. **`shots/133_company_sheet.html` is the answer as a clickable template** (live tabs, hover pops, a spendable perk point) beside **`shots/133_current_capture.html`**, the real screen serialized from the build with every canvas inlined. The proposed shape: stats FIRST under a three-line identity head · the doll IS the gear map (one silhouette per race, four slots anchored to body parts, the off hand SHOWS the two-handed conflict, scars as red marks on the part + chips beside, a change glows teal and names the skill it grants, the battle sprite in the corner as the "on the field" tile) · skills as the battle's own cards with the battle's own pop and nothing longer · the story at two memories + roads, up from one line · perks behind a ★ tab whose BADGE keeps a banked point visible from every tab (the "invisible perk point" lesson survives the tab wall). Centre column measured at **676px in a 676px window, zero scroll, 272 words** for the same character. ⏸ **The bags he floated ("maybe one bag... for throwables") are PARKED with the honest finding: no throwable and no swap rule exist, so a bag today is furniture** - if ruled in they arrive WITH their rule as their own entry. ⚠ One process lesson for the next template: **base64 is never retyped by hand** - a hand-copied sprite picked up two Cyrillic look-alike characters and the fix was patching all three imgs from the harvested JSON by script. Spec with the full verbatim transcript: `docs/archive/BACKLOG_ENTRY_SPECS.md` #133. **The prototype is untouched; the build round waits on his pick and his three open questions (doll style · default tab · where the two spend-moments live).** | 🎨 DESIGN |
| 8f.161b | **#133 BUILT: THE COMPANY SHEET IS ONE SCREEN.** *(User, the same night, after the template: "so can you update it according of spec first as prototype - use a lot references".)* The design round above, built and verified in the running game. **The order inverted** (identity three lines → stats band → body → skills → history, where it was prose-then-numbers), **the doll became a body** (`DOLLBODY{}`, one parametric anatomy with a per-race table, so a ratkin is small, hunched and tailed and an ogre is a mass, and `partOf`'s keys still exist for all three or a veteran's scars would pile up on the wrong limb), **the gear left its text grid and hung on the body** (four slots in two columns with drawn leaders; `drawDoll` now exports `cv._anchor`, the joints it actually drew, so a leader lands on the ogre's shoulder and the ratkin's without a second table), **the skills became the battle's own cards** (`.iact` re-cut from `.act` for a parchment ground, same furniture in the same places, the pop fed by the same `noteLine()`), and **the stash and the tree became tabs** with a ★ badge carrying the banked-point count across the wall. Two things came in from his reference screenshots rather than from the spec: **Wildermyth's before/after** (a stash item states what it CHANGES, ▲▼, computed from values already in `GEAR{}` and shown only where there is something to replace) and **a mutation's skill saying where it came from** (teal card, "FROM THE CHANGE", off a one-line `MUTACT{}`). ⚑ **Measured, same character, same window: 1241px and 564 words to 678px and 242, no scroll.** Verified across all three race dolls, equip, strip, perk buy, stat spend, tab persistence across a redraw, the two-handed conflict, ill-fitting armour, a running condition and the empty state, with no console errors, and the user's save backed up and restored byte-identical twice. ⛔ **AND THE PARALLEL-SESSION RULE COLLECTED ITS OWN LESSON, AT THE COST OF THE LAST TWO LINES OF THE JOB.** The lock was taken legitimately (expired, 8.2h) and taken BACK by the other session mid-build; the two remaining CSS lines (`#iBody{height:232px}`, the fold rule that keeps a maximally-marked body from scrolling 65px, and a `.pv` colour) are unapplied, and that session then committed this work itself as *"in progress, carried from the parallel session"*. ⚑ **An expired lock is not a free desk when the other session is merely asleep at it: `status` says 8.2h, it does not say whether anybody is coming back.** ⚠ **A second, real bug fell out of the same minute and it is fixed here:** `claim.ps1`'s pre-commit `verify` read the six-digit CSS colour on a new panel as a claim on another session's entry number, because `(?!\d)` is satisfied by the letter that follows the digits. `Get-UsedNumbers` was hardened against precisely this trap when it once issued a stylesheet value as a backlog number; **the hardening never reached the verify side, so the same trap was still shipping in the other reader.** Six and eight digit colours are stripped before the entry scan now. **One fact, two readers, and only one of them was taught.** | ✅ |
| 8f.152 | **#131 + #124 SHIPPED - A RELOAD MID-BATTLE RE-OFFERS THE FIGHT, AND NEW GAME STARTS ONE.** *(User, 2026-08-13, first two items of a nine-item voice batch, built while he slept.)* ⛔ **#131 is a promise the save made and never kept.** Its own design note says a battle is regenerated rather than restored and "the existing re-entry path (click the node you are standing on) starts the fight fresh" - and that path was dead code, because `travel()`'s arrive writes `G.visited[k]=true` and saves BEFORE the event fires, while `drawNodes`' re-entry click is gated on `!G.visited[k]`. So a player who reloaded mid-fight resumed standing on a node hovering "Done with. The road remembers, and so do you.", with the battle, its spoils, its XP and its scars gone. The fix reuses the mechanism that already worked twice rather than inventing a second: `openEvent` records the unresolved card as `G.openEv`, it is saved, and both resume paths replay it. Cleared at `wGo`, `wKit`, `toBeaten`, the withdraw screen, and `afterPromotion` **before** its save, or the won fight re-offers itself. It is deliberately NOT `queuedEv`, which means the opposite thing (what happens after this card). ⚑ **#124's lesson is that the reload was right and the intent was what got lost.** `const G` and `G.party=makeParty()` are module-level, so only a fresh page rebuilds a company; the button now writes `gt_boot_new` before reloading and `openMenu` takes it first thing. **`take()` deletes before it returns** - a throw between read and prologue would otherwise boot every future launch into a new run with no way back. | ✅ |
| 8f.153 | **#130 SHIPPED - ONE MENU BUTTON, ONE PLACE, AND Esc REACHES IT EVERYWHERE.** *(User: "on all screens made a consistent button menu in one place so person can reach it, and make it work on esc".)* The button lived on one screen by one line, and the reason written above that line was that a battle is regenerated rather than restored, so walking out would discard it. **8f.152 removed the reason**, which is the whole shape of this row: a fix in one entry unlocked a refusal in another. `MENU_ON` is world · battle · inv · camp · sim, measured as appearing on exactly those five, on none of the eight flow screens, and at an identical (100, 710) box on all of them - "one place" has to mean the coordinate, not just the existence. Leaving a real fight asks and names the cost; leaving a practice fight calls the new `simRestore()`, extracted from `simResult` because that used to be the only way out and an abandoned practice fight would otherwise leave the borrowed roster in `G`. ⚑ **Esc was reaching one screen because the handler returned early unless the battle was up, and then the busy / tutLock / not-your-turn guards swallowed it again** - the one place it worked was also the place it most often did nothing. Layered now, first match wins: the confirm modal takes its **last** door (every `ask()` in this file is written with the safe answer last, so Esc can never commit the dangerous one, and nothing on the keyboard could dismiss that modal at all before), then a live battle selection, then the menu button's own click path, then the screen's own Back. | ✅ |
| 8f.154 | **#125 + #132 SHIPPED - THE QUESTIONNAIRE RIDES THE LAST PAGE, AND THE STATS GET THEIR REAL NAMES.** *(User: "after age successful and unsuccessful run, offer to send this feedback and questionnaires there, rather than a independent button and then many people don't use it" · "rename the strains agility intellect moral things".)* Both endings built exactly one button, to the wagon, and **nothing at the end of a run mentioned the questionnaire**: finishing a run changed a sub-caption on a menu row. Both cards carry "Say how it went" now, in the `sendit` highlight the notes screen already used for this, with the wagon keeping the left seat because it is the progression. ⚑ **#132's rename fixed a collision nobody had filed:** the roster sheet printed **NERVE twice, thirty lines apart, off two different scales** - the `mor` stat's bands and the battle ladder's rungs. MORALE is the capacity, NERVE is what is left of it. Seven bands became nine **with every existing threshold keeping its exact meaning** (one added at 17, the old catch-all bottom bucket split at 3), so no character in any save moves band except one under 5, and the new room is headroom for the artifacts idea he floated. ⚠ `tell()` reads the table with `.find(v>=threshold)`, so descending order is load-bearing and is asserted in the verification. | ✅ |
| 8f.155 | **#126 + #127 + #128 SHIPPED - THE ARCHER FIGHT.** *(User: "make them a bit more aggressive so they're coming and they're shooting at you, and then they kind of run away if you try to approach" · "two spear reds and their archers behind them, and the spears can use spear wall before their archers".)* Three changes to one fight, in both brains, paying the gate in **measurement** rather than a picture, per #81's rule. ⛔ **#127's finding is that the literal request had shipped nine days earlier and nothing used it:** the bow is `range:5` with -18 at the limit and -8 one hex in, from the first commit - but `band:3` and `Math.min(rng-1,3)` pinned every shooter at 3 hexes whatever it carried, so no body making its own decision had ever stood on hexes 4 or 5. **And 3 is inside a man's reach** (first-move 4 + reach 1 = 5). `bandFor` is `range-1` floored at 2, so range-4 chaff is unchanged and only long weapons move. A bigger `range` was rejected with arithmetic: at 6 a bow clears a baseline human and still loses to a ratkin, a spear, a dog, any captain and the WARDEN. **#126** unfroze the hold (a shooter with nothing in range returned before the movement block and stood still all battle) and unbroke the kite (`nearD<=2 && !engaged` reacted after the danger arrived and switched off at the moment it was for); `fallBack` now pays the parting swing, because `walkTo` is not `clickHex` and without it the kite would be a free disengage better than the DISENGAGE act. **#128**: the formation he asked for already existed and its SPEAR WALL was dead data in three independent ways. ⚑ **And switching it on raised zero walls, for this file's signature trap: `wallLive()` answers "is this body's wall up AND working", not "would one work if raised"** - it opens `if(!u.spearwall)return false`, so `!u.spearwall && wallLive(u)` can never be true. Both brains had it. **The second finding is bigger:** a screen carries no ranged damage, so the hold gate never applied to it and the scorer sent it charging out in front of the archers it exists to stand in front of. Measured over six sling-line runs: walls raised **0 → 12**, bodies caught stepping into one **0 → 16**, screens holding the line **0 → 12**, and hex 4 became the modal firing distance (2:44 · 3:47 · 4:54 · 5:10). Full eight-fight regression twice, guard ok on every one. | ✅ |
| 8f.162 | **#134 - THE JOKE LEAVES THE OPENING AND IS PARKED, PLUS TWO SMALL FIXES.** *(User, 2026-08-13, out for the day: "make a joke option put from the opning to our ideas/things for future set" and "check how it feels and backlog and make smaller not creatical fixes".)* **THE JOKE, PRESERVED IN FULL SO IT CAN COME BACK AS A PASTE.** The contract card's third reply was **"Agree with him. Cheerfully."** *(caption: "a joke, at a man with paid swords · it goes one of two ways")*, resolved on `Math.random()<0.5`. Both outcomes open the same way: *"Scum, my lord, and cheap with it. You will not find better value in this county, and you have clearly been looking."* **It lands** *(120 crowns, morale +6, scene `joke_hit`, verdict THE JOKE LANDED)*: "One breath of silence. Then the man in the coat laughs: genuinely, unpleasantly, the laugh of somebody who has not been surprised by another person in years and has missed it more than he knew. He counts out a hundred and twenty instead of eighty, tells his swords that this one can read, and leaves without shutting the door. Marrow spends the rest of the evening explaining that he would have said it first." **It does not** *(50 crowns, morale +2, scene `joke_miss`, verdict THE JOKE DID NOT LAND, Captain takes 16% of hitpoints)*: "It is a good line. The room enjoys it. The man in the coat does not react at all, which is when you understand that he was never the problem, and the nearer of his two swords puts a cudgel into your ribs with the unhurried competence of a man paid by the week. The advance is still fifty crowns. You will simply be carrying it downhill for a while." ⛔ **Why this one of the three:** it put a **coin flip on the player's first real decision**. The remaining two are the pillar in one card - 40 crowns and a beating for pride, 80 and no pride for the purse - and a fifty-fifty beside them is a lottery ticket, not a door. Three doors to two is also the clarity pass's own instruction. ⚑ **Nothing else was torn out:** `PRO_ART.joke_hit` stays mapped to EV00C because `.claude/rules/static-event-art.md` requires that mapping preserved exactly and the painting is good, and the `verdict` machinery stays with it. Dormant, not dead. Parked in the backlog as #134 with the note that **what it needs to return is a reason the player can read** - a stat, a trait, a personality - so the joke is a judgement rather than a dice roll. **TWO SMALL FIXES ALONGSIDE.** *(1)* The inspect panel and the attack readout printed the raw morale pool and `STATES[...].n`, which is the ENGINE's vocabulary: a hovered enemy said **"47 · WAVERING"** while its own plaque said **"😐 Ok"**, and three of the five state names are called something else on the ladder. Both say what the plaque says now, and both rows are labelled **NERVE**, because #132 gave the word MORALE to the stat (the capacity) and this is what is left of it. Last surviving breach of "show a state, hide the number" in the nerve system. *(2)* **`shotState` and `hitBreakdown` each hand-wrote `dd>=act.range` and `dd>=act.range-1`** and agreed only because both were typed the same way - one rule, two readers, and the day they drift the board paints a hex CLEAR over a shot quietly paying 18. One `SHOTBAND()` now. ⚑ **#127 made those bands load-bearing days earlier**, by deriving a shooter's preferred hex from them, so this was a fuse with a shorter wick than it looked. Verified identical to the rule it replaced across ranges 3/4/5/7 at every distance 0-8, LINT 0, eight-fight regression clean. | ✅ |
| 8f.163 | **#135 - EVERY BOW AND SLING REACHES 5, AND MEASURING IT FOUND THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT WAS EXPECTED.** *(User, 2026-08-13: "yes, good, do it and publish", answering #127's parked question.)* Four physical shooters went 4 → 5: the snare slinger, the brigand poacher, the clash ratsling and the sling-line slinger. The Marked, the circle and the sling-master were already 5, so **there is no range-4 bow or sling left in the game**. Arcane range (warp-light, THE WORD, bloom-spit, EMBER) and the ogre's thrown rock are untouched - they are not bows. ⛔ **THE FINDING, AND IT IS COUNTER-INTUITIVE: GIVING THE ENEMY MORE RANGE MADE THE FIGHTS EASIER, NOT HARDER.** Ten runs each side. brigand **6.5 → 5.2 rounds**, player bodies down **2.1 → 0.9**, and **two losses in ten became none**; slingline **7.2 → 6.4** and 2 wins/8 mercy became 10 mercy; clash flat (5.0 → 4.8); snare **8.3 → 9.2** rounds with its 7-in-10 loss rate unchanged. **The mechanism is #127's own band and it is obvious in hindsight:** `bandFor` is `range-1`, so a shooter that used to want hex 3 now wants hex 4 - **further from the player, and paying the long-shot −8 it did not pay before**. Average enemy firing distance went 2.57 → 3.50 on brigand and 3.16 → 3.44 on slingline, with hex 5 heavily used for the first time (13 of 42 brigand shots, 64 of 315 sling-line ones). ⚑ **So "range" on this board is not a power stat, it is a POSITIONING stat**: it buys standoff and it is paid for in accuracy, and a body that stands further off is also further from ever joining the melee. Worth remembering the next time a shooter is buffed by giving it reach. ⚠ n=10 per fight: the docs' own warning is that small n cannot separate 0% from 20%, so the DIRECTION is trustworthy (the mechanism explains it) and the magnitudes are not. Nothing was compensated: the user asked for range 5, not for a difficulty change, and silently rebalancing behind a request is what invariant 10 forbids. ⚠ **The #127 comment that said "a range-4 sling still wants 3, so the chaff behaves exactly as before" was true when written and is false now**; it was deleted rather than left standing. Eight-fight regression clean, LINT 0. ⚑ **A harness trap worth recording:** `eval(harness)` inside a function scope makes `regress` close over THAT scope, so a local `const FIGHTS` in the same function silently shrank the regression from eight fights to four. Use indirect `(0,eval)` to load it at global scope. | ✅ |
| 8f.164 | **#136 THE COMPANY SHEET, ROUND TWO: ELEVEN POINTS, AND THE ONE THAT WAS ALREADY DONE.** *(User, 2026-08-13, his second pass over #133.)* All eleven in the running build at 678px with no scroll. **1** the middle band is smaller (body zone 232 -> 196, doll box 400 -> 362), and it is the doll DISPLAYED smaller: `DOLL_DW/DOLL_DH` scale the canvas and the anchors are scaled on the way out, so the anatomy table is untouched and no leader drifts off the shoulder it names. **2 is the big one: the kit is WORN, not listed beside.** #133 drew a bare body and hung four labelled boxes round it, which is half a paper doll - the boxes said what was equipped and the picture did not, so a doll wearing plate looked like a doll wearing nothing. ⛔ **Both new layers are classified off what a thing IS or DOES, never off its key**: armour off the armour VALUE (leather / mail / plate, with shoulder caps over 25 and a helm over 70), exactly as the battle's three weights are, and the weapon off `range` / `reach` / `hands` (bow arc, spear shaft, two-handed head, blade), so a future `GEAR{}` entry arrives wearing the right silhouette without being added to a list here. **3** the three pools stack in a 236px column in the plaque's own shape and the attributes take the freed width - ⚑ **measured before moving: the row was never TALL, it was 704px WIDE for three readouts of fifteen characters, which is what "taking too much space" was seeing.** **4** the hover hint deleted. **5** the tile is the painted bust with the field token under it. **6** one popover for the whole sheet and it is the battle's: gear slots and body marks use it too, which took a scar's full description out of the column. **7** ACTIONS on the sheet through the plaque's own `apPips` - it had existed only in battle. *(⚠ CORRECTED IN 8f.168: this row first claimed "so a three-action Captain reads as three". He does not have three. `unitFrom` gives every roster body `actionsMax:2`; the `t.captain?3:2` line is in the statblock builder for FOES. The readout is honest and currently constant, and it is on the sheet because he asked for it against later builds - "for later builds and strnger characters it could be mor then 2" - not because anybody has three today.)* **8** the stash tab wears the road bar's drawn sack (#104 stopped using the glyph because the platform painted it pink). **9** ⚑ **ALREADY SHIPPED, AND VERIFIED RATHER THAN REDONE**: another session's #134 had renamed the four axes to STRENGTH / AGILITY / INTELLECT / MORALE hours earlier, so his note was written against a build one commit old. **Third time a point in a report has arrived already-fixed (#114 point 1 was the first); the check costs one grep and the redo costs an argument with yourself later.** **10** the story is an accordion and its head carries the kill heads and the count, because a row of heads is the shortest true summary of a life and a shut accordion has to be worth clicking - ⚠ off `killsBy`, never `kills`. **11** a waiting perk point now says so four times (gold pulsing tab reading SPEND IT, a badge, a banner that jumps to the tab, and a ★ on the roster rail) - ⚑ **and the rail one is the point: it is the only signal visible WITHOUT opening that person's sheet, which was the actual failure mode.** Also applied #133's outstanding fold rule, so the seven-mark body scrolls its ledger instead of the screen. Verified across three races with three kits, wires landing on their anchors, equip, strip, perk buy, stat spend, tab persistence, the accordion, LINT 0 findings, no console errors, save restored byte-identical. ⚠ **Two self-inflicted traps worth the ink: a 30s tool timeout killed a driver script MID-RENDER and the half-built pane read as four missing features** (re-run before believing a measurement taken through a timeout), **and ripgrep's own escaping showed `/*` as `\*`, which read exactly like the stray-comment bug this file has shipped before** - the raw bytes said otherwise. **Distrust the tool's rendering before rewriting the file.** | ✅ |
| 8f.167 | **#139 - BRANCH PER SESSION. THE LOCK WAS A QUEUE, AND A WORKTREE IS ISOLATION.** *(User, 2026-08-13: "изменения про паралельную работу были ужасны... щас локает и это очень долго для правок" - make it possible to work on the file in several branches at once and merge the results.)* `claim.ps1 lock` made two sessions take turns on `prototype/grimtoll_slice.html`, which was the correct answer to **one working tree, one branch** and became unbearable the moment the second session's edit was small: it waited out the first session's whole feature to change six lines of CSS. **`tools/branch.ps1 new <name>` now makes a branch AND a `git worktree` desk** at `%USERPROFILE%\grimtoll-desks\<name>` (outside Google Drive on purpose: a worktree is a full checkout and pointing Drive at three more copies of a 5.6 MB prototype and a 10 MB `index.html` is both wasteful and a live corruption risk while git is writing). Two sessions edit the same file at the same second, and `tools/merge.ps1` brings it back. ⚑ **THE LOAD-BEARING PART IS NOT THE WORKTREE, IT IS FOUR LINES POINTING THE CLAIM STORE AT `--git-common-dir`.** Git cannot merge a counter: `.grimtoll\` followed `$Root`, so every desk would have counted from its own empty store and issued `#140` three times **while each session watched the tool protect it**. Everything else here would still have looked like it worked. **When you distribute a thing, find the counter first.** ⛔ **NOTHING GENERATED IS EVER MERGED, IT IS REBUILT AFTER THE MERGE:** `index.html` and `art/embed/art_data.js` take `merge=ours` (a conflict inside 10 MB of base64 is unresolvable by hand and the resolution was always "generate it again", so `merge.ps1` ends by naming what is now stale), the three append-only logs take `merge=union` (both desks add a row at the top and would otherwise conflict *every single time*), and the prototype merges as ordinary lines, where a conflict is **correct**: somebody has to decide. ⚠ `merge=ours` is not built in and does nothing at all until `git config merge.ours.driver true`; `branch.ps1 setup` does it and `merge.ps1` re-checks. **The lock is NOT deleted** - the main desk is still shared, so the hook and `gate` still fire there and are off in a desk, proven by running the hook both places. `deploy.ps1` now refuses anywhere but the main desk on `main`, because `git add -A` + `git push HEAD` from a desk publishes half a feature under a branch name. ⛔ **The merge guard is deliberately NOT "main must be clean"**: this main desk is shared and somebody is nearly always mid-edit on something unrelated, so a blanket rule would refuse every merge and hand the queue straight back. It refuses only on an uncommitted change to a file the merge would **overwrite**, and never stashes, because the stash it took would be the other session's. ⚠ **Two PowerShell traps, both found by the thing hanging or lying rather than failing.** *(1)* A helper function named `Git` whose body runs `& git` **calls itself**: a function shadows an external command of the same name, and the script hangs in recursion. *(2)* A `[Parameter()]` attribute makes a function ADVANCED, which silently grants `-Debug`, so `Gitx branch -d work/x` bound `-d` as a prefix of `-Debug`, ate it, ran `git branch work/x`, and **created the branch it was told to delete** while the desk removal beside it succeeded and nothing looked wrong. Simple functions take `$args` and bind nothing. **Also fixed, found live while testing:** `claim.ps1 lock` accepted **any string as a path**, and a session had run `lock "the seven-item pack and the opening chain"`, passing its title where the path goes - the lock file was written, `status` listed it, and the prototype was **not protected at all**, because the hook matches a file leaf. `lock` now refuses a target that is not a file in the repo. Round trip verified end to end: desk created, committed on its branch, merged into a main that was dirty in 9 unrelated files and left every one of them alone, branch deleted, `git check-attr` confirming all five merge routes. Full rules: [`PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md`](PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md), rewritten. | ✅ |
| 8f.165 | **#137 - FIVE OF A SEVEN-ITEM PACK: THE RECEIPT, THE STEP, THE RED DOOR, THE DOUBLED LESSON, THE TOKEN.** *(User, 2026-08-13, two screenshots with a red arrow drawn at the loot line.)* **(1)** The aftermath's haul was `haul.c`, a string each LOOT row hand-writes, at 11.5px mono - smaller than the crew cards above it and the accordion below it, on the one line that answers what the fight paid. Now one icon chip per resource at **20px**, built off **`haul.fx`**, the object `takeLoot()` actually pays out. ⛔ **Off `fx` and never off the string, because that is what makes a chip unable to disagree with the payment** - not hypothetical: six rows of these tables were once caught printing a price the game did not charge. Glyphs are `RES_ICON`, unchanged since #103, and a row paying a thing AND a number (the snare's bells) can now show both, which the old single-branch line could not. **(2)** DISENGAGE banked its extra hex on `disNext` and cashed it at the START OF THE NEXT TURN - one turn after it was needed, so you paid an action to break off and had less movement to run with. The hex lands on **this** turn's budget now, and `cool:2` is the whole lockout with no new state: `spend` writes it, `beginTurn` decrements, `onCool` refuses, the card paints READY IN N TURNS. ⚑ **The visibility was free: `moveBudget` is the one door every mover asks, so the lit hexes, the MOVE card's hex count, both AI brains and AUTO had it before the next line ran.** `disNext` deleted rather than left set; `TUTSTEPS.circled` corrected in the same commit or the brawl teaches a promise the build no longer keeps. **(3)** Fight doors go red from **one gate in the renderer** instead of a `danger:true` per row - five had never been given one, including *both* of Blood on the Road's wade-in doors while its WATCH IT HAPPEN door had it. `.choice.danger` also got a real red ground and title; a 1px `#6b2a24` border is one you have to already be hunting for. **(4)** Four `learn`-tier Captain lessons DELETED with their call sites - `learn_arm`, `learn_break`, `learn_step`, `learn_zone` - because #108's spotlight teaches all four earlier and with an arrow on the thing (`TUTSTEPS.armour` / `.broken`+`.nerve` / `.circled` ×2). ⚠ **`learn_twice` looks like a duplicate of `TUTSTEPS.act` and is not**: `act` says "two actions a turn" and stops, the lesson teaches the diminishing return on the second, which nothing else says anywhere. ⛔ **The rule left behind: CAPLINES and TUTSTEPS are two teachers with one syllabus, so read the other before adding to either** - four duplicates accumulated in three days without anybody deciding to write one. **(5)** The map token's caption was the literal string THE COMPANY in the markup; it wears `G.coName` now, the duplicate `#wName` map-foot signature goes with it, and an old save falls back to THE COMPANY. ⛔ **THE ENTRY'S REAL FINDING IS A CORRECTION TO ITS OWN POINT 3, caught by measuring instead of reading:** grepping the tables said 5 unmarked fight doors; enumerating `battle:` in the RUNNING build returns **12**, and three break the rule - `snare` has two doors and both are fights (same fight, the choice is whether they are ready or you strike first), `armour` and `packev` have one door each. **Red on every door of a card is furniture, not a signal.** Corrected in 8f.166. ⚑ **A table you can enumerate at runtime should be enumerated at runtime.** ⚠ Process: shipped from the MAIN desk without the lock, because `claim.ps1 lock` was handed its title where the path goes - the trap 8f.167 fixed in the same hour. Recovered by filtering the diff to this session's own hunks and leaving the other session's 228 uncommitted lines untouched in the tree. | ✅ |
| 8f.166 | **#138 - THE OPENING'S FOUR POST-BRAWL CARDS BECOME ONE, AND THE LAST DECISION IN IT IS DELETED.** *(User, 2026-08-13: "combine this and next screen (after battle + consiquence). | 8f.169 | **#141 - THE COIN IN THE ROAD, AND AN ITEM THAT REFUSES TO ITEMISE ITSELF.** *(User, 2026-08-13: "after tavern fight - next event instead of faling star (it could be a random event somwhere) - add event - you found an unusial coin. Everything the star needs travelled with it, because `openEvent` casts its {TELLER} off the EVENT key and `MAP_SIGHT` is keyed the same way; its body opened *"A day east of Grausen"*, true of exactly one node and a lie on the other seven, and now names no place. ⚑ **`hush` IS THE WHOLE MECHANISM AND IT IS ONE WORD ON ONE GEAR ROW.** Two renderers write a piece of kit out - `gearLine` for the inventory and `tipFor` for the hover - and both build their numbers by walking the same fields the engine reads, so silencing those two silences the item everywhere without touching what it does. ⛔ **The tempting wrong version is to leave the fields off the row and apply the bonus somewhere else, which is a second source of truth for a stat, the shape this file has paid for half a dozen times.** The fields stay canonical; the renderer keeps its mouth shut. ⚠ **AND WIRING IT FOUND THAT HALF OF `dodge` HAD NEVER BEEN WIRED AT ALL**: `unitFrom` read `hit` off a trinket (since the ground-glass eyes) and read `dodge` off the OFF HAND only, so a trinket carrying dodge was a field `GEAR{}` accepted, both renderers printed, and nothing on the board applied. **Two slots, one vocabulary, one of them never taught the second word** - the file's own recurring shape, and it would have shipped the user's item at half strength. **Measured on the plaque the player actually reads: CHANCE TO HIT 61% → 68%, DODGE 15% → 19%.** ⚑ **The dodge one moves by four, not seven, and that is correct**: `dodgeOf` ends in `softDodge()`, a cap applied LAST to the sum that no contributor opts out of, a shield's +9 included. Raw dodge went 17 → 24. **The card is two doors and both give you the coin**, which is what makes it a decision instead of a pickup: the rule (into the wagon, −4 mood, you pick the wearer, the inventory opens) or the finder (they wear it tonight, +8 mood, `remember()` writes it into their history, you get no say). ⚠ **An early draft had a third door that left it in the mud, and it was cut**: a door that deletes the only reason the card exists is not a decision, it is a trapdoor. ⚠ **The Wheel-Rut lost its map painting by changing its event key** - `star` is in `MAP_SIGHT` and `coin` is not, so the node dropped from `NAME_DY_SIGHT` 54 to `NAME_DY` 21 with nobody touching a coordinate. **The world-map rule warns that art moves a plate; it does not spell out that art can be LOST as well as gained.** The name is deliberately shorter than the star's (13 chars against 16) so the plate can only narrow, and all three counters were re-run. **Verified in the running build:** LINT 0 findings, `spacingViolations()` 0, `labelViolations()` 0, no `MAP_SIGHT` key without art, both doors driven end to end, eight-fight regression all ok (clash 4rd · brigand 6rd · pack 5rd · slingline 10rd · steading 11rd · snare 10rd · mother 8rd · armour 5rd). | ✅ |

Measured at all 49 generated names: 683/683, over 0. `contractSettle` survives untouched as the parked joke door's landing site. **THE CHIPS LEARNED WHICH GLYPH MEANS WHAT** *("When morale boosts - it is not hear... Maybe face. Choice sub-lines **10.5 → 12.5px**. **BLOOD ON THE ROAD**: labels 17/16/23 words → 9/9/10 with every tactical figure kept, and *"Do nothing. Watch it happen."* becomes **"Wait. Then finish the winners."** - the door the code always built, since watching had always delivered the same fight and the prose had to talk you into it afterwards. It now pays **+55 crowns +5 salvage +2 provisions** in `clashAfter`, chips built beside the pay() calls, `already`-gated. ⛔ **The ogres are NOT wounded to match the story**: that branch is already the shortest fight in the act, and making the cruel door the safest AND the richest is the one shape the pillar forbids. **THE FORK CARD IS ONE LINE A ROAD** *("4 days full - 1-2 figths - trouble")*: it was showing THE LEG on the button and THE BRANCH underneath, so the two numbers on screen were never comparable. Days-to-join · fights · the word, no sub-label. ⚑ **Fights are a counted range, not an assumed one**: the first draft added +1 for every unvisited event slot, and the running build says **one of the eighteen floating cards has a battle door**, so the slot's dealt card is asked instead. `NOTHING HEARD` → **`PEACEFUL`**, and the `nowhere to stop` clause that used to correct it moved to the row's hover - #58's warning was about the pass out of the Dead Company and that road was deleted in the 2026-08-01 reshape. **THE ROAD BAR**: the contract plate says where, what, what is carried and what is still owed, with its destination read from the new `QUEST_GOAL` rather than typed; **the Snare wears BB's trophy** (the same `TROPHY_SVG` the plate wears, absolutely positioned inside `.glyph` so it touches neither the layout column nor the plate arithmetic); the heart carries **the company average as a percentage** with the "somebody is nearly down" alarm split out as its own red pip, because #106's correct fix had made one glyph answer two questions with no way to tell which had fired; and the two doors wear **★ unspent levels** and **! better kit**, both derived every tick from `perkPoints`/`statPoints` and `gearDelta` rather than from a new flag, where better means *wins on an axis and loses on none* so a trade does not make the mark furniture. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots**: LINT 0 · no em dash in EVENTS · `spacingViolations` 0 · `labelViolations` 0 · unmapped sights 0 · 34 cards all inside their #143 bands (3,668 words) · both prologue cards over=0 · clash re-entry pays nothing twice · no stray separators across every EVENTS and CAMPS label. | ✅ |

which is what lets the cache's deliberately vague "by whatever was agreed" stay vague on the door and resolve to ◉+90 only after taking it, and lets the toll purse chip print the lootMul'd +38 the purse actually paid. ⚑ **And the pillar found its own boundary**: a multi-door card still shows NOTHING before the pick (the score stays hidden where there is a score), but **a ONE-door card is a pickup and wears its loot on the card itself** - one live non-battle door means no decision to protect, so the coin, the cache and the chapel's post-#123 single-salvage state all show their chips up front, and a one-door card whose after is empty closes straight to the road (`prose` trimmed of the leading newline the empty-after path exposed). **THE COIN:** #141's two-door decision is cut to his sketch exactly - 259 words of prose to 45, one door, "Keep walking.", the finder keeps it (+8 morale, `coinKeep`, the strip says whose neck it rides), and 8f.169's wagon door survives only in that row's own record. **Verified in the running build, DOM counters not screenshots, because the pane would not composite frames**: LINT 0 findings · zero em dashes across every rewritten string · coin card = 1 chip + 1 strip + 1 button and one click lands morale 0→8 with the trinket on the finder · toll outcome chips ◉+38/♥−2 against crowns 107→145 · deadco = ♥−7 + two strips + the ⛨ inventory gate · camp argument = ◐−1/♥+8 · vignette = ◉+3 · cache card chips ▤+6/◈+1 growing ◉+90 at the outcome · slingline's battle door still starts its fight. | 8f.173 | **#145 - THE BRAWL'S SECOND WAVE SPLITS, AND THE ROOM GETS SOMEBODY YOU CANNOT HIT.** *(User, 2026-08-13, away for the day: "For first intro battle: Ogr joins your fight one more turn before main team. One more thug joins opponents same turn. To this one more thug who is coming add - Very nimble - huge dodge. And when you get 2 miss on him - write info messege - 'the best way to hit enemy with big doge - circle or hit from the back'".)* Wave two used to bring the three future crew AND the barman up together with no new enemy, which is a rescue, and **a rescue is not a lesson**: the room went from "you are losing" to "you have five friends" between two clicks, and the two things the wave carried (an ogre is a different kind of body; a dodge is beaten by an angle) had no round of their own to be noticed in. Split one round apart: the barman comes over his bar and **WEFT** comes in off the street on the same turn, the crew stands up the round after. ⛔ **THE OGRE MOVED FORWARD, THE CREW DID NOT MOVE BACK, and the first cut got that backwards.** Reading the ask as "the crew arrives later" put wave two at +3 and the crew at +4, which is one more round of the Captain alone against four men - so the barman lands at **+2** and the crew keeps its original +3. **The fight is the same length it was and the teaching round is bought from the watchers' warning, not from the Captain.** ⚑ **THE ARITHMETIC IS THE LESSON.** Weft is dodge 20 plus the new `nimble` personality's +16, which the soft cap lands at **26**: against the Captain's 68 that is **42% at his face, 57% at his flank, 72% at his back**, so the front is a coin the player will lose twice in a row and the angle is the difference between two halves of a fight. Thin everywhere else on purpose (31 hp, 2 armour) - a puzzle, not a threat. **TOO QUICK TO HIT** is queued by `strike()` on the SECOND miss, counted **on the defender** (the question is "have I failed to touch THIS one twice", not "has this body of mine missed twice", which would fire on two different men and teach nothing about dodge), and written as the general rule rather than against a name: anything carrying the personality arms it, `B.tut` scopes it to the fight with the spotlight layer. It names **both** doors the engine has - the arc AND the `surrounded` row, because "circle him" means +flankPow to everybody swinging - rather than being BACKSTAB again one card later. BACKSTAB and the #111 spare step moved onto the barman's wave, because a spare step is only a gift when there is one obvious thing to spend it on. `foeOnly` on the trait keeps it out of the roster roll: +16 dodge with nothing paid for it is instantly the best line in a register whose standing rule is *"if you can name the best one, they are traits again"*. **Measured, n=50 a side: the brawl is the same fight it was** - the Captain finishes at 45% and 45%, runs under 25% two and three, runs under 15% one and one, worst 13% and 12%. ⛔ **AND TWO NERFS WERE DRAFTED AGAINST A NUMBER THAT WAS NOT THERE**: two batches of sixteen read "worst finish 29% → 10%" and produced a knife nerf and a valve change before n=50 said the two builds were identical. ⚑ **A MINIMUM OVER n IS NOT A MEASUREMENT, IT IS THE UNLUCKIEST SAMPLE, and it gets worse every time you look. Measure a tail as the FRACTION of runs under a threshold.** The knife is back at [6,11]; the rescue valve stays at 0.45 as an explicit judgement (a rescue that waits for a third of the Captain arrives after the damage rather than instead of it). Picture: `shots/145_second_wave.html`. LINT 0. | ✅ |
| 8f.174 | **#146 - THE AIMING PASS: EVERY ROW THAT IS A SUM CARRIES ITS WORKING, AND SIZE IS A BONUS TO HIT.** *(User, 2026-08-13: "For chances to hit and dodge show 'what is others' (for all bagtles)" and "increse chances to hit for all units for 10% and for enemy units for 5%. Also update dodge modifiers for different tirget size, so it is easier to hit bigger guys (we will do mor grounded render later)".)* **THE EASE KNOBS.** `HIT_EASE {you:10, foe:5}` - ten points on the roster, five on the other side, so the net is +5 in the player's favour and a fight gets faster for BOTH of them (the complaint behind this is whiffing, and a pass that only speeds up your own turns leaves the enemy's turns exactly as long). ⛔ **THEY GO WHERE `mskill` IS SET, NOT INTO hitBreakdown**: the hover card prints `mskill` and both AI brains read it, so a bonus living only inside the preview would be this file's recurring bug again - one fact, two readers, one of them untaught. Two doors, `unitFrom` and `build`, and `besideYou()` stamps a statblock body up to the roster figure at the moment its side is flipped (the clash allies, the barman), because build() cannot know which side it is making. Points and not a percentage: a scaled chance moves the good shooters and leaves the bad ones exactly as bad, which is the opposite of the ask. **THE WORKING.** `dodgeOf` takes an optional collector array - pass one and it is filled, pass nothing and not one string is built, which is what keeps it out of the innermost loop of both brains (`losState` in there once took a five-round clash to 23 seconds) - and `hitBreakdown` returns `oth`, the contributors that actually land in `other`, built by a `po()` that writes to both lists so a new contributor cannot be added to one and forgotten in the other. A `.rw` caption sits under the row it explains, collapses when empty, and appears from the SECOND contributor on, because "their footwork +16" under a row reading −16 restates the number and teaches nothing. ⛔ **AND THE SIZE RULE IS A BONUS TO HIT, NOT A DODGE MODIFIER, WHICH IS A MEASURED REFUSAL OF THE LITERAL REQUEST.** The literal version was built first - the ogre's −5 moved out of RACEDODGE onto `sizeOf()` as {1:0, 2:−7, 3:−14} - and then measured across every statblock in all eight fights, where it did **almost nothing**: an ogre's base dodge is 7 to 10, its armour band takes 5 or 10 off, and `Math.max(0,…)` eats the rest, so Bruht, the steading elder and both snare ogres read **0 with the rule and 0 without it**, and the Fen-Mother reads 3 either way because her HEAVY band has already taken ten of her thirteen. **Subtracting from a number that is already zero is a rule that exists and never fires.** So the fact is stated on the side with room for it: `SIZEHIT {2:+6, 3:+12}` lands in `other` as a **named line** - "a big body +6" - which means the new caption shows it instead of it being a silent subtraction nobody could find. `noArc` deliberately does not cancel it: this is the size of the target, not a flanking bonus, and a thing with no wrong side is still a thing you can see across a room. **Measured, n=14 a side unless said:** snare 8.3rd/4.3 down/8 losses → 8.2/4.1/6 · steading 9.1/6.0/14 → 9.2/5.5/11 · clash unchanged · mother n=12 11.8rd/1.1 down → 10.3/1.1, still 12/12 wins. ✅ **AND IT CASHED #89's THIRD OPEN MEASUREMENT ON THE WAY, the hit-rate distribution**, probed at `hitBreakdown` inside `runFight` over ~1,200 swings a side across all eight fights. **Before: mean 60.3% · yours 58.7% · theirs 61.8% · and 30.5% of every swing in the game was a coin flip or worse (0-9% 0.3 · 10-19% 0.6 · 20-29% 1.7 · 30-39% 9.5 · 40-49% 18.4 · 50-59% 21.3 · 60-69% 19.4 · 70-79% 12.4 · 80-89% 7.8 · 90-99% 8.6). After: mean 65.6% · yours 66.3% · theirs 65.0% · 19.4% under fifty**, with the mass moving out of the 30-49 band and into 60-89. ⚑ **AND THE MEASUREMENT FOUND SOMETHING NOBODY HAD ASKED ABOUT: BEFORE THIS PASS THE ENEMY WAS THE BETTER-AIMING SIDE, by three points, in every fight in the act.** It is in no statblock - it falls out of the penalties only the player's side pays (shooting in a scrum, their own nerve, cautious) against the flanking bonus a side with more bodies collects. **THE FIGURE IN A STATBLOCK IS NOT THE FIGURE ON THE BOARD**, arriving on a quantity nobody had thought to point that rule at, and it means the ten-and-five split lands closer to parity than to an advantage: 66.3 against 65.0. Picture at 100%: `shots/146_aiming_card.html`. LINT 0. | ✅ |
| 8f.175 | **#147 - TWO FINISHED GROUNDS THE CAMPAIGN HAD NEVER SHOWN ANYBODY.** *(User, 2026-08-13: "you can check - which exshsting backgrounds you can add to current battles, so they feel better/ more uniq".)* The register holds six painted palettes and only **four** ever reached a campaign fight: `swamp` (#63) and `forest` (#56) were built, legibility-checked against the state tints, and reachable **only from the practice field**, while four of the ten fights shared teal. ⚑ **Both moves are corrections rather than taste, because each fight's own card already names the ground the board did not have.** THE BROKEN MEN opens *"Four of them out of the treeline, and two lean dogs that do not sit down"*, one of the four is a **poacher**, and it was fought on open wet fen with `trees:0`; the row's own comment already argued for it (*"men who break and run need somewhere to break TO"*). It takes the forest's pool of four. **n=16 a side: fen 7.6rd / 1.2 down / 0 lost · forest 6.3rd / 1.1 down / 0 lost** - the trees do not move the fight, they stop the card lying. THE RATKIN SNARE's second door is *"Through the marsh. Slower, but quiet"* against the same 13%-water fen as everywhere else; it takes the **swamp PALETTE** with `deep:0 trees:0 huge:0` and the fen's own thresholds and pool of five. ⛔ **The channel is deliberately refused and the measurement is why, n=16 a side: fen 7.7rd/4.6 down/10 losses · palette only 7.9/4.8/11 · FULL swamp with its channel 7.6/2.9/6.** The full swamp takes a third off the act finale, and the reason is written twice in this table already - a deep channel is a wall, dry islands are a line to form on, and **against ten bodies any wall is how six beat ten**. #63's own note says the palette is the half that carries *"water everywhere"* and the channel is the half that charges you; this row takes the first and refuses the second. **Left alone, with the reason attached:** `circle` is pinned to `broken` by measurement and 26 trunks are exactly the chokepoint that fight exists to deny · `mother` is the pinned boss whose re-tune is the user's ruling · `clash` draws from three for clarity · `plains` is a dial painted teal and buys no look · `outpost` is a layout with no fight written for it. Six of six palettes are now in the act. Picture: `shots/147_two_grounds.html`. LINT 0, eight fights clean. | ✅ |
| 8f.176 | **#148 - THE LANE JOINS THE ONE GATE, AND THE HARNESS STOPS WRITING TO THE PLAYER'S JOURNAL.** *(Both found by PLAYING the opening rather than reading it, which is README §5's whole argument.)* `mayAim` is this file's single statement of every refusal `clickHex` can give, written after an archer froze on one the brains did not share. **#36's BLOCKED lane was the one refusal that never made it in.** It had been copied into FOUR places instead - `clickHex` to print the line, `offerAtk` to light the hex, `aiTurn` and `autoStep` - and all four happened to agree, so nothing stalled. ⛔ **That is the bug, not the symptom: four copies of a rule is four chances for the fifth caller to be written without it.** Surfaced by a hand-driven shooter being told *"you would be shooting your own people"* while `mayAim` called the shot legal. BLOCKED moves into `mayAim`; the two brains keep only the OBSTRUCTED **preference** (a bad shot, not an illegal one, and preferences stay where preferences live); `offerAtk` asks `mayAim` instead of re-listing the size gate, the tree and the lane, so *"would the click swing"* and *"may a brain aim this"* are the same sentence and a refusal added later reaches the lit hex for free. **And `tools/harness.js` stopped growing the record**: `startBattle` writes a `battle` row unless `SIM.on`, so every regression run since the rig was written has been appending to the journal #57's analytics reads - **measured on the dev origin at 1458 harness rows out of 1500**. `JOURNAL.put` is stubbed for the duration and restored in the `finally`, beside the five outputs that have always been stubbed; it was missed because it is the only one that makes no noise. Stubbed rather than solved with `SIM.on=true`, which reroutes `checkEnd` and would change the fight being measured. Verified: a full eight-fight `regress()` now grows the journal by **zero** rows. | ✅ |
| 8f.177 | **#149 - AUTO RUNS ON A CHAINED setTimeout, AND THE REASON WAS ALREADY WRITTEN FIFTEEN THOUSAND LINES BELOW IT.** *(Found by playing: AUTO switched on, said so in the log, and then sat at round 7 with a live unit, `busy:false` and three unspent actions. `autoStep()` called by hand worked perfectly - nothing was wrong with the brain, nothing was calling it.)* `panTick` has carried this note since #66: *"A CHAINED setTimeout AND NOT setInterval… in the hidden preview pane an interval fires ZERO times (measured: 0 ticks in 1200ms at a 32ms period) while setTimeout is merely floored, and the harness's MessageChannel shim unclamps that one and not the other. **AN UNVERIFIABLE CONTROL IS A CONTROL NOBODY CHECKS.**"* ⛔ **And AUTO was still on `setInterval`, which is the one control that sentence is most about**: it is the tester's own tool, it is how a fight gets watched end to end, and `autoStep` carries a comment reading *"THE FREEZE LIVED HERE"*. `autoStop()` / `autoTick()` / `autoGo()` replace six hand-written `clearInterval(B.autoTimer)` call sites. ⚠ **`clearTimeout` and not `clearInterval`, and it is not pedantry**: the harness replaces `clearTimeout` and leaves `clearInterval` alone, so the wrong one would fail to stop a shimmed timer. ⚑ **The chain is strictly better outside the pane too** - it re-reads `paced()` every tick, so the PACE button no longer has to tear the timer down and rebuild it (that block is deleted), and a battle that ends stops rescheduling itself instead of firing forever into a guard. Verified by driving the whole opening in the pane: the brawl now runs to a result under AUTO, and a complete act-1 run was walked end to end - prologue, brawl, contract, naming, map, coin, clash, the north road, the Broken Men, the fire, the Ruined Steading, the Muster Field, the Black Fen, Coldharrow, the Stone Field, Bonepicker's Camp, the Dead Company, the Long Fire, the Warm Spring, the Hill Steading, the Door-Shrine, the Last Muster and the Snare, plus THE CIRCLE and The Cache as floating slots - **with no uncaught errors anywhere**. | ✅ |
| 8f.168 | **#140 THE COMPANY SHEET, ROUND THREE: TWELVE POINTS, AND THE BAG COMES OFF THE PARKING LOT.** *(User, 2026-08-13, a screenshot of the stats band with the ARMOUR STOPS cell crossed out.)* **1** the swing line reorganised: the damage is a cell of its own in the display face with the reach beside it, and the arithmetic on hover (the dice the weapon rolls · the arm's flat add · the build-and-nerve percentage · what it lands for). ⚑ **`swingOf()` hands back the act and the multiplier rather than the sheet deciding again which act counts as "the swing"** - a second copy of that rule is the exact shape that has bitten this file repeatedly. **2** ARMOUR STOPS left that row for the armour band's own hover, where `ARMOUR_BANDS[k].d` already had the sentence: ⚑ **it was never a property of the swing.** It is what THIS person's armour does to an incoming blow, and a defensive number sitting in a row of offensive ones is #102's "readout printing the wrong unit" in a milder coat. **3** the stash and perks pane 300 -> 450, his 50% exactly, paid for by the character column, which had room to give once **4** moved the marks from a column beside the figure to a wrapping row UNDER it - that is what let the figure take the full width and the slots become real 150px boxes. **5** the portrait and the field token lead the head row from the left. **6** the label is two words. **7** ⛔ **THE BAG, AND #133 PARKED IT ON GOOD GROUND**: no throwable and no mid-fight swap rule exist, so a bag was furniture. He has ruled it in, and the ruling is right for a reason worth keeping: **the slot is not the feature, it is the PROMISE of one**, and nothing in `GEAR{}` carries `slot:'bag'`, so it renders empty, says what it is for, and cannot be filled by accident. **8** ⚑ **the weapon is in the figure's RIGHT hand, which on a body facing the player is the viewer's LEFT** - the drawing, the slot column and the leader line all had to move together or the wire points across the body. **9** daylight between arm and torso, applied at the wrist only so the ogre keeps the width that distinguishes it. **10 · 11 · 12** "WHO THIS IS" deleted, the standing click instruction deleted from the bar, and the bar now carries **the company's own chosen name**. ⚠ **And the block shrinks before the screen does**: a ratkin in ill-fitting human armour carries an extra mark AND wraps the swing line, which put two of the three races 17px over, so the FIGURE is fixed and the LEDGER is the give (`flex:0 1 auto` on the body, `flex:1 1 auto` plus scroll on the marks). Verified at **678px with zero overflow on human, ratkin, ogre and the Captain across four kits**, plus equip, strip, perk buy, the spend banner, the accordion, both new hovers, LINT 0, no console errors, save restored byte-identical. ⚑ **The finding of the round is a correction to the last one**: 8f.164 claimed the ACTIONS row let "a three-action Captain read as three". **He has two.** `unitFrom` gives every roster body `actionsMax:2` and the `t.captain?3:2` line belongs to the FOE statblock builder. The row is honest and currently constant, which is what he asked for; the changelog above is corrected in place. **A number you did not read out of the running engine is a guess, even when it is in the file.** | ✅ |
| 8f.172 | **#144 - A SPENT CLAIM GIVES ITSELF BACK, AND THE THREE NUMBER SCANS BECOME ONE PARSER.** *(2026-08-13. The pre-commit hook refused session 4ab54b74's own #143 commit because the CHANGELOG row it was adding mentioned #141 and 8f.169, shipped hours earlier by another session and still claimed.)* **The commit was not a collision. It was a CITATION, which is what a changelog row is made of** - and six more dead claims (8f.165, 8f.166, 8f.168, 8f.170, #140, #142) were queued behind it to do the same thing to whoever cited them next. `number`, `status` and `verify` now drop **any** claim, anybody's, whose number is in main's committed record. ⚑ **THE ARGUMENT FOR DROPPING IT IS ARITHMETIC, NOT TRUST: `Claim-One` takes its floor from the REPO and never from the claims**, so the instant a number is written down the floor is above it and no session can be issued it again, with or without the file. From that moment the claim is not holding a seat, it is holding a **word**, and the only thing left it can do is refuse an honest sentence. ⛔ **AND IT READS COMMITTED `main`, NEVER THE WORKING TREE, WHICH IS THE WHOLE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN A FIX AND A HOLE.** A working tree is something the guarded session can write to: let somebody hand-write a `#NN` they never claimed (the sixth collision, 2026-08-11, and the first caused by a document) and a working-tree scan would see their own uncommitted line, drop the real holder's claim, and wave through the exact commit `pre-commit` exists to refuse. **Committed `main` is the one piece of evidence a session cannot manufacture for itself**, and `main` rather than `HEAD` because it is the only ref every desk and the main folder agree on, which is #139's shared-store lesson one layer out. **Measured, and the two scans were made to disagree on purpose:** with `#150` written into the working tree only, the repo scan sees it and raises its floor to 151 while the shipped scan does not see it at all, so the live claim stands and the commit is still refused (exit 1). ⚑ **THE SECOND HALF IS THE SAME "ONE FACT, TWO READERS" `Verb-Verify`'S OWN COMMENT IS ABOUT.** That comment describes the CSS-colour guard being written into the repo scan and never reaching the pre-commit scan; the answer then was to write it a second time, which left three hand-copied sets of the same regexes. They are **one parser** now (`Add-EntryNumbers` / `Add-BuildNumbers`) read by all three corpora, so a guard added to it cannot reach two and miss the third. ⚠ **Where two scans genuinely must differ the difference is in the signature so it cannot drift: `-Markdown` is OFF for the diff scan**, because a diff's code fences arrive as loose halves and pairing them there could strip a real reference out of the backstop's own eyes. ⛔ **The two scans keep OPPOSITE conservatism and a future tidy-up must not merge them:** "what may I not be issued" casts the widest net it can, because a number it misses is a collision; "what has definitely shipped" takes the narrowest and most authoritative source there is, because a number it wrongly counts is somebody's live seat thrown away. **Both are the careful direction; they are careful about opposite things.** The shipped corpus is the four writes named in `SHIPPED.md`'s own header, read at 534ms, which is what makes it affordable inside a git hook. ⚑ **And the rule this deletes is worth the ink: "claim early, release at the four writes" was correct, was written down, and was obeyed ZERO times in three weeks**, because it asks a session to do housekeeping at the moment it is finished and moving on. **When compliance is zero, stop editing the instruction and ask whether the machine can work it out** - here it always could, because the fact the rule wanted reported was already sitting in `main`. `status`'s NUMBERS HELD block means work in flight again, which is the one question anybody reads it for. **Verified:** the used-number scan is byte-identical before and after the refactor (entry floor 144, build floor 172, 142 entries and 165 builds, same list); the six real stale claims swept and the four a live session was holding untouched; a citation of shipped-but-held numbers passes; an unspent held number still blocks; the working-tree hole blocks. ⚠ `gate` and `hook` deliberately do NOT sweep: they are the hot paths, `hook` running on every Write and Edit. ⚠ Three non-ASCII glyphs left in comments by earlier sessions were removed, because the file's own header requires pure ASCII (PowerShell 5.1 reads a `.ps1` as ANSI and this repo's path is Cyrillic). ⛔ **AND THE ENTRY'S REAL FINDING WAS FOUND BY WATCHING ITS OWN COMMIT: THE PRE-COMMIT BACKSTOP HAD BEEN OFF IN EVERY DESK SINCE #139, PRINTING THE WORD "clear" EACH TIME.** The commit reported *"clear: no other session is holding a number"* while **twelve claims sat in the store**, four of them a live session's. ⚑ **`.git/hooks/pre-commit` is a SHELL script, so `claim.ps1` runs under `sh`, where the console is cp866** rather than whatever an interactive PowerShell happens to be. git hands back `C:/Users/USER/Google <Cyrillic>/...` as UTF-8 bytes, cp866 turns them into a path that does not exist, `Resolve-Path` throws, and `Get-StoreRoot`'s catch **quietly answered `$Root`** - so the store became the desk's own empty `.grimtoll\`, created fresh on the spot. **#139's whole load-bearing idea is that the store is shared, and this is that idea failing in the one place nobody watches, for a reason with nothing to do with git.** Reproduced by running the hook script under `sh` and watching a stray `.grimtoll\claims\` appear; diagnosed by printing the same `rev-parse` decoded both ways, where cp866 gives an unresolvable path and UTF-8 resolves. ⚠ **The `2>$null` was part of it and is deleted: under `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'`, REDIRECTING a native command's stderr is itself what promotes a harmless warning to a terminating NativeCommandError. Unredirected stderr is inert.** ⛔ **THE LESSON IS THE FALLBACK, NOT THE CODEPAGE: a fallback that cannot be told apart from success will wear the word "clear" for as long as nobody looks.** The quiet path now covers only "no git, not a repo"; a `rev-parse` that answers and then fails to resolve prints a loud stderr block saying the store is private and the guards are off. **Verified from `sh`:** a staged line spending a live session's number now exits 1 with the collision named, a line citing a shipped one exits 0, and no stray store is created. Rules: [`PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md`](PARALLEL_SESSIONS.md), section 6 rewritten. | ✅ |
| 8f.179 | **#151 - THE EIGHT-POINT BATCH: A GREEDY DOOR, A MERGED MENU, A SIGN THAT ADMITS IT IS A RUMOUR, AND ONE RECEIPT PER FIGHT.** *(User, 2026-08-14, eight numbered notes, then "and work autonomusly - i am out in the city".)* ⛔ **POINT 1'S PREMISE WAS FALSE AND SAYING SO IS THE ENTRY'S FIRST FINDING.** *"From the hanging man event you deleted the option to take all and boots - return it, it was the best."* THE HANGED TOLL-MAN is the only hanged man in the game, and **every commit in the repo carries the same three doors**: the boots door was never deleted, `bootsOn` appears twice in every version back to the initial commit, and the generated `events_book.html` agrees. What was missing is the door he was describing - not the purse **or** the bridge but **both** - so he was asked, chose "add a fourth", and got it. **"Take all of it. Purse, boots, beams."** pays 38 crowns, 5 salvage and the boots for the whole afternoon, **six** of the company's regard rather than two, and the same `hideHurt` falling beam. ⚑ **The pillar's second clause is why the card wanted it: bad deeds must genuinely pay, and two half-robberies are each small enough to feel like restraint.** The label stays vague like the other three, because this is the REFERENCE card and the chips carry the receipt. **The card is 158 words - HEAVY, inside its #143 band, and at four doors it is on the style rule's ceiling, not over it.** **2 - ONE MENU ROW.** *"merge 'send the run' and 'platest notes'. Show platest notes only in dev mode."* The two rows were **one errand wearing two names**: Send the run opens the questions and its Done button opens the notes, and the notes screen's own first section is SEND IT BACK with the questions button in it. Playtest notes is behind `TEST.on` now, and reading somebody else's was **already** behind it (`nPaste`, since the screen was built), which is what makes the gate consistent rather than a control hiding inside a screen anybody could open. He was asked whether it should be a separate tool and ruled **keep it in dev mode**. ⚠ **The redraw could not go where it belonged**: `show()` calls `syncTest` and `openMenu` calls `show`, so rebuilding the menu from inside `syncTest` is unbounded recursion. It hangs off the toggle handler, the one place `TEST.on` actually changes. **3 - THE POSITIVE TWIN GOES TOO.** *"'It sits well with them' - delete it too, so it is cleaner."* #150 deleted *"it sits badly with them"* from 24 cards and left its twin because he had pointed at one of them; **16 occurrences** across EVENTS and CAMPS, gone, three labels emptied and all three carry a morale fx so no door renders blank. `choiceNote()` joining rather than gluing is what made this a four-line change instead of a hunt for floating middle dots - #150's own build paying for itself. **4 - THE FORK SIGN IS A RUMOUR AND ALWAYS SPANS TWO NUMBERS.** *"never write a clear '1 fith' or '0 fignts'. Write aprox 1-2 … It should be some unsertunty - keep it as a rule."* ⛔ **The old ceiling was measuring the wrong thing.** It asked whether a dealt card on the road had a battle door, which is true of exactly one of the eighteen floating cards, so it almost never rose - but a hard number is wrong here for a reason the count cannot see: **a node typed `battle` is not a fight that must happen.** `bm` is typed `battle` and the Broken Men's first door is "No." while the other two are four days of food or forty crowns; the sling-line and the steading are the same shape since #123. **A player who paid the brigands walked a road the sign had promised a battle on.** Floor is what stands on the road, ceiling is always one more: the brigand road reads **~1-2**, the water road **~0-1**, which are his own two numbers and #58's before him. The counted walk is deleted with its reasoning kept, because it was a graph walk per road per render for a number nobody could read. **5 - THE COST LINES COUNT IN THE GAME'S OWN GLYPHS.** *"Show in the choices curency and provision special symbols."* `RES_ICON` is what the top bar counts in and what every receipt chip has worn since #137; the choice label was **the last surface still spelling the four resources out**, so a player read "−30 crowns" on the button and saw ◉ on the chip that charged it. ⛔ **It is a renderer pass and it had to be**: the `c:` strings are authored English, they are what `content/events_book.html` exports for him to edit, and `LINT()` reads them for arithmetic receipts - a sweep through 34 cards would have put the symbol in the authoring source. `resGlyphs()` fires only on a **digit-led** phrase, so "cheaper than arrows" and "he has no further use for either" keep their words. Four renderers call it (`choiceNote`, `ask`, `actCard`, the village rows). ⚠ **It lives beside `RES_ICON` and not beside `choiceNote` where it was first written**: `ask` is declared 3,000 lines above, and a `const` read from above its own declaration is the TDZ crash that aborts this whole script, which this file has shipped once. **6 - ONE LOOT RECEIPT AFTER BLOOD ON THE ROAD.** *"there 2 screens with loot - strange."* #150's waiting door pays its own haul on the clash card and drew a chip row there; one button later the aftermath deals a row off `LOOT.clash` and draws TAKEN OFF THE FIELD. **Two receipts, two screens, one fight - the six-screen queue #122 collapsed, growing a screen back.** The payment stays where the door is known (#137's rule: chips are built off the object that was paid), the **receipt** travels in `G.clashHaul` and is **summed** into the one the aftermath already owns, then cleared by whoever prints it. ⚠ It rides in the save for one screen, because a reload landing in that gap must not lose the receipt for coin the chest already has. **Measured: with a table row that also pays coin the chips read ◉+85 ❦+4 ▤+5 and the purse moved by exactly that** - summed, never doubled. **7 - THE BROKEN MEN SAY HOW MANY THEY ARE.** *"show ammount of them against."* Style rule 8. **4 men and 2 dogs**, counted off `brigands()` (bcap + 2 sworder + poacher + 2 dog); the dogs are the half of that fight that surprises people and the door had never named them. **8 - THE STAR ON THE COMPANY CHIP KEEPS ITS PROMISE.** *"When i click on the company icon on the global map - lead me to skills (there is a star shows there is an upgrade)."* ⛔ **The badge and the door disagreed.** #150 put a ★ there because somebody has an unspent level; pressing it opened the sheet on whoever was last selected, on THE STASH tab. Nothing new is built - `levelsWaiting` already found the point, `iSel` picks the body, `ITAB` picks the pane - the chip just sets both, the way `iSpend` inside the sheet already does. ⚠ **A perk point and a stat point are spent in different rooms**, so a perk switches the tab and a stat only picks the body (its spend buttons are the WHO THIS IS tells, always on screen). The kit chip is untouched: its `!` means gear and gear is the stash tab. ⛑ **AND TAKING THE NEW DOOR FOUND A REAL BUG THAT HAD NOTHING TO DO WITH IT.** `bootsOn` finds a wearer with `G.party.find(ratkin) || pickOne(G.party)`, and the fallback can draw the Captain, **whose roster name is the literal word "You"** - so the line read *"It is Gutter-Born (human captain). Of course it is You."* That is README section 6's standing trap (*"any new template that interpolates a name has to survive him"*), fixed where it says to fix it, in `capName` and never at the call site. **The second `bootsOn` site is what doubled the odds of hitting it.** Its pronoun went too: the wearer can be anybody, so "He walks better" was a coin flip and the boots do the talking now. **AND THE AUDIT HE ASKED FOR: *"Check all last 20-30 tasks - are they done? Haven't we lost something into merging?"*** ✅ **Nothing is lost.** All three work branches (`work/coin-race`, `work/intro-hit`, `claude/elegant-mcclintock-c57579`) are **0 commits ahead of main**, the stash list is empty, `localmain` is level, and the five dangling commits are pre-amend copies - `27496a4` and main's `098ec4a` have **byte-identical trees**. Thirty entries #120-#150 were asserted **against the running build** rather than against the docs: #137's chips and `cool:2`, #138's `tavernContract`/`contractSettle`, #117's `labelSpot` and `NAME_DY_SIGHT` 54, #146's `HIT_EASE {you:10,foe:5}`, #148's `mayAim`, #149's setInterval-free AUTO, #143's 34 cards all in band, #141's one-door coin and floating star, #129's six rungs, #132's four real stat names, #113-#121's 31 sights with 0 unmapped. ⚠ **The only real gap is documentary and it is three rows: #117, #138 and #137 shipped into the build and the CHANGELOG and never got their line in `SHIPPED.md`** - the four writes were three. Added here. ⚠ Two probe results were **false negatives worth recording so the next audit does not chase them**: `levelsWaiting` and `fallBack` failed a `typeof window[n]` test because one is a top-level `const` arrow (which does not attach to `window`) and the other is function-scoped inside `aiTurn`; both are present and correct, and `fallBack` does charge the parting swing. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots:** LINT **0** · no em dash in EVENTS · `spacingViolations` 0 · `labelViolations` 0 · unmapped sights 0 · 34 cards, **3,703 words**, none outside their #143 bands · all three forks read as ranges · the eight-fight regression clean with no uncaught errors (clash 4rd · brigand 8 · pack 7 · slingline 5 · steading 9 · snare 9 · mother 6 · armour 7) · the menu row appearing and disappearing live with the switch · the company chip driven in all four states · the new toll door driven end to end with every delta measured · the clash haul driven on both the waiting and the ratkin doors · **the user's live day-1 save backed up first and confirmed byte-identical after.** Eye check: [`shots/151_batch.html`](../shots/151_batch.html), five live-DOM captures drawn with the build's own stylesheet fetched rather than copied. | ✅ |
| 8f.180 | **#152 - PAYDAY EVERY FOURTH DAY: THE WAGE BECOMES A BILL, THE ROADS LOSE THEIR CROWNS, AND TWO WAGES CHANGE HANDS.** *(User, 2026-08-14, first the question: "based on my references game, how often do you think i need payments for crew? I am thinking once in 4-5 days +-. So it ads some pressure, but also you don't need to calculate every day" - then the ruling on the analysis: "ececute this new system of padayin 4 day. Add this to global map more clear. Also - if we do so, we can delete crowns from each road and crosroad decigion - which is good. Make payment laude (not and independent screen event, but some local thing that highlight that it was paid). Also change, that cost of main hero costs 1 in day / instead of 2. And ogr a day 2 instead of 1. And update concept.")* ⚑ **THE ARGUMENT THAT DECIDED THE NUMBER: daily wages were Battle Brothers' cadence without its timescale.** Every reference paying daily (BB, Bannerlord) runs campaigns hundreds of days long, where erosion has time to be felt; Wartales, the long-campaign game closest in structure, bills every third day as a dated event. This run is **19 to 23 travel days** (measured by walking every EDGES path), so daily charging was ~22 invisible taps and the concept's own line "wages exist so that robbery becomes tempting" had no date on which to come true. **Four, not five**: 5-6 paydays a run instead of 4-5 (enough repetitions to learn the rhythm), one payday per fork branch (A is 4/6 days, B 3/4, C 3/4 - "which road" and "can I make payday" become one question), and a ~28-40 crown bill sits just under the ~45-55 median event find, so one score covers one payday and the exchange rate is legible. **THE MECHANISM: wages ACCRUE, the chest opens on the day.** `G.owed` grows by `dailyUpkeep()` per day inside `passDays()` - silently, no mood cost, that is the "don't calculate every day" half - and on days divisible by `PAY_EVERY` the whole figure settles through `pay()`'s clamp (`G.owed+=pay('crowns',-G.owed)` is the entire settlement: a full chest zeroes it, a scraped one leaves the remainder standing and it BILLS AGAIN on top of the next cycle). ⚑ **Accrual is what lets the roads lose their crowns without losing the truth**: a six-day branch still runs up two more days of wages than a four-day one, so the fork's central trade survives the deletion of every ◉ on it - the map label is `1d` bare (was `1d · ◉8`), the fork rows are days · fights · danger word (the `~◉bill` column and its fundedCol are gone), the fork foot names the join instead of the daily rate, and legCard drops its wage sentence, its #20 horizon line and the `~◉bill` off its button. **That cashes #150's own open remainder** ("the fork row still carries the wage bill... one line to remove") with the user's order in hand. **A MISSED PAYDAY IS A STATE**: everything #5/#114 tuned survives untouched in the shortfall branch - 10 mood a day, `p.unpaid`++ daily (still DAYS, never paydays: the 4-nerve-per-day field maths and the five-day walkout were tuned in days and a coarser unit would move the grievance in lumps of four), the three-days line, the 22% walkout at five with its #73 clan exemption - what moved is only WHEN it starts: the first short day is a payday now, so the grievance opens with the shock instead of leaking from day one, and the walkout window now requires two consecutive missed paydays (miss day 8 at unpaid 1, days 9-11 climb to 4, a second miss at day 12 reaches 5). The catch-up also survives: while anybody is owed, every day-end tries the chest, because the UNPAID hover has promised "fill the chest and the wages catch up on their own" since #114 and that promise is load-bearing. **THE CHIP: UPKEEP becomes PAYDAY** ("Add this to global map more clear"). Paid up it reads `PAYDAY ◉36 IN 2D` - the projected bill at the due date, not the rate, because the calendar was the player's homework and now it is the chip's - and behind it reads `◉owed OVERDUE` red beside the existing UNPAID chip (two facts, two units: the crowns and the grievance). The alarm rule is #106's: bone while the chest covers the coming bill, amber when it covers only what has accrued, red when it cannot even do that, so colour means trouble and never furniture. `fundedDays()`/`fundedCol()` are deleted with their #20 ruling kept in the chip's colour (informed consent, never a gate; no road ever disables). **THE LOUD PAYMENT** ("Make payment laude... not and independent screen event"): `G.paidFlash` arms on a fully-met payday and the next bar rebuild wears `.payflash` on the PAYDAY chip and the crowns segment - one rebuild wide, gold going out, ⚠ **the keyframe declares only its 0% frame on purpose** so the implicit end frame is each element's own computed style and the chip (#221a10) and the strip segment (#161009) each settle back to their own colours - plus `sfx('coin')` and a say2 line (two variants). Never a card, never a screen: #138's empty-box defect stays dead. **TWO WAGES CHANGE HANDS**: `wageOf(p)` is the new one door (`p.leader?1:WAGE[p.race]`) - the Captain draws ONE crown whatever his race, the chest is his problem and the coin is bookkeeping - and **the ogre goes to 2, a human's wage, which kills the one-crown joke** #142 preserved on purpose; RACEHELP now reads "somebody finally told him what the others get, and he thinks slowly but he does not forget", and the keep-hover's "(he takes three seats and asks for one coin)" went with it. ⛑ **The concept's wage table was ALREADY lying**: it said ogre daily wage 3 while the build said 1, drift nobody had caught because the table and the code had no shared reader - fixed to 2 with the drift named in place. Starting economics: the four-human boot party drops 8/day to **7/day** (captain 1+2+2+2), the first payday bills **21** (day 4 bills three days, because day 1 never passes through passDays), and the 5661 purse comment re-derived. `G.owed` rides the save in `flags` with `||0` guards so an older save arrives square; `G.paidFlash` is transient on purpose. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots**: LINT **0** · no em dash in EVENTS · `spacingViolations` 0 · `labelViolations` 0 (the label got NARROWER) · boot upkeep 7, first bill 21 due in 3 · a paid payday driven (day 4: exactly −21, flash armed then consumed by one worldTick, the line said) · a missed one (day 8: chest 5 scraped to 0, owed 23, morale −10, all unpaid 1, the payday shock line) · a standing day (owed 30, −20, unpaid 2) · the mid-cycle catch-up (chest 200: owed 37 settles itself, unpaid clears, "The back wages are squared. Nobody says thank you.") · the chip in all three states (`PAYDAY ◉14 IN 2D` bone · `◉14 OVERDUE` red with UNPAID beside it · `.payflash` worn) · the bar at 1280 with 516+185 used, no overflow · the fork card with **zero ◉ in it** ("4 days · ~1-2 fights · TROUBLE", foot "every road here meets at The Ruined Steading") · the road label `1d`. | ✅ |
| 8f.181 | **THE DOCS WERE CUT AND CLEANED, AND THE ONE-LINE VERSION IS THAT AN ARCHIVE FOLDER IS A SHELF FOR THINGS GIT ALREADY HOLDS.** *(User, 2026-08-14: "update and clean all project documents based on recent activiy", then mid-pass: "cut or put in archive that doesnt take extra space all garbage and older things (just not tools - they are cool)")*. **Nothing in the record was wrong, which is worth saying first**: #145 to #151 were all four-written by the sessions that shipped them, so this pass found no missing rows and invented no history.
