It is now a fifteen-row table plus **ten lessons that outlive their entries**, 59 KB to 40 KB, with every rule kept and every number checked against `SHIPPED.md`. **`WHAT_TO_TEST.md` keeps 2026-08-12 onward** (198 KB to 94 KB, 3,064 lines to 1,543): most of what went had been superseded by a later round of the same screen, and testing a round that no longer exists is worse than not testing. **`docs/archive/` lost all six dated snapshots**, 1.3 MB to 287 KB, and ⚑ **the reason they could go is this pass's finding: a superseded document does not need a shelf, it needs a commit hash.** `git show 5bb2bf2:docs/archive/<name>` returns any of them whole, so keeping them on disk was paying twice for one copy. | 8f.182 | **#154 - EVERY DOOR ON THE ROAD WEARS ITS INTENT, AND THE SET WAS MEASURED DOWN RATHER THAN INVENTED.** *(User, 2026-08-14: "adding some visual emoji to each choise - so they visually distingushible ... each can have up to two", with his own worked example: "Blood on the road - first two choices fight + hand. For rest - ⛺ is good", then "when i hover on this in choice - show me the tag of that type", then the race pass.)* **HIS FOUR CATEGORIES WERE RIGHT AND THE BUILD NEEDED FOUR MORE, WHICH IS A COUNT AND NOT AN OPINION.** Every door in `EVENTS` and `CAMPS` was read before the set was proposed: FIGHT/HELP/EVIL/LEAVE cover his example exactly, and TRADE, TAKE, REST and HONOR are what the remaining ~40 road doors do. ⛔ **A first draft had TEN and the count killed two of them**: TALK, because every talk door carries another intent underneath (the ogre courtesy is a payment, the toast is help, the wishes carry their own colour), and RISK, because it duplicated `danger:true` - **a glyph that says what the button styling already says is furniture**, which is #137's finding arriving on the same surface it was learned on. Final eight: **⚔️ FIGHT · 🤝 HELP · ☠️ EVIL · 👣 LEAVE · ⚖️ TRADE · 🧺 TAKE · ⛺ REST · 🙏 HONOR**, verb first and moral colour second, so ⚔️🤝 is fighting *for* somebody and ⚔️☠️ is fighting as the predator. ⛔ **☠️ AND DELIBERATELY NOT 💀: the morale chips have worn 💀 as the lowest `MOODSTATES` face since #150, and one glyph may not mean two things on one screen** (#102's wrong-unit rule, caught in design rather than in play this time). **🧺 IS THE OPEN SACK UNICODE HAS NOT GOT** - he asked for one, 💰 is tied shut, and the basket is the open thing loot goes into; it was shipped with that limitation stated rather than silently substituted. **⚖️ then freed TRADE from sharing a money glyph with TAKE**, which was the actual defect in the first pick. **THE RACE MARK IS DERIVED, NEVER AUTHORED** (🐀 · 👹 · 🧑): `needRace` already IS the fact, so `choiceIco()` reads it, and this is **#137's rule on its fourth surface** - red was hand-flagged per fight door and five doors had never been given it. ⚠ **There is no human-gated door in the build and that is correct rather than missing**: the company is human by default, so the gate would open on nearly every company and a mark that fires on every door is furniture. 🧑 is defined and waits for the door that earns it. **The cap is two and `LINT` 6c holds it**, which is why a race-gated door authors exactly one intent. **ONE BUILDER, TWO RENDERERS** - `choiceIco()` beside `choiceNote()`, both called by the EVENTS and the CAMPS renderer, nothing concatenating at a call site; that is #150's `choiceNote` lesson reused rather than rediscovered. Glyphs render at **18px** over the label's 15px so the eye sorts before it reads, and each carries a `title` so hovering names the tag (**HONOR · bury, pray, respect**). **THE FIRE'S RULINGS STAY BARE ON PURPOSE**: the CAMPS deck is mostly judgments rather than actions - the debt, the aunt, the nine seconds - and bare reads as *this is you deciding, not doing*. **94 of 94 road doors carry an intent; 31 of 53 camp doors do.** **THE FEN-MOTHER DROPS TO TWO DOORS** *("for fen mother leave only 2 choices ... + 2 days travel to the next stop + tooth. Fight.")*: the back-away door is cut, the third this card has lost since #123 took the ambush, and the tooth door's gem and morale ding go with it - **its whole price is the two days it takes to walk wide of her hollow**, and the chips say so. ⚠ **The kill door is NOT tagged evil, by his explicit ruling** *("this option isn't evel ... i don't want it to be for the first playthrought")*: hunting a beast for its hide is a hunt, and the glyph set has to survive the author disagreeing with it. Card is **97 words, MEDIUM, inside its #143 band**. ⛑ **AND THE PASS FOUND TWO EM DASHES THAT HAD SHIPPED FOR MONTHS**, both in CAMPS prose (`debt3`'s *"So does the rule — quietly"* and `fiddler2`'s *"The tune does not — it simply moves"*), which every previous gate missed because **the standing check reads `EVENTS` alone and the CAMPS deck speaks the same player-facing language** - the identical blind spot #151 hit counting a stock phrase over one table. Both fixed; the gate in `.claude/rules/event-cards.md` now names both. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots** (the pane composites no frames): LINT **0** · no em dash in EVENTS **or** CAMPS · 94/94 road doors tagged · 0 unknown glyph keys · 0 doors over the two-glyph cap · 34 cards, **3,694 words**, none outside their #143 bands (14 light · 10 medium · 10 heavy) · the toll-man, clash, pedlar, oasis and Fen-Mother driven live and their rendered `<b>` and `title` attributes read off the DOM · the four race-gated cards driven with a seeded ogre and ratkin in the company, all showing intent+race and none showing three. | ✅ |
| 8f.183 | **#155 - THE OPENING PASS: TWO SCREENS DELETED, A THIRD STOPPED STRETCHING, AND THE ARCHER FINALLY STOPPED HOVERING A SWORD.** *(User, 2026-08-14, a numbered list during a playthrough, then four more notes as he kept playing.)* ⛔ **THE ARROW BUG IS THE ENTRY'S REAL FINDING AND IT IS #102 POINT 12 ON ITS SECOND SURFACE.** *"When archer focus on something to shoot - it is arrow, not sword."* Since #91 MOVE is the standing selection, so a click on an enemy IS the attack and the grid wears `cMove`; the one CSS rule under it, `#bGrid.cMove .hex.hfoe`, hard-coded `--curStrike`. ⚑ **`--curShot` has existed since #111 and this rule was never told**: an archer hovering a body in bow range was promised a blade by the cursor and loosed an arrow on the click. **The identical fault was fixed once already, in `iconFor()`** - `ICON.main` is the literal `'⚔'` because *main is a SLOT and not a weapon*, and the action card was taught to ask the act whether it has range. The board's cursor was the last site that never got that lesson. `hshot` is stamped off `w0.range` beside the existing `hfoe`, so it is DERIVED from the weapon in hand, and a new ranged weapon gets the right hand untaught (#137's rule, fifth surface). **TWO SCREENS DELETED, BOTH BY HIS OWN WORDS.** *"this thing delete completly"* pointed at **THE GRAUSEN ROAD**, the last text card between the tap-room and the road; *"this one is also unnesesery for explanation"* pointed at the tour's **THE DAY** step. ⚑ **The boot card could go because three rounds of cutting had already emptied it**: #71 took its three-roads paragraph, 2026-08-02 merged two coach pop-ups into it, #120 moved the map/resources/company/day paragraphs out into the `w_*` spotlights. **Each round took prose and left the box**; this took the box. Nothing it said is lost - the contract plate has held the job since #118, the tour holds the rest, the strongbox is the crown chip, and the pillar line *"you cannot afford to be good to everyone"* is what the road's priced doors have BEEN since #123. `enterWorld()` calls `worldTut()` directly; `wDlg` stays because it is the road's event dialog. ⛑ **And deleting it paid off a stale-number warning that had been standing since #112**: the strongbox figure was written twice, once as a constant and once in WORDS in the card's prose, with a comment saying so. One copy now. `HEADWORD`/`headcount()` went with it rather than being left for a future caller; `w_day` is deleted and not left dark, which is #120's own register rule. **THE OPENING CARD WAS BEING STRETCHED TO ITS OWN CEILING** *("Adjust size", then his pick: the card is too tall and empty)*. ⚑ **MEASURED FIRST, WHICH IS #88's RULE, AND THE MEASUREMENT INVERTED THE DIAGNOSIS**: the card sat at 0,0 of the 1280x720 screen at 800x684 with `scrollHeight === clientHeight`, so it was not overflowing, it was **empty** - `.screen.on` is `display:flex` with `align-items:normal`, which is stretch, so the box grew to `max-height` whatever the prose measured and the dead space landed under the door. `#prologue{align-items:center;justify-content:center}` and it is 800x542 centred, 89px of ground above and below. ⚠ **AND CENTRING EXPOSED A 7px PHANTOM SCROLLBAR THAT HAD BEEN HIDING IN THE SLACK.** `.popts` is a STICKY footer built for a card that CAN scroll: `padding-bottom:24px` with `margin-bottom:-24px` to cancel it. Chrome counts that padding in `scrollHeight` and does not count the card's own 17px bottom padding, so a card whose prose fits still measured 7px over and `overflow:auto` drew a bar. Matching the two paddings makes it exactly zero; the 684 ceiling and the real scroll on a tall card are untouched. **⚔️ ON THE FIRST DOOR IN THE GAME** *("Put swords like any fight decigion on (1st screen)")*. ⚑ **It was the only battle door in the build without the mark, and the reason is a gap in the gate rather than an oversight**: #154 walked `EVENTS` and `CAMPS`, `LINT` 6b reads those two tables, and **the prologue is neither**. `proCard` calls `choiceIco(o)` now - the same one builder, nothing concatenated at a call site - so every proCard door can wear an intent, and the comment carries the guard `LINT` cannot. **THE ♥ MENDING CHIP GIVES ITS SPOT TO THE HEADCOUNT** *("you can delete morale - and put party size in that spot")*. ⚠ **His first phrasing put this on the road bar and he corrected it mid-session** *("no, taking morale not for road bar - from opening screen where you choose name. Return to road bar")*, so the road bar is untouched and still wears its mood chip. ⛑ **The naming screen had no morale on it to delete - #150 had already cut that chip** - so he was shown the strip's actual two chips and picked the heart. **⛊ 4 HEADS beside ◉ +88 CROWNS**: this is the screen where the run stops being one man at a table, and the one number that says so is how many of you there are; the heart was answering *who is hurt*, which the road bar's health chip answers permanently and better. ⛔ **Bodies, not wagon seats**: the road bar's ⛊ 8/13 is `partyUsed()` summing `COST[race]`, which reads "8" for four humans - right for a wagon, wrong for a sentence about people. ⚠ **⛊ and not ♥, which is #102's wrong-unit rule**: ♥ is blood everywhere in this game. **BLOOD ON THE ROAD AT HALF LENGTH, WITH A FACE ON EVERY JOINER** *("2 times less text and a portrait near the ones who joins")*. ⚑ **The strip is the prologue's own** - `.tvrwho`/`.tvrp`/`.tvrf` and the `data-bust` canvas the tap-room naming strip already uses - so one set of rules means *somebody just joined* and the painted head arrives free through `bustFor`. ⚑ **And the text halved itself once the face was there**: `traitDesc` is the SHEET's paragraph (Nib's runs 35 words about what other clans say) and `traitShort` is the one-line form written for exactly this job, with the long version one click away on the sheet. Every prose branch keeps its one concrete image and loses the restatement. ⛔ **Both em dashes in the card went out with the words**, and a third was found in `TRAITSHORT.kind`, which this change put on screen. **AND A THIRD STOCK PHRASE IS DELETED** *("what does it mean 'the company stands toller for this' - delete this from all choices")*, after #150's *"it sits badly with them"* (24 cards) and #151's *"it sits well with them"* (16). **11 occurrences**, counted across EVENTS **and** CAMPS **and** VIGNETTES because a count over one table is the wrong count (#151's own lesson). ⚠ **The MERCY card's renderer had to be guarded**: it writes `'<i>'+o.c+'</i>'` unconditionally and one of its rows is now empty - `<i></i>` still costs its line-height, which is the fault #150 pinned on proCard. **The two gated doors keep "needs an ogre"; the phrase alone came out.** ⛔ **"You scum" loses one word** *("this one nicer ... we don't need 'scum' here")*: "You lot" keeps the address and the pleasant delivery, which is what the line was for. **The ogre spotlight gains a sentence of lore** *("near description of ogre - a bit lore - it is a barmen")*: *"The room is his, and so is the furniture you are breaking."* **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots** (the pane composites no frames): LINT **0** · no em dash in EVENTS, CAMPS **or** VIGNETTES · 0 doors missing an intent · 0 occurrences of the stock phrase across all three tables · `spacingViolations` 0 · `labelViolations` 0 · unmapped sights 0 · **spear over an enemy hex renders the crossed-blade cursor and archer over the same hex renders the fletched arrow**, both read off `getComputedStyle` and told apart by their SVG path data, with a melee unit out of reach correctly offering nothing · the prologue card measured at 800x542, left 240, top 89, bottom 89, no scrollbar, door reading `⚔️Draw your sword.` with the `FIGHT · starts a battle` hover · the contract card still inside its 684 ceiling at 683 with `◉ +88 CROWNS` `⛊ 4 HEADS` and three painted faces · the door driven end to end to the map, landing on `world` with the company named, **no wDlg card**, and the tour queue reading `w_res · w_company · w_road` · Blood on the Road driven live, its joiner canvas measured as fully painted · the road bar re-read afterwards and still carrying `mood:😐 AT EASE`. | ✅ |
| 8f.184 | **#156 - TWELVE PLAYTEST NOTES, AND SIX OF THEM ARE THE SAME SENTENCE: THE BOARD WAS ANSWERING QUESTIONS NOBODY ASKED AND NOT THE ONE THEY DID.** *(User, 2026-08-14, a numbered list during a playthrough, then three more as he kept playing.)* ⛔ **THE ENTRY'S REAL FINDING IS THAT THE ENEMY-REACH FEATURE ALREADY EXISTED AND WAS INVISIBLE EXACTLY WHERE IT MATTERED.** *"Показывать доход противника - куда он может дойти... срабатывает при наведении на противника без выбранного действия (или с ходьбой). Если наведение с ударом - то только куда противник может дойти и % удара."* `peekOn`/`paintPeek` have painted a hovered body's reach since the hover cache went in, and two things hid it. **First, `paintPeek` only ever wrote a hex whose base fill was TRANSPARENT** - and since #91 the standing selection is MOVE, so every hex within your own stride already carried the teal walk wash. The enemy's reach was therefore drawn only on ground you cannot reach yourself, and **the overlap, which is the entire question** (*can he reach me where I am about to stand*), was the one part never shown. **Second, `readout()` returns early on the aiming path and never calls `peekOn` at all**, so the moment you picked a blow the reach vanished. ⚑ **The fix is a rule, not a special case: while the cursor is on a body, the board is about where THAT body goes.** Its tint now outranks both washes describing your own turn - the walk preview and the selected act's footprint - and `peekOn` moved to the top of `readout` so both cards carry it. Everything louder than a wash (a lit target, a lane, a lift, a landing) still wins, because those are OFFERS and an offer is never overpainted by a hover; nor is the acting body's own reach when you hover yourself. The three washes are named constants now (`REACH_WASH`) for one reason: a colour compared against a literal written in two places is a colour that drifts. **Measured live: 18 of the bow's 35 footprint hexes repaint dark red, 0 walk hexes left unpainted, 0 when hovering the actor.** **TWO READOUTS DELETED OFF THE HEXES.** *"Не показывать на гексах номера ходов"*: the step count was printed on all 59 reachable tiles while the one number that decides anything - how far this body goes - is already on the MOVE card and the plaque off the same `moveBudget()` call. `badge` is gone from the whole loop and `.hbadge` with it. *"Убирать иконку местности при ходе - достаточно основного пнг"*: the impassable ▲ ♣ ▮ ✿ ♨ was written in the #15 era when a blocked hex was a flat plate with nothing on it, and it has been losing ground ever since - the 2026-08-01 round already cut it to *"only while choosing a move"* for the reason he now gives in full, that a ▮ centred on a stacked timber wall is a beige box stamped over the one thing telling you it is a wall. ⚑ **The hover title stays and is now the whole answer**; the dark fill and hard border stay, because they are the rule and not a glyph; **the liftable boulder keeps its mark, because it is an OFFER and not a label**. `askingWhereToWalk` is deleted with it rather than left dark. ⛔ **THE TUTORIAL SPOTLIGHT WAS NOT STOPPING THE FIGHT, AND THE LEAK IS ONE LINE FROM WHERE IT LOOKS.** *"Когда на битве в туториале подсказка - останавливать все другие действия... чтобы противники не ходили, во время обьяснения."* `B.tutLock` already refused every PLAYER input - `selectAct`, `clickHex`, the hotkeys, Esc - so the missing half was never the player's. **`capTick()` is what runs `tavernTick()`, which calls `tavTeach()`, which flushes the step to the screen; and `capTick` runs inside `nextTurn` at the END of the turn that just finished**, while `cur()` is still the player. The card went up and `nextTurn` carried straight on into `beginTurn`, handed the round to a brigand and let the AI take its turn behind the reader's back. `B.tutHold` holds the clock **after** `checkEnd` (a decided fight must still resolve; `tutClose` drops the flag without resuming), and `tutNext` releases it. ⚠ **The resume passes `nextTurn(true)` so `capTick` cannot run twice** - a once-per-turn seam with two consumers is this file's oldest shape. Driven end to end: held at idx 0 with the card up, three steps drained, and the turn advanced to Harl 900ms later. **TWO ACTIONS, EVERYWHERE, AND IT IS A REAL BALANCE CHANGE.** *"Почему у капитана 3 очков действия - давать везде только 2."* Two sources: #111's brawl spare step (the one place a player could count three crystals on their OWN plaque, which is what he asked about) and `t.captain?3:2` in `build()`, which gave three to six enemy bosses. ⚑ **A declared count still wins** (`t.actions||2`), so the Thing in Armour keeps 3 and the Fen-Mother 5 - what went is a count borrowed off a FLAG. The lesson the spare step bought is still taught by `TUTSTEPS.backstab`. ⛑ **The `? RULES` line said "the Captain and great beasts get more", true of enemy captains only and reading as a promise about yours** - fixed in the same edit. **Measured with ARENA, n=16 a fight over the five captain fights: 53/80 wins before, 59/80 after, and the shift is concentrated in the steading (2/16 to 6/16).** It is one expression if it has to come back. **THE ACTING BODY WEARS A HEXAGON NOW.** *"На поле боя чуть явнее подсветить - кто из моих юнитов ходит."* It already breathed light, and its hex already took a pale inline background - written onto the BUTTON, which is a rectangle behind a hexagon, so what it lit was the seams between tiles. `.hex.acting .hedge::before` is the same free layer `.hex.spot` uses, ivory for one of yours, gold for an ally, red for whoever is coming at you, breathing on the sprite's own 1.1s. **THE FRONT DOOR IS LABELS AND A PILLAR.** *"С меню на главной странице удалить подписи под пунктом мелким шрифтом. И подписи другие 'ты не можеш быть хорошо ко всем' сделать больше шрифтом."* Five rows of 9px mono under five 14px labels, and the one sentence saying what this game IS set at caption weight in the same dim ochre. Labels to 16.5px, captions gone, `#mSub` to 21px in the display face with the title's own shadow. ⚑ **One row keeps its figures and it is not a caption**: `Continue the road` has to say WHICH road or the only irreversible choice on the screen is made blind, so day, company and place ride ON the label. `o.c` is still written on every row and is the `title` hover - the strings are the rows' own documentation. Measured: title at 99, last row ending at 585, footer at 622, inside 720. **THE CONTRACT IS THE HEAD, NOT THE LETTER.** *"So deliver letter or kill - i prefere kill and shorten that block - so it itakes less space on global map"*, and *"don't need this part in the tutorial on the global map: The contract"*. #150 grew this plate to four facts because its one line was too thin to picture, and it over-corrected: at 400px two of the four wrapped, so a corner of a 1280px map carried five rendered rows of a job that has not changed since the tavern. The letter goes (a second objective the player can never discharge), the road-east line goes (the map draws it - the goal node wears the same trophy), the vassal clause goes (it is the WHY and the hover holds it whole). ⚑ **The weekly letter stays in the man's MOUTH**, because it is not the errand, it is the man: he has been reading them since the thaw and doing nothing, and losing the errand puts the two sentences side by side and sharpens it. **400x77 to 305x53, both rows on one line, measured.** `w_quest` is deleted from the tour the way #155 deleted `w_day` - a spotlight explaining that a contract plate is a contract plate was reading the plate out loud - so the tour is two steps and both teach something the surface does not say. **AND THE SECOND COMPANY DOES NOT FIGHT THE BRAWL AGAIN.** *"После туториала, если ты раз проиграл - второй раз начинать с экрана создания компании уже после драки в баре. ЕЕ не повторять."* ⚑ **The condition is "it has been played", not "you died"**: `LEGACY.priorDied` was the obvious flag and is the wrong one, because somebody who WON and pressed *A new company* would still be sat back down in the tap-room. `LEGACY.brawlDone` is set in `tavernAfter` after the SIM fork, so the practice field and the menu's own tutorial row can never claim it, and the row is still one click away on the front door. ⚠ **Safe because the brawl does not BUILD the company** - all four come out of `makeParty()` at boot and the fight only stands them on the board - and the two things it PAYS (the 8-crown loot row, +4 morale) are paid by hand off the same two constants, so a returning player starts neither richer nor poorer. `tavernContract` gains a third OPENING and nothing else: two sentences differ because the shipped pair describe him crossing wreckage and his first line is about a brawl that did not happen. **AND THE ROAD HAS A CAMERA.** *("allow to zoom in and zoom out and a little bit closer on the global map. And start a bit more zoomed in.")* ⚑ **WRITTEN ON THE BATTLEFIELD'S OWN SHAPE, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN DECISION RATHER THAN LAZINESS**: this game already has a camera the player has learned - three NAMED STOPS, the wheel steps between them, a drag looks somewhere else, the stop is a SETTING that outlives the run - so the road gets that one and not a second control language with a slider in it. Free zoom would also have to answer *what is 1.37 of a map*, which is a question no reader has. `WCAMS` is **ROAD 1.00 · NEAR 1.22 · CLOSE 1.50**, the bar is `#wZoom` top-right sharing every rule with `#bZoom`, and the stop rides `gt_wcam` beside `gt_cam`. ⛔ **1.00 IS THE FLOOR**: the painting is exactly the size of its box, so below 1 the player would be shown the ground where the map stops; "zoom out" means back to ROAD. **NEAR is the first-launch default**, which is the "start a bit more zoomed in" half, and it is a default rather than a decision taken away - the whole map is one press away and the stop the player lands on is remembered. ⛔ **THE LAYER IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** `#wCam` holds exactly what is drawn in DESIGN SPACE - the painting, the company, and the nodes and travel labels `drawNodes` appends - and every piece of chrome stays a child of `#wMap`, because a caption that zooms is a caption that leaves the screen. ⚠ **A `transform` MAKES A STACKING CONTEXT**, so `#wCam` takes z 9 to keep the shipped order (the map's furniture ran 2 to 9, the chrome that must stay over it runs 11 up) - and **the two plates that were interleaved move above it**, which is a fix: the contract plate's own note has said it *floats over the map* since #104 and it sat at 6, under every travel label that landed on it. ⚑ **NOTHING IN THE MAP'S ARITHMETIC MOVED**: the camera scales the FINISHED view, so `vy()`, `clampNodes`, `labelSpot` and both linters go on measuring the same 1280x638 design box, and `spacingViolations`/`labelViolations` read `NODES` and never the DOM - which is *why* this could be a camera rather than a re-layout. **`wcamPt()` is the one door for chrome anchored to a place on the map**, and `placeDlg` is its caller: an event card still opens beside its own node at every stop, which it would not do if the anchor were left in design space. **The follow is the battlefield's `CAM_EDGE` rule** (re-frame only when the company is near a rim, or the map slides under somebody reading a fork), and `WCAM.free` is handed back the moment the company sets off - a camera you have to give back is a camera you forget you took. ⛔ **No rim push, unlike the board**: on the board the cursor is over the field all turn, on the road it is on its way to a node, and a map that slid whenever the pointer passed an edge would be sliding most of the time. ⚠ **The registration sits directly under its own declarations and NOT beside `camBar(); panInit();`** six thousand lines above - a boot-time reader of a `const` that has not been evaluated is the TDZ crash this file has already shipped once. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots** (the pane composites no frames): LINT **0** · no em dash in EVENTS · 0 doors missing an intent · `spacingViolations` 0 · `labelViolations` 0 · unmapped sights 0 · **all eight fights through `regress()` twice, before and after the final edits** (clash 5rd · brigand 5rd · pack 5rd · slingline 5rd · steading 10rd · snare 8rd · mother 9rd · armour 8rd, every one `ok`) · 0 `.hbadge` and 0 `.blk` on a live board with 32 reachable hexes · the acting ring's computed `box-shadow` read off `.hedge::before` · the peek measured over the walk wash and over the footprint and refused on the actor · the spotlight hold driven from card-up to Harl's turn · foe captains reading `actionsMax 2` on the board · the plate at 305x53 · the front door's own geometry · the skipped opening driven to the map with `◉ +88 CROWNS` `⛊ 4 HEADS` · **the road camera at all three stops** (ROAD 1/0/0 and not panning · CLOSE maxX 640 maxY 339 · the clamp refusing both far corners · the floor and the ceiling holding under repeated steps · 25 nodes and every travel label inside `#wCam` with 0 left outside and the legend outside it · the card anchored to a node at design x832 opening at its drawn x1248 · the follow held while `free` and re-framing once it is cleared · `placeToken` snapping home · a fresh launch landing on NEAR with no stored stop). Eye check: `shots/156_playtest_batch.html`, four live captures on the build's own CSS. **The user's save was snapshotted out of `localStorage` and written back afterwards, three times.** | ✅ |
| 8f.185 | **#157 - TWELVE PLAYTEST NOTES, AND THE FIRST THREE TURNED OUT TO BE ONE BUG: THE TOKEN'S FURNITURE IS NAILED TO THE HEX, AND THE BODIES STOPPED FITTING THE HEX.** *(User, 2026-08-14, three notes during a playthrough and nine more as he kept playing.)* ⛔ **THE HEALTH BAR DISAPPEARED EXACTLY WHEN IT MATTERED, AND NOBODY HAD NOTICED BECAUSE THE TWO NOTES THAT DESCRIBE IT ARRIVED AS DIFFERENT NOTES.** *"Show health and armour above the heads of characters in battle, and a bit thinner"* and *"if surrounded (more than two) show a status over the head"* are the same defect from two sides. `.ubars` sat at `bottom:-6px`, and `.hrow{margin-top:-10px}` means that strip belongs to the row IN FRONT, which is painted after it: **proved with `elementsFromPoint` on a body with three on it, every probe point on its bar had another body's `.usp` over it.** So the bar vanished under whoever was standing in front - which is to say it vanished whenever the body was surrounded. ⚑ **AND THE SECOND HALF OF THE SAME CONSTANT WAS ALSO WRONG.** `.stbar` is a fixed `top:-15px`, chosen by measurement in #96 against a **43px generated sprite**; the painted bodies run **46 to 56px**, so a head tops out at -16 to -26 and **every status badge in the game has been drawn across the body's own face since the art landed**. Nothing warned, because the constant was still doing exactly what it was written to do. This is the README's own trap - *a constant derived from another box's size is a bug with a delay on it* - and both halves are fixed by one number: **`--head` is set per unit in `render()` as `30 - sp.h`**, the arithmetic `.hin`'s own flex-end layout already implies, and both the bars and the badges hang off it. A body that grows takes its furniture with it. The health bar goes 3px to 2 (his *"a bit thinner"*, the pair 6px to 5); the armour bar stays at 2 because its fill is already one pixel. **SURROUNDED IS A BADGE IN THE REGISTER THAT ALREADY EXISTED, AND NOT ONE RULE IS ADDED.** Two rules already scale with how many bodies are on a target and **neither had a mark anywhere on the board**: every attacker gets `+flankPow` per body past the first, and the situation charges the surrounded body `-(on-1)x5` nerve a turn. ⛔ **THREE IS THE THRESHOLD AND IT IS NOT A TASTE CALL.** Measured over **2,769 swings across all eight fights**: the average chance to hit runs **60% with one on it, 72% with two, 77% with three, 88% with four**, and **17.7% of every swing in the act already lands on a body with three or more**. Three is also where the nerve rule saturates its own ceiling (`-(3-1)x5` is -10 and `SIT_CAP` is 10), so a fourth body cannot make the nerve any worse. **Two on you is a fight; three is the arithmetic running out**, which is exactly where the user put the line. ⚑ **"Depending on the people" is the second half of his sentence and it is already true**: `flankPow` runs 10 on a beast to 22 on a clever soldier, so the tooltip is computed from the bodies actually standing there - *"Three of them on it, and none of them has to watch its own back. Each one swings at +20."* ⚠ **It names the RULE and not the outcome for the nerve half** (*"pays 5 a turn for every one past the first"*), because the charge goes through `SIT_CAP` and is summed with everything else the situation says, so a printed *"-10"* would be a receipt that does not match the till. ⛔ **AND `noArc` NEVER WEARS IT.** The Thing in Armour takes no flanking bonus at all by design, so a SURROUNDED badge would be the board recommending the one play the fight exists to punish; it stays bare, and the bare token IS the teaching. `bare:true` suppresses the bracketed count because the glyph already carries `⊛3` and the sentence already says *three*. **THE CASTER LOSES TWO SCHOOLS.** *"The mage at the start has only two spells - one basic and one random."* ⚑ **The caster he meets at the start is Marrow, and Marrow is the BATTLE-MAGE**, so two spells means EMBER plus ONE rolled school, and the company's first caster went from four arcane cards to two. ⛔ **The `mage` class keeps TWO, and not out of timidity**: its entire signature is a second casting each turn and every school carries a 2-3 turn cooldown, so a mage with one school would spend its second casting on nothing every turn for the whole game. **A/B'd through the harness, 32 fights a side: 7.0 rounds and 25/32 won with two schools, 6.7 rounds and 27/32 with one** - no detectable difference, which is the argument FOR the cut: the battle-mage casts once a turn either way, so the second school was buying variety at the price of a whole card of reading on the first fight. ⚠ **The top-up test used to be `<`, a one-way valve** that would have left an existing save holding all three forever and made the change invisible in the one save that matters. It trims as well as fills, keeping the schools already in hand. **PROVISIONS STOP BEING A TAX ON BEING HURT.** *"Is food spent per day or on healing the wounded automatically? It should not be - only events, choices and deliberate healing."* ⛔ **It was: any day anybody carried a wound took one provision, with no door and no card**, and on an empty barrel the mending halved and the mood dropped two. It was put there by playtest #6 for a real reason - *a resource nobody is ever forced to consider is not a resource* - and it is the same shape the user rejected once already when he took the food cost off TRAVEL. ⚑ **The sink is not deleted, it is turned the right way round**: the wounded mend at `MEND_BASE` for nothing, and DOUBLE RATIONS still buys twice the pace. ⚠ **Playtest #6's finding is therefore live again and is the thing to watch.** The long-fight drain from round 11 STAYS and is named here so it is not mistaken for a miss: it is the FUSE that ended an 835-round Fen-Mother stalemate, and it is a situation rather than a timer. **THE THING IN ARMOUR CANNOT BE LEFT.** *"You feel there is no running from this one."* ⚑ **`NOWITHDRAW` stopped being a list and became a register**, so the fourth fight is one row - and ⚠ **the ternary it replaced was telling the TAVERN BRAWL that "the bells are behind you as well"**, in a tap-room in Grausen Hold, an act away from the bells. ⛑ **It also cashes QA-7**: the open ruling was that fleeing this fight deleted the Dead Company beat, so THE LONG FIRE one node later spoke about *"nine men in a ditch three miles back"* to a player who never met them. No fled path, no seam. **THE ! ON THE SACK NAMES ITS PERSON.** ⚑ **The test already knew who it was and was throwing the answer away**: two nested `.some()` calls compute exactly *this item, on that person* and then reduce it to a boolean. Returning the pair costs nothing. ⛑ **The ★ chip he asked for in the same breath was already built** - #150 wired it - so it was verified rather than rebuilt: the star opens on the person holding the point, with the perks tab in front. **THE CHEST TUTORIAL SAYS WHAT EACH ONE IS FOR.** It was pointing at four glyphs and spending its whole card on the arithmetic of one, so a player meeting ❦ ▤ ◈ for the first time was told the wage bill grows by seven a day. Four lines, one per glyph, in the bar's own order and off `RES_ICON` so the card and the bar cannot drift; the payday clause survives as one sentence because the PAYDAY chip three inches away counts the days down by itself. `#tutCard` takes `white-space:pre-line`, since `tutShow` fills it with `textContent`. **THE MUSTER LOSES ITS PARAGRAPHS.** 66 words of who these people are and why they are still sitting there, on a screen whose actual question is *can I afford one and do I have room*: both openers are one sentence now (**intro 100 words to 32, the whole screen 113**), the purse and the surcharge untouched. **The personality is a name on the row and a sentence on hover**, where it had been three lines per stranger and nine on the screen. The shop rows lose their flavour to a `title`, and the cart reads **THE SHOP · 1 of 3**, which is his own phrasing and the whole rule. ⛑ Cutting the first opener deleted a small lie with it: it said they had heard what is in the fen, written when the muster stood AFTER the Black Fen. **THE RUN STARTS WITH ONE BIT OF JUNK.** `stash:['pot','cudgel','broom']` to `['pot']` - and `pot` IS the cook-pot and straps, the only one of the three worth putting on somebody. **AND A LOST FIGHT CAN BE TAKEN AGAIN.** *"So a person can carry on the run if they were enjoying it."* ⚑ **The snapshot IS the save's own blob** and the restore is `loadRun()`: a second field list would be a second source of truth that goes stale the first time somebody adds a flag to the save and not to this. ⛔ **AND THE FIRST VERSION OF IT WAS A REAL BUG, KEPT ON THE RECORD BECAUSE THE SHAPE IS THE INTERESTING PART.** It called `saveRun()` and read the file back - and **`startBattle` runs inside the test harness too**: `runFight` swaps `G.party` for an arena company and `SIM.on` is false in there, so **a regression run wrote a scratch company straight over the player's real save.** `saveRun`'s own first line is a guard against exactly that danger (*"a practice fight borrows `G` wholesale, and writing it out would overwrite a real company with a scratch one, which is the single worst thing this feature could do"*) and it could not see a caller three thousand lines away. ⚑ **The fix is the refactor that should have been done first**: the object literal comes out of `saveRun` as **`runBlob()`**, both callers share it, and the snapshot touches no storage at all. ⚠ **It was caught by measuring the save's byte length before and after a regression, not by reading the code** - and the check is now part of the run: `saveUntouchedByHarness` compares the blob across `regress()`. ⛔ **A guard on a function does not protect a value the function reads; grep the CALLERS of anything that writes persistence.** ⚠ **Three things `toDefeat` does had to be undone in the same breath** - `G.over` gates `saveRun`, `clearRun()` deleted the file the restore has to read, and `LEGACY.priorDied` is what tells the next company somebody died before them (#156 found it being read as *skip the brawl*). ⚠ **Held in memory and never persisted**: a replay is an offer about the fight you just lost, in the session you lost it in. **AND NOBODY CHASES A BROKEN DOG INTO A CORNER.** *"When one dog is broken and running away, end the fight rather than chasing it into the corner like BB - it is boring"*, then a minute later *"you could ask - they are all running, do you want to end the fight? so it is clearer."* ⛔ **The mercy branch already did this and structurally could not reach his case**: `beg` is deliberately the broken PEOPLE and never the broken bodies, because only people surrender - so a field of routed BEASTS has an empty `beg`, no mercy screen, and nothing to end the fight but killing every last one. The rule was right and simply had no answer for the half of the bestiary it correctly excluded. ⚑ **The same outcome with no prisoners, and offered rather than taken**, which is his own second message and also the honest version, because running them down is a real choice with a real reason. ⚠ **Asked at the top of the player's own turn, which is why it needs no clock hold** - `checkEnd` runs from several places a turn, and gating on `cur()` being one of yours means the card can only go up when nothing is moving; #156's spotlight needed `B.tutHold` precisely because it could fire at the END of somebody else's turn. ⚠ **And never under AUTO**, which is README §6's invariant: AUTO plays the fight, not the run, so it chases and the offer waits for the reins. ⛑ **This is #89's parked "mop-up tail" arriving as a playtest note** rather than as the measurement it was filed as. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots** (the pane composites no frames): LINT **0** · no em dash in EVENTS · 0 doors missing an intent · **all eight fights through `regress()` three times, every one `ok`** · `--head` measured across a live board from -5px on a 35px sprite to -24px on a 54px one, with the bars 8px above each head and the Fen-Mother's second hex correctly carrying none · the badge reading `⊛3` with its computed tooltip · Marrow built with `EMBER + UNPICK` and `castCap 1` · the A/B over 64 fights · `NOWITHDRAW` reading four keys with the armour line exact · the ! chip driven and landing on Vesna with the stash tab, the ★ chip on Marrow with the perks tab · the muster measured at 32 intro words · **the defeat screen driven end to end and the replay restoring day 9 to 7, 3 crowns to 91, `over` false, `priorDied` false, the save back, and the brigand fight standing with four of mine on their feet** · **the rout card raised in a REAL campaign pack fight** with eight dogs routed (title `THEY ARE ALL RUNNING`, both doors, a second `checkEnd` raising no second card and resolving nothing twice, never offered under AUTO, and correctly suppressed on the practice field, which has its own report). Eye check: `shots/157_before.html` and `shots/157_after.html`. **The user's save was snapshotted out of `localStorage` and written back afterwards, twice.** | ✅ |
| 8f.189 | **#161 - A CASTER'S REACH BECOMES GEAR, THE MOOD SETTLES ON THE ROAD, AND THE RUN GETS ITS FIRST SECOND OBJECTIVE.** *(User, 2026-08-16, a three-item batch.)* **SPELL RANGE STOPPED BEING A CONSTANT.** *"The mage has an artifact in hand, a magic stick. A jab with it is clearly weaker than with a sword. It gives +2 range for magic (then ember and the other spells need to be made much closer by default, but not closer than 1)."* ⛔ **The defect he is describing is real and it is structural: where a caster stood was decided by his spell list and by nothing the player owned.** Every school lost 2 hexes - **BLIGHT-WIND 5→3 · UNPICK 4→2 · EMBER 4→2 · WITHER 3→1 · IRON-OATH 2→1**, the floor being his own - and the **WORKING STAVE** in `GEAR` hands them back. Four of the five therefore come out at exactly their old figure in the hand that is holding the stave; IRON-OATH is 3 rather than 2 because it only had 2 to give above the floor. ⚑ **THE JAB IS THE WORST WEAPON ON THE RACK AND THAT IS THE POINT**: 6-10 against the gut-knife's 11-16 and the arming sword's 16-24, so the comparison the user made in his own sentence IS the decision. **Marrow starts holding it and his short sword goes into the stash**, because he is the only caster the player has for most of act 1 and a change nobody is carrying is a change nobody sees (#156's own finding, one entry old). On the sheet, live: stave **jab 6-10 · EMBER range 4 · BLIGHT-WIND range 5**, sword **jab 13-19 · EMBER range 2 · BLIGHT-WIND range 3**, and the swap is one click because the sword is in the cart. ⚑ **`castAct()` IS THE ONE DOOR from a school to a card on a body**, and it is not tidiness: **the magpie personality re-rolls a caster's whole school list at the top of every round** (`capTick`), so the second copy of `Object.assign({},SPELLS[k])` would have handed a magpie two-hex-shorter cards once a turn and silently switched the stave off. `u.spellRange` is the fact and it is **read off all three slots**, so a trinket or an off-hand piece carrying the field works the day it is written - the opposite of #141's `dodge`, which was wired on the off hand and ignored on a trinket. ⛔ **AND NOT ONE RANGE FIGURE SURVIVES IN A SPELL'S PROSE.** Both card renderers already print `range N` off `a.range`, so *"RANGE 5"* in a note was a second readout that goes wrong the moment somebody picks up a stave: the event-card rule's *"the prose never spends a sentence on a figure the receipt is already holding"* arriving on the battle's own cards. IRON-OATH's *"within 2 hexes"* stays, because that RADIUS is a hard 2 in `clickHex` and nothing moves it. ⛑ **Two stale class hovers fell out in passing**: the battle-mage said *"Two schools"* and the mage *"Three"*, and **#157 cut them to 1 and 2 four days ago without touching the readout** - #102's wrong-unit rule on a fourth surface, found only because the same paragraph was being rewritten. **THE COMPANY'S MOOD SETTLES ON THE ROAD.** *"On the global map, if morale is above or below the norm it tends toward the norm. If it is 1 parameter above or below, it tends toward the norm by 1. If more, by 2."* ⚑ **The norm is a BAND and not a point**, which is what stops this being a slow eraser: mood walks back to the edge of AT EASE and stops there, so a company kept happy stays a rung up on nothing but neglect and only a SPENT mood decays. The step is measured in `MOODSTATES`' own rungs, and **`MOOD_NORM` is derived as the band containing zero** rather than written as an index or a name, because both of those are constants that rot the day somebody adds a rung. Measured over 14 days in the running build: **45 → 43, 41, 39, 38, 37... 28** (two rungs out pays 2 until it enters STEADY, then 1) · **20 → 19... 15, 14, and stops** · **5 → 5, unmoved** · **-20 → -19... -15, and stops** · **-60 → -58... -40, -39...**. ⚑ **It cannot overshoot, and not by a clamp**: the norm band is 30 wide and the longest stride is 2, so there is no arithmetic that walks past it. ⚠ **It is FIRST in the day loop on purpose** - the payday and the 10-point fine for an empty chest are today's news, and news the player is about to read must not be shaved in the tick it lands in. **THE RUN GETS A SECOND OBJECTIVE, AND IT IS ABOUT THE COMPANY.** *"On the road to the first event the first quest pops up: there are few of you and you are not very experienced in these lands, if you expand your group travelling will get much more comfortable. Second objective: increase the party size. Morale +10."* THE CONTRACT is a place at the far end of the valley; this is the first thing the game has ever asked that is about the people, and it is deliberately the smallest shape that holds it - **one fact in the run band, one row under a hairline in the plate that is already there, one payment.** No quest log, no chain. ⚑ **THE TARGET IS COUNTED ON THE DAY, NEVER WRITTEN DOWN**: `need` is the roster the card opens on **plus two**, so it survives `makeParty` changing size, an ogre eating three seats, and the clash allies arriving before the muster. A literal 6 would be the same defect as a rule anchored to run PROGRESS, which this map has already broken on once. **Two and not one**, because taking a side at Blood on the Road hands you a body for free and a target of one would have asked the player for nothing. ⛔ **`goalTick()` SITS AT THE HEAD OF `worldTick` AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT HUNG ON THE SIX PLACES THAT PUSH ONTO `G.party`** - the muster wall, the returner at the fire, the walker out of the sinkhole, two event recruits and the preset join - because six call sites is the shape that ships with five of them wired, and `worldTick` is what every one of them comes back through. A seventh recruiter inherits it untaught. ⚠ **Guarded on `SIM`**, since the arena empties and refills `G.party` to stage a fight and a practice board may never pay the campaign (invariant 8). **The card fires above the `clashDone` gate in `rollCampAt`**, on the road to The Wheel-Rut - the one stretch that gate keeps deliberately silent, so it costs the road no slot and does not blunt Blood on the Road - and it sets the leg's own latch so a multi-day first road cannot stack a vignette on it. **Verified in the running build, DOM and counters rather than screenshots** (the pane composites no frames): `LINT()` **0 findings** · every school ≥ 1 · `castAct` leaving the `SPELLS` templates unmutated · the unit's `spellRange` 2 with the stave and 0 with the sword · **both act-card renderers read out of the live DOM** for both hands · the drift walked day by day from five starting moods · the objective issued at need 6, refusing to pay at 4, paying exactly **+10** once through `worldTick`, never twice, and **paying nothing and staying open under `SIM.on`** · the card opened by driving `rollCampAt` on the hold→coin road, its button closing it and running the leg's `next()` · the plate measured **52px → 93px**, still 304 wide, bottom at 145 against a topmost node at screen y 236 · **`spacingViolations()` 0 and `labelViolations()` 0**. ⚠ **The user's own save was never at risk and was never touched**: this session's server took port 62033 because 8777 was busy, and localStorage is per origin. | ✅ |
| 8f.187 | **#159 - THE WEDDING GETS A FIFTH DOOR, THE ROAD GETS ITS FIRST `?`, AND THE AFTERMATH LEARNS TO CHARGE FOR A WIN.** *(User, 2026-08-16: "Я хочу добавить в ретлинг веддинг доп. опцию: Выризать всех и собрать лут, там внизу где подпись - ?" · "запустить бой - где ретлинги все безоружные... И тебе нужно вырезать 10 пьяных ретлингов. И выйдет - что механически бой легкий. Просто масака" · "Как концепция - на некоторых выборах ставить '?' - в основном я буду говорить. На определенных выборах по лутанию" · then, mid-build: "обязательно один из врагов ретлингов Жених и невеста - чтобы добавить драмы".)* **THE `?` IS A CONVENTION AND NOT A STRING**, which is the whole of why it is a flag: `mystery:true` makes `choiceNote` return one question mark with a hover, and ⛔ **it hides what a door PAYS and never what it CHARGES.** The pillar's own line is that prices stay on the buttons as intent and the score is what is hidden; a `?` is the card admitting it has no honest intent to state, because the company cannot see into the cart either. So a `need:` the chest cannot meet **still prints beside the question mark**, `danger` still paints the door red, and the #154 glyphs stay - on a `?` door they are the only thing left to sort it by, which is that entry turning out to have been the load-bearing half. **`LINT` 6d holds both halves of the rule** (no `c:` line, and no `days`/`morale`/negative coin or food at the pick), so the next card cannot quietly ship a hidden price. ⚑ **AND THAT PUSHED THE RECEIPT SOMEWHERE NEW, WHICH IS THE ONE STRUCTURAL CHANGE IN THE ENTRY.** The door pays nothing at the pick and starts a fight, and an automatic haul may never charge morale (#55's rule), so there was nowhere left for the bill except the aftermath: **`AFTER` rows may now carry `mor` and `remember`**, applied by `consequences()` and printed as a MORALE chip in the aftermath's own row. It is `BEATEN`'s `mor` field on the winning screen instead of the losing one, and **this is the first fight in the game that charges the company for WINNING it**. The chip wears the company's face out of `MOODSTATES` and never a heart, which is #150's rule reaching the last surface that had not heard it. ⛔ **THE FIGHT SHIPPED ITS FIRST CUT AT THE ENGINE'S STANDARD TWO ACTIONS A BODY AND WAS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT WAS ASKED FOR.** Ten bodies at two actions is **twenty swings a round against a starting company's eight**, and `ARENA.match('four','wedding',8)` said **2/8 wins, 3.3 of yours down** for a card whose entire brief is *"механически бой легкий. Просто масака"*. **One field fixed it**: `actions:1` on nine of the ten (#156's own sanctioned door for a body whose shape IS the number), which took the same company to **20/20 at 8.5 rounds and 0.7 down**, prepared 10/10 at 5.3, swarm 10/10, bows 10/10, wall 10/10 - **50 of 50 across five companies in mirror-AI, which reads easier again in human hands.** ⚑ **A door's prose can be right while the fight behind it says the opposite, and only the arena can tell you which one the player meets.** The other two difficulty knobs are fiction rather than tuning: **`drunk` on all ten** (#108's own flag, -12 to hit, -8 dodge, and ten 🍺 badges is the picture the card is about) and **`ambush` on the door**, so round one is theirs and nothing in it moves. ⛔ **`noRout` ON ALL TEN MEANS THE OPPOSITE OF WHAT IT MEANT AT THE CIRCLE** - they hold because they are drunk, because the carts are behind them, and because two of them will not leave each other - and ⚠ **it costs the mercy card, which is the price of the literal ask**: `toSpoils` reads the enemies who BROKE and lived, so a field where nothing breaks has nobody left to spare. Correct here (you answered that question on the road) and named in the code as the one line to flip. **THE COUPLE ARE THE DRAMA AND ALSO THE ARITHMETIC**: the groom is the only crest on the field and the only body still on two actions, standing in front of her; the bride is the weakest thing in the game (23hp, 2 armour, 2-5 damage off her nails) and does not leave. Nobody at this wedding carries a weapon - the two "armed" bodies hold a jug and the roasting spit off the fire, which is the entire armoury. **THE GROUND IS `plains`, POOL `[open]`**: cover would be a gift to whichever side needed it and neither side is supposed to need anything. **The card stays in its band** at 142 words (the new door's `after` is empty, so the road is unchanged at 34 cards / 3,695 words). ⛔ **AND THE FIFTH DOOR WAS FLAGGED AS A DOOR TOO MANY AND RULED ON WITHIN THE HOUR**: a company holding both races opened every race-gated door at once, so the wedding showed five - and so did the Sitting Stone, which nobody had noticed. *(User: "if party has both ratking and ogr - show only ratkin option".)* **`raceDoorPick()` collapses them to one, `RACE_DOOR_ORDER` is `['ratkin','ogre','human']`, and it is a PRIORITY rather than a ban** - every race door still exists and still fires, the rule only decides which is offered when more than one could be. ⚑ **One function, called by the road renderer AND the fire renderer**, which is the law `choiceNote` and `choiceIco` are already under: the two `visible` filters were the second-source-of-truth shape this project keeps deleting, and a rule bolted onto one of them would have drifted by the next card. ⚠ Ratkin first because this is a ratkin island and both cards are about the clans; **the visible maximum is 4 again on every card in the deck**, measured by driving the wedding and the Sitting Stone with four companies (humans · +ratkin · +ogre · both). **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots** (the pane composites no frames): LINT **0** · no em dash · 0 doors missing an intent · exactly **1** mystery door in the whole road · the card driven with the `?` and its hover read off the DOM · the fight driven end to end under AUTO to **8 rounds, 10 dead, morale +4 to -26** with the chips reading `◉+80 CROWNS` and `😟-30 MORALE` and the memory line on the roster · **all eight existing fights through `regress()`, every one `ok`** · the four LINT registers (AFTER · dispatch · LOOT · FIELDS) taught the new key so it cannot silently inherit the Snare's army, spoils or ground. Eye check: `shots/159_wedding_board.html` and `shots/159_wedding_card.html`. | ✅ |
| 8f.188 | **#160 - THE ROAD KEEPS A LEDGER, AND THREE DAYS FROM THE BELLS SOMEBODY READS IT BACK TO YOU.** *(User, 2026-08-16: a road event in the second half of the run where "enforcers" stop the company and ask about what it has done; a parcel for a company that has done nothing; and an "ask to join them" door on the final camp.)* **THE LEDGER IS DERIVED AND THE PHRASE IS AUTHORED, WHICH IS THE ONE DESIGN DECISION EVERYTHING ELSE FOLLOWS FROM.** `G.sins` gets a row per evil door taken, in order, and `markSin` decides what an evil door IS by reading the **☠ that #154 already put on all eleven of them** - so a twelfth written next month joins the ledger untaught. That is #137's derive-it rule on its fifth surface, and it is why there is no hand-kept list of "the bad doors" to go stale. What IS authored is the short phrase a serjeant could read off a note (*"the pedlar on the ridge, and his cart"*), because the card title says WHERE and the label says WHAT YOU CHOSE and neither is a sentence anybody could say out loud. **LINT 6d holds the pair together in both directions**: a ☠ with no `sin`, and a `sin` on a door with no ☠. **THE STOP IS ANCHORED TO A DISTANCE, NOT TO A NODE.** `stepsToGoal()` counts roads backwards from `QUEST_GOAL` and the two cards fire at fixed bands: **the Hold's men at 3 out, the courier at 2**. On the map as it stands that is `e1`/`p1` (whichever quiet arm of the last fork was walked) and `shrine`. ⛔ **The reason is `armourWhere`'s, written out again because it cost a near-miss once**: a rule phrased against a node's PROGRESS breaks silently the day somebody reorders the map, and #71 moved the Muster Field and nearly fired the Thing in Armour on top of the Fen-Mother. **LINT 8c enumerates all eight roads** and asserts each passes exactly one node in each band, that neither band is a battle node, and that neither leads straight into one - which is the user's own *"but not before a battle"* turned into a check rather than a promise. **THE TWO CARDS ARE MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE BY THE LEDGER AND NOT BY A FLAG**, so no third field has to be kept in step. **THE SERJEANT ASKS A QUESTION AT ONE AND STATES A FACT AT TWO.** `needSins`/`maxSins` are a new door gate in `openEvent`'s existing filter: at one deed the doors are lie *(morale −5)*, bribe *(−◉40)* and fight; at two or more they are bribe *(−◉100)* and fight, because he has stopped asking. ⚠ **When the chest cannot pay, the bribe greys out with "cannot afford" and the fight stays live** - checked, because that is where a soft lock would be. **THE ASK AT THE SNARE IS NOT MARKED EVIL, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.** `markSin` fires on the door that is clicked and the card that door opens reads `G.sins.length` to decide what the clan says back - so a ☠ on the ASK would have added a sin before the ANSWER counted them, and told a company with a clean ledger *"we heard about you"* about nothing it had done. **A ledger the question writes into cannot be read by the answer.** The villainy is on the door in `joined`, where the deed actually happens. ⚑ **And "one deed, and you did not fight the patrol" needs no flag either**: fighting them is itself a deed, so it can never leave you at one. The three answers are `sins.length` alone. **THE HOLD'S MEN ARE THE ONLY UNIFORM IN THE ACT.** Grausen Hold's road-men, which is lore-consistent rather than an import: §5 says human holdings cannot bring force at scale and §9 says the lord will not send his own men EAST, and neither says he does not police his own valley - which is where the whole act is fought. **The picture is three crests**: `human_foe_captain` is the painted sprite for `captain:true`, so three of them in one line reads "not bandits" before a word is spoken, and nothing new is painted. **`plains` FINALLY GETS A CAMPAIGN FIGHT** - built in #61, defined as *"open, dry, and there is nothing to get behind"*, and reachable only from the practice field until now, exactly the way the forest was until #147. **THE JOIN FIGHT BUILDS THE REAL CLAN AND DOES NOT COPY IT.** `snareClan()` calls `foes()`, drops one body and turns the rest round through `besideYou` - so it scales with the company and rolls its kit the way the finale does, and a second hand-written clan table cannot drift from it. ⚠ **The ids are renamed `clan0..N` because `build()` hands out `foe0..N` from the same counter** and two bodies answering to one id breaks `at()`, every `B.units.find` and every ally test. **THE DIAL ON THAT FIGHT IS THE BILL COUNT AND IT IS NOT THE HEADCOUNT**, measured against a seasoned six plus the eight-body clan, counting only the ROSTER's losses, n=15 a row: **17 coats with 5 bills won 12-13 of 15; 17 coats with 6 bills won 8 of 15; 18 coats with 6 bills won 4 of 12.** ⚑ **One bill is worth twenty-seven points of win rate and a crossbow is worth nothing measurable** - a bill is a reach-2 body behind 58 armour, and six of them is a wall two parties cannot open before the bows work. This is #93's finding from the other end, and it is why the first three passes read as noise: they moved the count, the plan's columns and the bows together and no delta could be attributed to any of them. ⛔ **Change ONE at a time and re-measure at n≥15.** The shipped row sits at **53% against the honest finale's 45%** (`snare`, same instrument, n=20), which is the intended ranking: bringing eight friends must not make this the EASY way to end the act. The road patrol is **13/20, 9.3 rounds, 3.5 of six carried off** - harder than everything else on the road and easier than the finale, which is where it belongs. **THE CAGE IS DELIBERATELY NOT OPENED ON THE JOIN PATH.** Wynn Aldreth is the clan's property and you have just fought to keep the clan standing; cutting the withies afterwards would be the game handing the player the door AND the reward for not taking it. ⚠ **`G.epiLine` is set in `cageThenConsequences` and not on the door**, because `toSpoils` is only ever reached on a win or a mercy - an epilogue written at the door would end a wiped company in triumph. **A CLEAN ROAD GETS THE ONLY TRINKET IN THE GAME WITH NO CLAUSE AND NO COST.** ⚠ **It does not break the pillar and the arithmetic is why**: *"good deeds must not secretly pay better"* is about the running total, and the running total of a clean road is eleven refused hauls - several hundred crowns, a mail shirt, a gem and two carts. One trinket on one body on the last leg does not close that gap and is not meant to; what it does is stop the clean road paying literally nothing, which is the other way the pillar fails. ⛔ **AND THE LINTER CAUGHT A REAL HOLE ON ITS FIRST RUN**: `joined`'s body indexed into the ledger and capitalised the result, so `evBody` - which reads every card to scan it for tokens - died on a clean company and took the whole of `LINT()` with it. The card is only REACHABLE with the entries it quotes, which made the crash invisible in play and total in the one tool built to find it. **A body that cannot be read out of context is a body nothing can check**; `sinAt(i,cap)` is the one door now and it degrades to a sentence. **AND THE STANDING WORD COUNTER WAS FOUND TO BE MEASURING THE WRONG THING.** `.claude/rules/event-cards.md`'s snippet sums EVERY `after` on a card, which was the same as "what the player reads" only while every door was shown to everybody: THE HOLD'S MEN measures **183** by it and the player reads **137 on the one-sin branch and 125 on the two-sin branch**, because three of its four outcomes are unreachable on any single run. The rule file now carries the branch-aware counter and the general lesson - **a card with a function body must be measured on every branch it can take**, since `body()` returns one string and a fresh page silently measures the clean branch. **Verified in the running build, DOM not screenshots**: `LINT()` **0 findings** · no em dash in EVENTS · **0 doors without an intent, 13 evil doors, 0 without a `sin`** · the distance bands resolving to `e1`/`p1` and `shrine` across all 8 roads · **all ten fights through `regress()`, every one `ok`, no errors** · both branches of the card driven and read out of the DOM with their chips (**morale 10→5 with the 😐 face, 200→160, 200→100 - every chip matching the till**) · the broke company's greyed bribe with the fight still live · all three Snare answers routed (0 and 1 → THEY HEAR YOU OUT, 2 → OUR CALIBRE OF FILTH) and **the ask proven not to write to the ledger** · the courier wearing its loot on the card · **the join fight driven to a win end to end: 17 foes, 8 clan, no duplicate hexes, spoils card, cage correctly not opened, END OF SLICE reached** - the one path that could have soft-locked. ⛑ **Filed on the way past**: a lost fight in the harness can delete a run save, because `checkEnd` sends a non-`BEATEN` loss to `toDefeat()`→`clearRun()` asynchronously, after the harness's synchronous loop has returned. That is #157's finding with the WRITE fixed and the DELETE left; it is a pre-existing hazard and it is spawned as its own task rather than widened into this entry. | ✅ |
| 8f.190 | **#162 - THREE PLAYTESTERS IN A CLEAN ROOM: DIMA, SANYA, ANDREY.** *(User, 2026-08-16: three playtester agents - a genre fan who reads enemies fast, a middle player who knows Into the Breach but not Battle Brothers, and somebody who has played nothing like it and has poor eyesight for small elements - "closed off from information outside their session, so they play and note their experience from a clean slate", so that builds can be run through them and the feedback, results and interest read back.)* Nothing in the game changed. **`tools\playtest\run.ps1 -Who all` puts a build through all three and writes one page per tester into `docs\playtests\`** plus a comparison. ⛔ **The first design was `.claude/agents/*.md`, and it was wrong before it was written**: a probe subagent spawned from this folder listed the three `.claude\rules` files and the whole `MEMORY.md` in its own context and could name MOODSTATES and the intent glyphs, so no prompt could make it a stranger to this game. **The clean room is a process boundary**: each tester is its own `claude -p` in an EMPTY folder, `--tools ""`, `--strict-mcp-config`, and its one tool is `game`, served by `tools\playtest\eyes.py` (standard-library python speaking DevTools protocol over a websocket it implements itself, since the machine has no node) over a headless Chrome/Edge with a fresh profile - no save, no legacy, no journal. **Andrey's eyesight is enforced by the server, not requested of the persona**: text under 13px reaches him as `▒▒▒▒ (too small to read)`, glyphs as "a tiny mark", the screenshot at half size, and a `squint()` close-up exists whose every use is logged as a moment the game made him lean in. `look()` hides the developer's words (tiles are "45 of kind A", never `.hex.blocked`), reads through the tutorial's translucent spotlight as a veil, ignores transparent hit-catchers, and refuses the DEV.MODE switch by ref or by coordinate. **Interest is measured**: every 20 hand-actions the rig appends a CHECKPOINT and the next `note()` carries a 1-5 score; the first stop rule is "you would close it now - close it and say why". Each page carries the game's own telemetry (`JOURNAL` + `G`, read after the tester left: day, crowns, morale, roster, battles, run-end), the report under fixed headings, the diary of every note, and the folded transcript. Verified: the driver plays the front door and the brawl (screenshots real, hex board collapsed to kinds, skill labels found once the hit-catcher rule landed), the MCP stdio server answers a scripted client (tools/list, click returns a look, screenshot and squint return images), and `run.ps1` runs end to end up to the CLI's own `Not logged in` wall, which it detects and prints. ⚠ **ONE-TIME: the bundled `claude.exe` must be `/login`-ed by hand before the testers can think**; the runner names the exe. `docs\PLAYTESTERS.md` is the how-to. ⚑ `actions.jsonl` answers the plan's step 1 (clicks from launch to the first paid decision) as a by-product, for three players, every build. | ✅ |
| 8f.191 | **#163 - THE BATTLE SCREEN'S SIXTH CLARITY PASS: THE TOKEN LOSES ITS LUGGAGE AND THE TWO CARDS LOSE HALF THEIR ROWS.** *(User, 2026-08-16, a numbered list of six against three annotated screenshots.)* **THE THREE TOKEN NOTES ARE ONE NOTE AND IT IS ABOUT VERTICAL FOOTPRINT.** *"1) hitpoints closer to the unit head · 2) All units in the hexes 10% smaller - so it is easier navigate throught hexes, when it is crowded · 4) statuses below the unit."* Above a head there was, reading down, a status row 21px up, then the bars 8px up, then the body: **~26px of furniture standing on a row whose pitch is 32**, so a token in a crowd was mostly other tokens' luggage. **`TOKEN` 1.13 → 1.13×0.90** (written as the arithmetic, so the next pass can read the history out of the line), the bars close to **6px** off `--head`, and the badge row leaves the top of the token altogether. ⚑ **`TOKEN` REACHES THE BOARD AND NOTHING ELSE**, because every other caller of `sprite()` refits the bitmap it gets back - the order rail on `min(48/w,48/h)`, the sheet's icon on `58/h`, the road token on `TOKEN_H/h` - so the change cannot leak into a screen nobody asked about. Sprites measured **46-56px → 25-49px** on the board and unchanged everywhere else. ⛔ **AND "BELOW THE UNIT" WAS MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED, WHICH IS THE WHOLE ENTRY.** Below the feet is exactly the strip the rank in front paints over - #157's argument run backwards - so the first build of this was probed rather than eyeballed: in a **3x3 jam of ten bodies, 20 of 40 badges came out more than half buried, and a SINGLE centred badge - the common case - was buried 58% of the time.** The half-hex row offset does **not** leave a clear channel down the middle: a front sprite is 33-48px wide on a 37px hex and covers the middle of the hex behind it too. **There is no legible position anywhere below a head**, which is #157's finding arrived at from the other side. ⚑ **SO THE FIX WAS A LAYER, AND IT FOUND A DECLARATION DOING TWO JOBS.** `z-index:1` sat on `.hin` itself and answered both *"a body is above every hexagon of ground"* (what it was written for) and *"the whole token is one composited unit"* (which nothing asked for, and which made any child's z-index meaningless). **That is the README's own shape - one map answering two questions - and it was complete until the day the answers differed.** The 1 moved onto `.hin>*`, so bodies still beat `.hedge` and two sprites still tie and are still separated by DOM order; **`.hin>.stbar` takes a 2** and clears every sprite on the board whatever rank it stands in. **The bars stay at 1, and must**: health is read WHILE surrounded, which is the one moment the cover happens, so the row that cannot be covered keeps the place nothing can cover. Depth verified unchanged: **15 sprite pairs still overlapping in the same jam.** ⛔ **AND IT SHIPPED WRONG ONCE INSIDE THIS SESSION.** `.stbar{z-index:2}` written on the rule itself is (0,1,0) and so is `.hin>*` - a tie, broken by source order, and `.hin>*` is a thousand lines further down the file. **The badge row computed 1, the board looked exactly as it had, and nothing anywhere said the lift had not happened**: the README's *A RULE THAT LOSES ON CSS SPECIFICITY FAILS SILENTLY AND LOOKS LIKE IT IS WORKING*, caught only by reading the computed value instead of re-reading the logic. `.hin>.stbar` is (0,2,0) and wins outright, and both levels are now declared together. **THE AIMING CARD STOPS BEING A DECOMPOSITION.** *"a) Delete pieces that i crossed · c) instead of "other" wadd too things: your nerve / enemy nerve · d) if modifier doesnt work - don`t write it at all · f) Don`t write there that enemy nerve · m) Move damage on top as same as final chance to hit · q) Make it pop up one second later."* `your skill` and `their dodge` are the two largest terms in the number **and the two nobody can do anything about this turn** - they are these bodies being who they are - and they cost four rows to say it, two figures and two captions. What is left is the headline, what the blow takes off, and the four situational terms: **arc, ring, your nerve, theirs**, every one of which is a different place to stand or a different body to break first. ⛔ **The rows no longer sum to the total and that is the design**, not an omission to be repaired by putting `other` back; `parts`/`oth` are still built for whoever wants the full working next. ⚠ **`f)` READS AS A CONTRADICTION OF `c)` AND IS NOT ONE**: the struck-out NERVE row at the foot printed the target's rung as a state, which is now the right-hand half of `their nerve` and said once instead of twice - #102's wrong-unit rule on a row rather than a glyph. ⚑ **THE TWO NERVE FIGURES ARE BUILT BESIDE THE PAYMENT** (#137's rule on its fifth surface): `nerveA` is `STATES[a.state].hit` plus the rocking +5, and **`nerveD` is a DIFFERENCE of two `dodgeOf` calls** rather than a lookup of `STATES[d.state].dodge`, because every contributor there is pre-cap and `softDodge` is applied to the sum - on a body stacked past `DODGE_SOFT` the rung is worth less than its own number, and printing the raw figure would be a receipt that does not match the till. **Both are reporting fields that change no arithmetic**, so `total` is untouched and both AI brains score what they scored before; the second `dodgeOf` call is **gated on the rung being non-zero**, since `hitBreakdown` is the innermost loop of both brains and the README's 23-second clash was one careless call in this function. ⚑ **The face is the ladder's and the number is the engine's, and they cannot disagree**: proved across the whole 0-1 range - **💀 −99 · 😟 −12 · 😕 −9 · 😐 −5 · 🙂 0 (so the row hides) · 😄 +5**, every band mapping to exactly one state. **THE CARD OPENS ON THE FAR SIDE OF WHAT IT DESCRIBES.** *"when i hover enemy unit with hit - it shows % of hit on the ride (it hides my initial unit). Show it opposite side from my atacking unit."* `p.x+56` is always the right-hand side, **correct exactly half the time, and the half it is wrong on is not random**: a card about a swing is opened while the swinger stands next to the target. ⚑ **The side is chosen in GAME space** - a column comparison, never a pixel one - because the stage takes `rotate(90deg)` in portrait and a rule written off rects is right on a desktop and a right angle out on a phone. ⚠ **It falls back rather than clamping**: clamping the far side against a board edge would slide the card back over the very body it was moved off. **Both cards got it**, since a rule that applies to one of two cards on one surface is a rule the next reader discovers twice. **THE INSPECT CARD DROPS WHAT IS EMPTY.** *"6) Less wide enemy stats picture - if there is no trait or empty stuff - don`t show it at all."* The italic fallback *"Its blue overlay is where it can reach next turn"* was a caption for #156's reach tint **parked in the slot reserved for the trait**, so it appeared on every body in the game with no personality and taught the same thing on the twentieth hover as the first - #150's furniture rule on the inspect card. The trait half is untouched. The act list's divider and 8.5px heading are now gated on there being an act under them, which is #138's empty-box defect. **Both cards 262px → 222px**, the width declared once as `--readW` and read by `placeRead` through `readW()`, because the card is positioned **before** it is shown (`offsetWidth` is 0 there) and #163 is the first thing in this file's life that needed to know its own width to place it. **Dwell 1200 → 2200ms**, back inside the *"2-3 seconds"* of the 2026-08-02 request that constant argued its way out of - the argument was made against a card with eleven rows and the card the player now waits for has six. ⛑ **The static `#bRead` skeleton in the markup is deleted.** Both renderers set `innerHTML` unconditionally before reading an id back, so nothing there was ever seen - and it had rotted exactly the way an unread copy does, still saying `flanking` and `MORALE`, two labels the live card renamed in #146 and #134. **Verified in the running build, DOM and counters rather than screenshots** (the pane composites no frames and its viewport is 0x0, so `elementsFromPoint` hit-tests nothing - an early coverage probe returned a clean 0 that was a broken probe, not a clean result): `LINT()` **0 findings** · **all eight fights pass `regress()`** (clash 5rd · brigand 6rd · pack 7rd · slingline 6rd · steading 9rd · snare 6rd · mother 9rd · armour 11rd) · `.stbar` at offsetTop **30** on every body whatever its sprite height, which is the feet line · computed z **hin auto · usp 1 · stbar 2 · hedge auto** · the card measured **222px with no row overflowing on either card** · the side flipping **LEFT and RIGHT with a 26px gap** as the attacker changes column · a no-trait body drawing **0 italic lines** and a trait-carrying one drawing 1. **Eye check `shots/163_battle_clarity.html`**, shot headlessly through `eyes.py` (#162's driver) because the pane will not composite. | ✅ |
| 8f.192 | **#164 - THE DESIGNERS' CHECKLIST OUT OF `#dev-feedback`, AND THE THREE SCALES THAT ANSWERED FOUR OF IT.** *(User, 2026-08-16, with a Discord channel link: "нашел очень гуд штуку на геймдев фидбек от геймдизайренов... собрать стандартные фидбеки, создать по ним чек-лист и прогнат мою игру", then after the audit: "this sounds good - do it and ececute as a rules - to keep the fonts and borders one style. Minimum font i feel, nice to be 10. Adjust size of related boxes, if needed".)* **THE SOURCE IS THE TURN-BASED GAMES DISCORD, `#dev-feedback`**, which is the right room for this game rather than a general one: everybody in it ships turn-based tactics, so the notes are about turn order and grid readability. ~**70 messages read verbatim** across 2026-07-08/15 and 2026-08-13/15, plus a partial sweep of ~440 back to 08-07; Discord rate-limits history, so it is a sample and the checklist says so. ⚑ **THE VALUE IS NOT THE INDIVIDUAL NOTES, IT IS THAT THE SAME NOTES ARRIVE ON EVERY GAME**: four different developers were told to standardise their fonts inside one week. **Fourteen items in five groups** (the capsule, the distance test, UI consistency, combat legibility, the trailer) landed in `docs/DEV_FEEDBACK_CHECKLIST.md`, each carrying the line that produced it so a future argument is with a named designer. **THE AUDIT SCORED 8 PASS · 2 PARTIAL · 4 FAIL · 6 NOT YET APPLICABLE**, measured out of the source rather than eyeballed. The passes are the parts already worked: information architecture (#133/#102/#143), whose-turn legibility, eased movement (#81), hit juice (#87), who-attacks-whom (#156/#157), and a palette that is not the purple-blue-gold that got another dev's game called fake in that same channel. ⛔ **THE FOUR FAILURES WERE ONE FINDING WEARING FOUR NUMBERS: THE BUILD HAD NO TOKENS FOR SIZE, SPACING OR EDGE, SO EVERY RULE HAD BEEN DECIDED LOCALLY AND CORRECTLY, THIRTY TIMES.** **29 font sizes** in half-pixel steps including a `7.6px` nobody chose; **77 declarations at 9px or under**, down to 7px, which is the whole label layer; **42 border declarations over 7 near-identical hexes** at 88 sites, two of the pairs indistinguishable on screen and one of them already having a token (`--g1`) that 25 sites ignored; **72 paddings**, the most-used appearing five times; **560 distinct hex colours against 34 variables**. ⚑ **And the developer in that channel named the mechanism better than this entry can**: *"I think it's one of those things I stopped noticing because I stared at them for so long."* A build polished screen by screen for months converges on locally-perfect and globally-unrelated, and the author is the one person who cannot see it. ⚑ **IT IS ALSO, EXACTLY, THE CLARITY PASS**: a scale is a **merge**, not an addition, and nothing new arrives on screen. **SHIPPED AS THREE CLOSED VOCABULARIES.** Type: `--fs1`(10) `--fs2`(11) `--fs3`(12) `--fs4`(13) `--fs5`(15) `--fs6`(17) `--fs7`(20) `--fs8`(24) `--fsTitle`(74), and **`--fs1` is a FLOOR at the user's word** - 153 of the 316 type sites landed on it, which says the vocabulary is wide enough rather than narrow. Edge: `--e1`/`--e2`/`--e3` the gold ladder, `--e4`/`--e5` the teal chrome, 88 sites. Spacing: `--p1`..`--p7` at 2/4/6/9/12/16/24. **Zero literal `font-size` px remain in the file, zero structural border hexes, and exactly ONE literal padding**: `#wBar{padding:0 64px 0 var(--p5)}`, which reserves the run of bar the centred day plaque swings through and now carries a comment saying a scale step must not be substituted there. ⛔ **A PADDING PAIRED WITH A NEGATIVE MARGIN IS ONE NUMBER WEARING THREE NAMES**, and the spacing pass found two: both sticky footers (`#wDlg #wChoices`, `.popts`) cancel their own bottom padding with a matching negative margin AND a matching sticky offset. All three are written `calc(var(--pN) * -1)` now. ⚑ **The `.pcard` phantom-scrollbar fix turns out to be the same thing and is now literally the same token**: the card's bottom padding and the footer's were 17 and 24 and the phantom was the 7px difference; both are `--p7`, so "matching the two paddings" is a thing the file states instead of a thing somebody remembered. ⛔ **THE PREDICTION THAT WAS WRONG IS THE ENTRY.** The plan called the spacing pass the risky step because it moves boxes, and the floor the cheap one. **It was the other way round, and by a distance: all five broken boxes came from the floor and none from the spacing.** A padding shifts a box by two pixels;
